- **Ready/full times count from now (BR-02),** and `readyInHours` is null once a muscle is ready.
- **One load ratio (BR-03, BR-19):** `acuteChronicRatio` feeds both the systemic recovery factor and readiness; it is null until 3+ sessions span 14 of the last 28 days. readiness() keeps only check-ins from the 30 days before today.
- **Active lifts (BR-05):** progress, effort and deload rules and the weekly review skip lifts not trained in 42 days.
- **Assisted work (BR-06):** less assistance is progress; with the assistance flat, best reps decide. e1RM-based rules skip non-weighted lifts.
- **Plateau lever (BR-04):** last 8 weeks, 6+ sessions, flat means under 1.5% total change over the window (slope × weeks). Six sessions is the evidence bar, so the trend's own "low confidence" (under 7 points) no longer blocks it. Priority 305, and only the highest-priority progress note per lift is shown (BR-27).
- **Volume status (BR-07):** judged on completed weeks: under after two weeks below the band, over when this or last week is above it. The copy says "last week".
- **One set count (BR-16):** `effectiveSetsByMuscle` (direct 1, secondary 0.5, stabiliser 0); "hard sets" leave out easy sets (they used to count half).
- **Balance (BR-17, QA-R3a-3/4):** counted per exercise set, split across its primary buckets. Upper is push **plus** pull (plan row 9), compared after dividing by `UPPER_PER_LOWER` = 1.5: two regions against one, so a balanced programme carries more upper sets. A full-body week (4 bench + 4 row + 4 squat, 2:1) and an even upper/lower split (24:24) both read balanced; the mean used before flagged the even split as "upper trailing". The ratio shown to the person is the plain set ratio.
- **D9:** above the band means smaller gains for more recovery cost, not harm; over-band weeks trigger a lighter-week offer only together with a stalled active main lift.
- **Pre-session (BR-08, BR-09, D10):** the brief quotes the same target as the set rows (readiness, deload, equipment). Warm-ups are 50/70/85% of the first working set, snapped to the equipment, and a step at or above the working load is dropped. Escobar's exercise card and next-target tool use the same base.
- **Effort calibration (BR-10, BR-11, D11):** the note now tells people how to rate ("Ideal is about 2, Easy 3 or more"); the bias is still not applied to e1RM. One observation per session pair, load and label.
- **HRmax (BR-12):** the highest qualifying plateau; a fresh observation never lowers the Tanaka estimate.
- **Weight trend (BR-14):** least-squares over the last 28 days (7+ weigh-ins spanning 14+ days), % of mean per week. **Pace** is only compared for rising lifts (BR-13). **Week grade (BR-22):** the planned days are the target (3 when nothing is planned).
- **Autoregulation without equipment (BR-18):** a step that rounds back to the target moves a full 2.5 kg.
- **Density (BR-20):** per main lift with 3+ sets, first vs last set; rest median without each lift's first set. **Records (BR-21, BR-28):** rounded, in the display unit, as typed. **Heart post notes (BR-26):** the session day and the next only. **Recovery driver (BR-31):** the biggest-dose set, with that exercise's own set count.
- **Plates (BR-24):** minimum-plate DP for the largest per-side sum at or under the target.
- **Names (ST-12, ST-13):** exact muscle labels first; lower back before mid back; rear/front shoulder, serratus, brachioradialis added. A substring exercise match only counts when it is the only candidate; Escobar's writes use exact ids/names only; legacy import falls back to the one partial match with the same equipment (all gate fixtures map as before).
- **Cues (ST-17):** the two "Dumbbell" cues now match; reason cues show under the next-set reason; the 10 mindset notes take Today's quote slot on odd days of the year.
- **Body weight (UI-18):** typed and shown in the display unit, stored in kg.
- **Logged loads are loadable (BUG-11):** for every loaded exercise (dumbbell, kettlebell, barbell/plates, Smith, machine stack, cable, dip-belt added load, loaded carries; kg and lb), a target with no direction of its own (hold, confirm, plateau, re-entry, effort check, a carry's normal week) keeps a load the person already logged for that exercise in the profile's own unit, even when the built-in steps lack it (7 kg dumbbells stay 7 kg, a 16 kg kettlebell stays 16, a 32 kg carry stays 32). Increases, reductions and deloads still snap up/down to the rack; a first-time start still snaps to the plain nearest rung; a kg entry never counts on an lb rack. A directionless target that was never logged and sits exactly between two rungs goes to the heavier one (the BUG-11 card's proposed fix: ties break up for a hold). `loadableNear` itself is unchanged (warm-ups and live coaching use it). When a directionless snap moves the load, the reason says so ("Moved to 8 kg, the nearest weight your equipment has, so the reps may need to change") instead of recomputing reps, and `get_next_target` returns an `equipmentSnap` hint telling Escobar the move came from the equipment, not recovery, and to call `get_equipment`. Bodyweight moves with added load (pull-ups) progress by reps with no load target, so nothing is snapped. App-side only, no Worker change. The QA3-11b "32 kg carry → 32.5" pin now reads 32 (logged) and its rounding guard moved to a never-logged 32 kg target.

## Logic audit (QA-L1) follow-up, 2026-09-27

- **Decided (D-A1, owner 2026-09-27)**: of the seven audit rules that work as coded, (1) timed holds keep skipping the lighter week; (4) heart-guided rest never ends a main lift's rest before the user's own timer, the timer is the floor for main lifts and heart rate may only shorten rest on accessories; (5) the effort-mismatch rule compares an easy-rated set's peak with the hardest rated set of the same exercise, as decision P2 said; (6) the lighter-week offer needs 4 weeks of history and skips beginners under 3 months of training unless the trigger is 3 red readiness days out of 5; (7) the heart drift and effort-mismatch rules follow Appendix B of COACHING-PLAN.md (drift = pre-set heart rate of the last 3 sets vs the first 3, flag above 8%, sessions of 20+ min and 6+ sets, advice is hydration and longer rest, never a load cut).
  **Why**: the owner answered "do as recommended" for these; the recommendations follow the plan's own text (6.4 minimums, P2, F3.3, Appendix A point 11 and Appendix B) and the deload consensus (Bell 2023: a trained-lifter tool). Cards: (6) joins BUG-14; (4), (5) and (7) are BUG-21.
- **Decided (D-A2, owner 2026-09-27)**: rules (2) "keep the load snaps to the nearest equipment rung" and (3) "the 10% cap is checked before the equipment snap" are superseded by a new design: the coach suggests only loads that exist on the user's equipment, and when the available jump differs from the ideal step it re-solves the rep target for the rung the user will use (same e1RM and reps in reserve), and adapts the remaining sets and the verdict to the load the user actually enters. The same "adapt to the real scenario" principle is to be applied to the other areas where the app already has the input (gym switch, unknown stacks, lb, bodyweight and assisted, readiness and deload cuts with no rung, substitution).
  **Why**: the owner: "we don't suggest blindly, we suggest what's available and true for the user". Design doc: docs/LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS.md (in progress).
  **Source**: owner message 2026-09-27 22:10 UTC; qa/LOGIC-AUDIT.md "Owner decisions".
- **Decided (BUG-14, PR #47 second review, 2026-09-28)**: BR-04's 6-session / 42-day evidence bar and its confidence count (12+ → high) apply to the series actually judged (for a weighted lift with 4+ e1RM sessions in the window, only those sessions), not to every session in the window; otherwise the short path decides (never "plateaued"). The weekly e1RM note's "% a week" comes from that same 56-day window and is left out when that trend is unknown; the pace-vs-training-age note keeps the all-history slope, since it compares long-run pace and shows only for a rising lift.
  **Why**: counting sessions the rule does not judge let 4 e1RM sessions in 10 days pass as a six-week plateau, and a note whose direction and rate came from different windows could say "falling" with the rising rate.
- **Decided (BUG-14, PR #47 third review, 2026-09-28)**: the weekly e1RM note's "% a week" comes from `plateauSeries(history, mode, today)`, the rows `plateauStatus` judged on whichever path it took (the 8-week rule or the short path over the last 8 sessions), and is left out when that trend is unknown or its sign disagrees with the direction. Both functions share one internal picker, so the rows cannot drift from the verdict. Escobar's `plateau` and `deload_trigger` explanations are built from the same constants (a new exported `PLATEAU_HIGH_SESSIONS` = 12 replaces the literal), and a test pins the stated numbers (1.5%, 8 weeks, 6 sessions, 42 days; 4 weeks, 3 months, 3 of 5 red days).
  **Why**: on the short path the old rate came from the 56-day window, so a rising note could carry a falling rate; the coach's explanation still described the old last-8-sessions rule (supervisor widened BUG-14's scope by `src/escobar/knowledge/methods.ts`).
- **Decided (D-A3)**: for the load-aware targets design (`docs/LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS.md`) the supervisor settled the open points that need no owner action: jump caps per goal (strength 10 %, strength and muscle 12.5 %, lean 15 %, growth 20 %) rather than a flat 10 %; the one-time "which weight comes next?" chip in the Train card is in scope (Settings › Gyms has no inputs, so it is the only in-app way to learn a ladder); a load the user chose twice becomes the base only when its re-solved reps sit inside the goal's rep window, otherwise the app keeps steering to the planned line and says so; strength targets never go under 3 reps on set 1; acceptance is checked one rep short of failure for every goal, growth included; assisted moves stay reps-only per F13; the substitution ratio table is built in LT-5 with sources. Three points wait for the owner (§6): storing the shown target on each logged exercise, a stored "asked" mark on equipment profiles, and the PLAN:610 exception for the ratio re-solve from sets over 10 reps.
  **Why**: ACSM's 2–10 % step is for load-only progression; with the reps re-solved, the rung jump the window can absorb grows with the window, and a flat 10 % would turn the common 25 → 30 kg dumbbell case into "keep 25 kg" even for growth, where 8 hard reps at 30 kg is a sound target. The rep window is the goal; a user's heavier vote must not silently change it. Beginners judge reps in reserve poorly (Helms 2016), so the check is one rep short of failure rather than at failure.
  **Source**: the critic's 16 corrections and the architect's open questions, 2026-09-27; `src/data/goals.ts`.

- **Decided (D-A4, owner 2026-09-27 "do all recommended")**: for the load-aware targets design, (a) `LoggedExercise.target?: { kg, reps }` is added as a new saved field (the target shown when the set was committed), with a normalize rule that drops a malformed value; (b) no `EquipmentProfile.askedAt` field, the "which weight comes next?" chip's dismissal is held per run only; (c) the ratio-form rep re-solve may use sets over 10 reps (an exception to COACHING-PLAN.md line 610), and no estimated max from such a set is ever shown.
  **Why**: (a) the verdict and the next-session restatement must judge the set against what was planned, not recompute it later under a changed rule; (b) one more stored field for a cosmetic re-ask is not worth it; (c) increases fire at the top of windows that reach 12 to 20 reps, so the re-solve must work there, and the ratio form never displays a max.
  **Source**: `docs/LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS.md` §6; owner message 2026-09-27 23:10 UTC.

## Form guide: the 2D lab approach for the whole library (owner, 2026-09-27)

- **Decided (D-R16, owner 2026-09-27 23:05 UTC)**: the 2D Lateral Raise Lab approach (a comic-anatomy figure painted from the theme, whole-body motion from anatomy, muscles that shimmer with computed effort, the mistake side by side, readouts and key moments) is the standard for every exercise in the library, machines included. GU-7a-4 integration is superseded. Production architecture: `docs/FORM-GUIDE-PRODUCTION.md`.
  **Why**: the owner judged the lab against three reference pictures and approved it ("this will be the new approved").
- **Decided (D-R17)**: the open points of the production architecture: the reference for likeness is the lab's own committed renders, not the owner's outside pictures (third-party art, not committed to a public repo); the reference device for frame rate is the owner's phone, measured once per batch and recorded in the document's log (an owner action); the library is produced batch by batch with a token estimate posted before each batch (about 24M tokens in all, about 3.5M per batch), and the owner can stop between batches; GU-7a PR #40 is closed unmerged and its solve, stop spacing and types are ported with their own tests (no test deleted); PRs #37 and #38 merge before the player wiring; a machine setting with no ACE page may cite NSCA or the manufacturer's manual and is flagged for the owner's gym checklist; a machine's setup mistake is shown as a right-and-wrong pair in the setup moment, not as a second animated toggle; the load from the last logged set may be drawn on the bar or stack (read-only, no new saved data).
  **Why**: each follows the critic's evidence (AGENTS.md's "never delete a test", copyright, the 5-agent cap, the compositor facts for SVG) or the simplest product shape.
  **Source**: `docs/FORM-GUIDE-PRODUCTION.md`; the architecture critique, 2026-09-27.
- **Decided (D-FG1)**: the mistake figure's tint and its keep-quiet warning use a figure token `--mistake`, not `--negative`. It equals `--negative` in every theme except Ember, whose `--negative` is its accent (`#ff6363`), so a red mistake painted exactly like the correct figure. Ember's `--mistake` is violet `#b36bff`.
  **Why**: review of PR #48 (finding 2). Tests hold every theme's mistake body at CIEDE2000 ≥ 15 from the correct body (Ember now 27.1, was 0.0) and the keep-quiet warning at ≥ 10 from the mistake body (Ember 12.6). Violet stays clear of Ember's worked-muscle amber (`--target`) and its info blue; bluer violets gained under 2 ΔE. Same approach as Emerald's `--quiet` (info blue, because its positive green is its accent).
- **Decided (D-FG2, FG-2 builder)**: how an `ExerciseGuide` is sampled (`src/formguide/sample.ts`).
  - Stops: 100 intervals across each moving phase, holds and rests at their ends only. That is GU-7a's exact 203 for 1/0.5/2/0.5 s and 203 for any 4 s rep with both moving phases; the doc's "one stop per 4 %" does not match its own 203 (it is one per ¼ %), so the 203 was kept.
  - `keys`: a minimum-jerk (quintic) segment between each pair of keys with zero acceleration at every key and a monotone key speed (Fritsch–Butland, capped at 2× the neighbouring slopes). Two keys give exactly the minimum-jerk blend of `[a, b]`; a curve never overshoots a key, so the range checks can read the keys. Zero speed at every key was tried first: fitting the lab needed ~55 keys for one channel.
  - Key times are rep fractions in the file's own tempo; a slowed rep or the mistake's tempo stretches them phase by phase, so keys stay with their phase.
  - Hold tremor is windowed by sin(π·s), so the hold starts and ends on the held value (the lab jumps by up to 0.8° there), and it is left off the mistake (the lab's mistake has none). The stops have no points inside a hold, so the rig has to layer the tremor on top; that is left for the rig card.
  - The mistake `[a, b]` and `keys` are deltas evaluated in the mistake's own tempo. The lateral raise's mistake is fitted to the lab to 0.6° on the shoulder (hand ≤ 1.6 units over the whole path, 0.02 at the lab's mistake moment).
  - Research files live in `src/formguide/research/<id>.json`, not next to the exercise, so `exercises/` holds only exercise files for the registry's one-chunk-per-file import.
  - Alternating: two half-reps, the `_r` side first.
  - Lateral raise muscles: `helps: front_delts`, `keepQuiet: upper_traps`. The lab listed the upper traps in both, and its own effort model keeps them low (0.06 + 0.14 × load).
  **Why**: each follows the doc's tempo-derived windows, GU-7a's numbers, or the lab's own model. They are checked in `tests/formguide/guide.test.ts`.
- **Decided (D-B22, BUG-22 builder)**: how the floating Escobar dock stays off the page's content.
  - End of page: the `.app` end padding is built from tokens (and the measured dock, below): `--float-bottom + --dock-h (40px) + --dock-shade (12px) + --float-gap`, plus `--rest-h + --float-gap` when the rest banner is up. `--dock-shade` is how far `--shadow-float` reaches above the dock (blur 32 − spread 8 − offset 12 = 12px; same numbers in Paper). This replaces the fixed 140px, and fixes the rest-banner case, whose old padding (`--sp-5`, 24px) was 28px short of the dock's top.
  - Mid-page: the standard "hide on scroll" pattern for bottom bars and floating buttons (Material's bottom app bar and extended FAB): the dock slides away once the page scrolls down 8px, and comes back on 8px of scroll-up, at the top, at the end of the page (within 8px), on a tab switch and when the page does not scroll. A single step longer than the viewport (a tab restoring its scroll, `scrollTo`) re-anchors without flipping, so a restore never hides it. While hidden it takes no taps; keyboard focus brings it back. Under reduced motion it moves instantly; otherwise a 200ms slide and fade. While the Escobar sheet is loading (busy) it stays up. The trade-off: after a scroll-up mid-page the dock is back and can cover what is under it until the next scroll-down (a small scroll down moves it away again). The gate pins this on Train and on History > Stats, where the owner's screenshot had it over Exercise progress.
  - The owner's phone showed the dock over "Log a past session" at the scroll end. Checked in Chromium 141 at 390×844 on main before the fix: no nested scroller (only the document scrolls); a 48px bottom inset, both as `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` and as the `--safe-area-inset-bottom` Capacitor 8's SystemBars writes on `<html>`, keeps "Log a past session" clear and a tap opens its sheet. A Samsung-like run (360×740, a 48px inset written the SystemBars way, every `--fs-*` token ×1.3 for a larger default font) also clears it. The native side (MainActivity, NativeUiPlugin, patch_manifest.py, capacitor.config.json) sets no inset, edge-to-edge or text-zoom option, and `.app` and the dock read the same `--safe-area-inset-bottom` variable, so no split between the two was found; no Train-only wrapper has its own height or overflow. So the phone case was not reproduced after these attempts, and merging depends on the owner's phone check; the only cause that reproduced it is the dock sitting higher than `--float-bottom` says (gate variant `raised60`). Defence for that: Dock.tsx writes `--dock-clear` (its used `bottom` plus its height) on `<html>`, and the end padding is the larger of the token sum and `--dock-clear + --dock-shade + --float-gap`. A1 does not depend on the hide-on-scroll: at the scroll end the dock is shown and still clear. Needs the owner's phone check. `--dock-clear` also covers a stale `--rest-h` or a dock grown by larger text, since it measures the dock's used position and height. Each end padding is written first as a plain `calc()` and then with `max()`, so an engine without `max()` keeps the token end. The rules live in the BUG-22 block, overriding line 109 and the I1.6 rest rule by coming later.
  - Kept visible at the top of a page rather than hidden until needed: one small scroll clears it, and hiding it by default would make Escobar harder to reach.
  **Why**: the card's acceptance (A1, A2), the owner's report, and the pattern users already know from other apps. Tested in `tests/hideOnScroll.test.ts` and the BUG-22 gate block (Train, Today, History, Body at 390×844, rest banner up and down, Silent Black and Paper).
- **Decided (D-FG3, FG-3 builder)**: how the §5 checks read the doc (`src/formguide/check/`, `tests/formguide/checks.test.ts`, `npm run fg:check <id>`).
  - Supervisor rulings applied: stops are D-FG2's (100 even intervals per moving phase, none inside a hold or rest); the check also holds a rep to 3–5 s with both moving phases (§2), since outside that the spacing leaves GU-7a's ¼ % of a 4 s rep. The 2 KB file budget is gzip level 9 of the `.ts` source (lateral raise 1465 B). Checks whose subject is not built yet run against provisional shapes in `check/`: `machines.ts` (lever, carriage, cable, pads, setting adjusters; FG-7 may extend it but keeps these fields), `view.ts` `PARTS` (FG-5) and the front-only rig adapter (FG-6 adds side and back), `effort.ts` (the effort curve; FG-4's shimmer should read it). A file that uses a missing machine, part, view or pose fails the checks that need it, naming what is missing.
  - smoothness: (a)–(c) run over each moving phase of every correct rep, sampled at 1/120 s of the file's own tempo; a hold has no moving phase, so only (d) applies. The grip is the equipment's hand or foot points (all four when it names none), read in the body's frame: the sampler's 0.2° sway drift runs across every phase edge by design and alone puts the lateral raise's hand at 1.07 % edge speed; the sway channel itself stays under (d) and secondaryMotion. (c) reads the written values at the stops' exact times, because the written offsets are rounded to 1e-4 of the rep and that rounding alone fails a plain minimum-jerk raise at 4.5 s (3.07×) or 3.5 s (up to 49×).
  - mistakeSane needs two tells (§5 researcher), a delta ≥ 5° or ≥ 20 % of the research range on each, and the figure's fill outline (strokes not counted) inside `camera.full` at all 481 samples. mistakeDiffers compares the peak speed of the fastest grip point, rep 0 against the mistake, and fails when either peak is 0 (no motion is not a difference); a hold passes when its sag delta, read as the largest change of any one channel between the two figures over the hold, is ≥ 5°. The range checks are written as `!(min ≤ v ≤ max)` so a NaN fails.
  - The key moments are start, mid first move, end of first move and mid second move from the rep-0 windows (the lateral raise's 0, 0.125, 0.25, 0.625); a hold uses its quarters.
  - Machine drive (review of PR #50): a machine file needs at least one drive part, and every hand or foot in `equipment.attach` must be the `attach` of a drive part; handsOnHandle then holds each such point within 0.5 units of its part's anchor. The stop rule is the pure `stopsRule(stops, windows)`, tested on its own.
  - feetPlanted: each sole stays within 0.5 units of its start and of the floor (standing and seated; other poses have no rule yet); a ballistic file may leave it inside `equipment.release` and must be back within 0.5 after. A foot on a machine part is left to handsOnHandle.
  - muscleTiming: the torque model is the lab's (`effort.ts` header); "falls in the lower" reads as the second moving phase, the return (the lower for lift_first, the lift for lower_first), where the load falls in both; `mistake.muscles` replaces a muscle's effort (absolute), as the §2 lat-pulldown example reads; the file must name at least one target and one keep-quiet muscle. Only `upper_traps` has a cited mistake coupling (the lab's shrug and thumbs-down terms); any other keep-quiet muscle needs `mistake.muscles` to show its rise.
  - secondaryMotion: sway amplitude is the largest |sway| over the correct reps; blade rhythm "present" is a written `movement.bladeRhythm` when a shoulder passes 30°.
  - hash is FG-2's snapshot (every correct rep and the mistake) against `check/hashes.json`; a new file records its hash there. sizeBudget's chunk is the gzip of the shipped form-guide source (not exercises/, research/, check/), an upper bound until FG-4 measures the built chunk.
  - targetVisible census: with the pattern table alone 4 of the library's 182 primary targets are hidden (cable external rotation: rotator_cuff in front; sumo deadlift: adductors in side; hip abduction: glutes and abductors in front). Fix: the back overlays add `adductors` (the adductor magnus is the inner back of the thigh), and those three files override their view to back with a `viewWhy` (`CENSUS_VIEW_OVERRIDES`); then zero are hidden. The hip abduction machine is drawn back (§4).
  - Gaps left for the rig and machine cards (no sampler change here; FG-2's hash c1ac61634cd68bd4 unchanged): the sampler's fixed 0.2° sway drift moves a standing shoulder about 1.5 units, so no machine file can meet machinePivot's 0.05 or bodyOnPad's 0.01 until FG-7 holds the body still on a machine; hold tremor and the breath cycle inside a hold are not in the stops (D-FG2); written offsets are rounded to 1e-4 (a finer rounding would change the hash); the front rig draws blade rhythm for abduction only, not flexion; the setup moment is `setupMarkup` until FG-7 draws machines.
  - Found on the lateral raise: its mistake's sway delta swings the left dumbbell to x = -84.5, 4.5 units past the lab's viewBox (-80); the correct figure stays inside. Supervisor ruling (2026-09-28): the exaggerated sway is part of the lesson, so the frame fits it: `VIEWBOXES.standingFront` widens from the lab's [-80, -6, 560, 600] to [-88, -6, 576, 600] (symmetric about x = 200), mistakeSane stays strict, and a test holds both figures at least 1 unit inside it. FG-1's figure tests and FG-2's hash are unchanged (neither reads the viewBox).
  **Why**: every limit is §5's, research.json's or GU-7a's; each reading above is the one that measures the motion rather than a rounding or a design constant, and each is pinned by a test in `checks.test.ts`.

- **Decided (D-FG5, FG-5 builder)**: the free-weight parts library (`src/formguide/parts/`, `tests/formguide/parts.test.ts`, gate block FG-5).
  - Scope: dumbbell (moved out of `figureFront.ts`), barbell with plates, EZ bar, kettlebell, bench with angle, rack (a squat stand), box, loose plate, and the **pull-up bar**, added because the hanging leg raise in the owner's version 1 cut needs it. Dumbbell and barbell first (the Romanian deadlift). Each part draws a front and a side view.
  - Plate count: the app's own Plate Sense (`plateBreakdown`, `src/brain/units.ts`), so the picture loads what the lifter would: the gym's plate set and bar weight, the fewest plates at or under the load, the rest reported as `remainderKg`. No second plate rule in the form guide.
  - Sizes: barbell = IWF men's bar (2200 mm, 1310 mm between collars, 415 mm sleeves, 28 mm shaft); bench = IPF (pad top 42-45 cm, drawn 44; 30 cm wide); box = 20 x 24 x 30 in plyo box; kettlebell = competition size (280 mm tall, 210 mm bell) at every load. Drawing rules, not measurements (flagged): plate diameters (450 mm for ≥ 19 kg, IWF's; then 400, 330, 230, 190, 160 mm by weight) and widths (15 + 1.25 mm per kg); EZ bar 1200 mm, 10 kg unless the gym's profile says otherwise; rack 2.2 m uprights, bench angle −20° to 90°; pull-up bar 2.3 m high, 500 mm grip. The dumbbell part's heads grow with ∛kg (7 kg = the lab's, clamped 0.75-1.6) when drawn on its own; in the figure they keep the lab's size and only the label follows the load, because FG-4 passes the logged kg and a 12 kg head already pushed the lateral raise's mistake past `standingFront`'s left edge (−90.6 < −88; pinned by a test).
  - Side view of a bar is end-on and orthographic, plates concentric; each inner plate steps 1.6 units up-left so equal plates still show one rim each. The far side's plates are not drawn (behind the body).
  - Budget: every drawn shape counts (path, rect, circle, polygon…), not only `<path>`; at most 9 plates a side (the rest counted in `hidden`), so the worst case is 22 shapes. Circles are cubic Béziers because `check/svg.ts` measures arcs by their end points only.
  - Anchors: hands on the bar axis, shoulders at the rig's shoulder joints (396 mm apart), feet at the rig's standing stance (185 mm), bench back 450 mm up the pad from the hinge. `asDrawing` turns fixed parts into `bodyOnPad` pads and a held part into `handsOnHandle` slides along its path.
  - Check hook (called out in the PR): `check/view.ts` `PARTS` now reads the library's worst-case drawing per part; one FG-3 test that used `barbell` as the missing part now uses `sled` and also asserts the barbell passes. No limit changed.
  - A4: the lateral raise's figure markup is byte for byte unchanged in all five themes; its hash `c1ac61634cd68bd4` is unchanged because the hash reads joint stops only, never the dumbbell's geometry.
  - Risk: a front-view barbell (2.2 m = 667 units) is wider than `standingFront` (576 units); a front-view barbell exercise needs its own camera (FG-6/FG-8). The sampler's 0.2° sway drift (D-FG3) moves a standing back and feet just past `bodyOnPad`'s 0.01 drift, so pad exercises need the body held still (FG-6/FG-7).
  **Why**: one source for plates (the app's), official dimensions where they exist and named drawing rules where they don't, and budgets counted strictly.
- **Decided (D-FG6, FG-6 builder)**: the side view figure (`src/formguide/rig/figureSide.ts`, the side half of `rig/pose.ts`, `tests/formguide/side.test.ts`, gate block FG-6).
  - Drawing: the FG-1 front figure's units and landmarks (head top 27, shoulder 134, hip 262-272, knee 440, ankle 544, floor 566), its painter (`bodyPal` gradients, rim light, brush lines, `--l`/`--ink` outlines), bare skin above the waist and pants and shoes below, as in the front. The figure faces screen right. 130 paths (cap 260; the front is 204); `figureSide.ts` 7.7 KB gzip (cap 10 KB). The FormGuidePlayer chunk grows by 0.01 KB gzip (25.90 → 25.91 KB): nothing in the player draws the side view yet, so it is tree-shaken out.
  - Mirror rule: facing left is the same drawing reflected about x = 200 with the sides swapped, so the near limbs are the lifter's left and read the `_l` channels (`mirror` in the file, `SideOptions.mirror`, `SideFrameOptions.mirror`). Tested on every attachment point and joint in all four poses, and it fails without the swap.
  - Layering: the order is far leg, far arm (`fg-arms-behind`), trunk, near leg, hip block (pants over the pelvis, so the glute covers the thigh's root), near arm (`fg-arms-front`, last). The near arm must cover the near leg (a deadlift's hanging arms), so its group sits in the pelvis group and its transform is written as the whole spine × chest × shoulder matrix; it moves exactly as a child of the chest (tested against the forward kinematics). The `layer` channel does nothing in the side view: §3 says the near arm is always in front, and the far arm is always behind the trunk.
  - Signs, facing right: shoulder, elbow and hip flexion turn the limb forward, the knee bends back, `ankle_flex` + is dorsiflexion, `torso_lean` + leans back (joints.ts, ranges.ts). The shoulder rises with flexion above 30° by FG-1's blade rhythm (`riseOf`).
  - Poses: standing turns the pelvis by hip − knee + ankle of the near leg so the near foot stays flat on its spot (206, 566) and sways about it; seated keeps the pelvis upright with the feet on the floor; `lying_supine` (head left) and `lying_prone` (head right, face down, feet pointed 30°) rest the pelvis's contact point (sacrum or hip front, read with the trunk straight so a back extension lifts only the chest) on the surface. Supine, the upper back is read through the trunk's actual bend, so a crunch lifts the back and a bridge (trunk extended) lifts the hips off it (review r2: a test fails when `S_BACK` is dropped); prone, the hip front and chest are read with the trunk straight, so a back extension lifts the chest. A lying figure is moved along the floor (supine 34, prone 46 units) so it fits the standing camera's 576 units.
  - Support height: a lying file names no bench, so the check rig (`sideGuideRig`) sets the surface from the start pose: as high as the feet need to reach the floor (a bench press lies on its bench), else the floor. The stills place the FG-5 bench (44 cm pad) under the back.
  - Muscle overlays: the 20 of §3's side list, one tint (`fg-t-<muscle>_<near>`) and one shimmer band (`fg-b-…`) each, on the near side, hidden until the player sets opacity. The parts draw them from one table (`OVERLAY_D`), so the A3 bite test removes a muscle from the drawing code itself, not from its output. The tint colour comes from `roles` (target, help, quiet; quiet paints `--mistake` on the mistake figure); default target.
  - Arm solve: `solveSideArm` puts the fist's centre on a target (a bar, a handle), iterating because the shoulder rises with flexion; a target out of reach gets the straight arm pointing at it. A back-squat grip is a turned-out arm whose drawn projection bends the other way, so `turnedOut` returns elbow_flex 0 to −180; a real back-squat file (FG-8) will need its research to allow that drawn value or a turned-out arm channel.
  - fg:check: `check/view.ts` is outside this card's write scope; the wiring (`if (view === 'side') return sideGuideRig(g)` in `rigFor`) goes to card V1-06; the test wires it with `vi.mock` and runs every §5 check on a supine file (bench press) and a prone file (superman), both test fixtures with their own research and hashes: all 20 pass. `markupOf` in the player still throws for a side file until FG-8 wires the player.
  - A4 likeness: `docs/design/form-guide-lab/` is not in the repo (FG-5 review found the reference PNGs missing), so the stills were judged against the FG-1 figure: same palette, rim, brush lines, proportions and pants/shoe treatment. Stills attached to the PR.
  - Risks: lying figures fill the standing camera edge to edge (prone −87.3..481 of −88..488); FG-8 should add a lying camera to `VIEWBOXES` (`model.ts`, not in this scope). Frame rate of the side figure on the owner's phone is a device check (medium). The far limbs are hidden behind the near ones when both sides move together, by design.
  - Review r1 (likeness): the side view is the FG-1 person. The side depth over FG-1's front width, at the same landmark, follows the ANSUR II male means (Gordon et al. 2014): chest 0.65 (chest depth 257 / biacromial breadth 397 mm), waist 0.74 (243 / 327), hips 0.69 (buttock depth 252 / hip breadth 363), with the round limbs (thigh, upper arm, deltoid) at 1.0, each held to ± 0.08 by a test. To get there, the trunk is widened 1.2× about x 204, the hip block 1.1× about x 196 and the arm pieces 1.08×, all by static group transforms, and the upper arm gets fuller biceps and triceps. Measured: chest 0.645, waist 0.729, hips 0.739, thigh 0.999, upper arm 0.984, deltoid 1.031.
  - Review r1 (hips): the trunk outline runs on down behind the hip block, and the spine bends about the hip joint's height (y 272), so a squat or a hinge never opens a gap between the lower back and the glute. A test holds the trunk's lowest back point inside the hip block and requires at least 400 units² of overlap, in the squat bottom, a 120° hinge and standing.
  - Review r1 (details): the thigh's end and the shin's top are rounded, so the back of the knee has no step. On the feet, the neck lifts the head by half the trunk's forward tilt, so a squat looks ahead and down instead of at the floor (a drawing rule). The squat stills balance the bar over the mid-foot (x 224): the top leans the whole body 6.9° at the ankle; the bottom (hip 120°, knee 120°, ankle 38°) leans the trunk. The ankle's 38° is loaded dorsiflexion, past AAOS's unloaded 20°, and FG-1's long thigh (168 units against 126 for the shin and foot) needs a strong trunk lean. FG-8's squat research has to allow both.
  **Why**: one drawing for both facings, one skeleton and painter shared with the front, and the layering done by static group order and transforms only (§3), each rule with a test that fails when it is broken.

## Readiness inputs and chronic load (BUG-16, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-B16, BUG-16 builder)**: how readiness treats acute load and thin inputs (COACHRULES-F2, RECOVERY-F2/F8/F9/F10, PROGRESSION-F12; Plan Appendix B "self-report leads").
  - Chronic load divides by the days training actually covers, `min(28, days since the first session + 1)`, not a fixed 28. The 14-day / 3-session gate (BR-03) is unchanged. Steady training on days 14 to 27 now reads about 1.0, not up to 2.
  - The load part scores 1 up to ratio 1.3 (`SYSTEMIC_LOAD_RATIO`, the systemic factor's own start) and falls to 0 at 1.5. Below 0.5 it adds the driver "training load is well above your recent weeks".
  - Load confirms, it never leads: a low load part counts in full only when another input is also past its driver line. Otherwise it is raised to the green line (67), so it cannot lower the band by itself. With load as the only input the result is green, "normal", low confidence, and the coach shows a low-confidence "Training load is up" note (asks for a check-in) instead of an alert. Chosen over returning null because an existing test (BR-19) requires a result once the load part exists, and over a red/amber band with no advice because a band the app then ignores would still count toward the lighter week.
  - "Reduce" needs today's check-in or 2+ inputs past their driver lines; a red band from one other input gives "no_increase", and the red note then says "Keep today's loads where they are" instead of "drop a set". The red band itself stays score-based, so one sharply raised resting HR still reads red (existing coach test kept) and still counts toward the 3-of-5 lighter-week trigger (D-A1 (6)); only acute load was ruled out of that by the card.
  - Copy names only the inputs that fed the score (`readinessWithInputs`; the result object keeps its shape). "Several signals" / "a mixed picture" only with 2+ inputs. The green Monday note names only inputs that read well (one past its driver line is left out; "is" for one, "are" for two, "are all" for three or more) and is tagged `gated: 'health'` when it names sleep, resting HR or HRV, so it stays off the coach when health sharing is off (BUG-20). Whenever the load driver is set on a green day, the "Training load is up" note shows instead of the Monday praise.
  - Known risk: the systemic recovery factor (`recovery.ts` `systemicFactor`, ratio above 1.3 slows every muscle) and the load part both come from the same sessions, so a load spike on a scheduled day can lower muscle recovery too and the two can count as "2 agreeing inputs" (red, "reduce") with no check-in. The systemic slowdown is capped at 1.25 and muscle recovery also needs real recent dose on today's muscles to fall under 60 %, so this needs heavy recent training of those muscles; left as is for this card and named for the owner.
  - Train's amber hold names readiness ("Readiness is middling today…") unless the muscle is under 60 % recovered.
  - One existing test changed its input, not its assertion: `tests/readiness.test.ts` "is red with reduce advice when recovery and resting HR are both poor" had no scheduled split, so recovery never counted and resting HR was the only input. It now schedules a push split so both inputs count, as its name says.
  **Why**: plan Appendix B and the audit's correct behaviour; checked in `tests/readiness-bug16.test.ts` (each test fails on `main` b28bfe5 and passes after).
- **Decided (D-B17, BUG-17 builder)**: recovery model states (RECOVERY-F1, F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, F11; plan 6.11 points 5, 7, 8; App D.1).
  - More than 5 days to go (F1): the ready time is solved up to the 120 h cap as before and, if the muscle is still under 90 % there, on to the model's own 7-day floor (100 % by definition), so `hoursLeft` is always the real time left. A muscle with more than 120 h still to go gets `beyondCap: true` and no ready window; Today, the Body tile and detail, and the muscle panel then say "5+ days", and the coach's next-session warning says "in more than 5 days". Everything else shows its real hours, so "5+ days" never lingers once less than 5 days remain (review of 82c4625: at 110 h it had still read "5+ days" and the coach warned about a session past the real ready time). The flag lives on the computed result only; nothing new is stored.
  - Mark as fresh (F3): doses at or before the latest mark up to now never stack again, also after later sessions; a mark later than `now` (coach projections into the past) does not count yet. `fRef`, the muscle's typical dose, still reads doses before the mark: it sizes a session and is not a residual, and dropping them would make one easy set after a mark read as a full session.
  - Noise margin (F4): an e1RM change counts only beyond two typical errors, TE = 4 % (plan's default personal TE; App D.1 gives 2-5 %), so the up-step needs a drop of 8 % or more. Chosen over "confirmed over 2 sessions" because that needs the previous comparison's result, which is either a new stored field (owner approval) or a history re-scan at every finish. The down-step rule (held or better while predicted at or under 65 %) is unchanged: predicted 65 % is already far from ready, and the audit found no false down-steps. A personal TE from the user's own consecutive sessions is left for later.
  - Decay (F4): each finished session that trains a muscle as primary without moving its tauScale moves it 5 % of the way back to 1.0 (about 14 sessions to halve the gap, roughly 7 weeks at twice a week). Per trained session rather than per day, so it needs no stored date and a rebuild reproduces it exactly. Muscles not trained keep what was learned; observations count only real steps. A comparison beyond the noise margin is evidence even when the 0.7-1.6 clamp stops the step, so that muscle does not decay that session.
  - Layoff (F6): a comparison calibrates only when the exercise's previous matched session is under 7 days old (the model's own window; beyond it the predicted pct is the 100 % floor whatever happened). This covers the card's "skip the first session after a 28+ day layoff" and also a 1-3 week break. The gap is counted in calendar days (the exercise summary carries only the day), so a comparison 6-7 calendar days apart but under 168 h is skipped: it errs toward not learning.
  - Rebuild (F7): `rebuildRecoveryModel` now calls `replayRecoveryModel` (brain/recovery.ts), which builds every session's doses once with tauScale 1 and, at each step, applies that step's tauScale to the doses before it (x × 1 = x, so the numbers match finish bit for bit), with the real training start and the last summary per exercise. To make a dose independent of what came after it, the whole-body factor for a session now reads sessions that started up to it, not later sessions on the same day. A same-day second session no longer changes the first one's recovery time; this changes displayed recovery only for same-day doubles. `session.ts` (outside the card's listed write scope) changed only to call the new function.
  - Check-in (F5): the sheet opens on today's answers; saving skips unanswered fields and merges soreness per muscle, so nothing already saved is wiped. The day is fixed when the sheet opens, so a sheet left open past midnight saves to the day it showed. A rating cannot be cleared from the sheet (same as before).
  - Flat history (F11): the check-in z-scores use a minimum spread of 0.5 rating points, so a user whose history is all 3s still gets a 1 counted as a bad day (it scored neutral before). `readiness.ts` is outside the card's listed write scope; the change is the one `zScore` line the card's F11 item needs.
  **Why**: the audit's correct behaviour and plan 6.11; checked in `tests/recovery-bug17.test.ts` (every test except two regression guards fails on `main` cfb7dc7 and passes after).

- **Decided (D-B15, BUG-15 builder)**: how the lighter week starts, runs and ends (PROGRESSION-F1/F4/F5/F6/F26, COACHRULES-F7/F8; F3.3, plan 6.13, BELL23).
  - The window is derived from saved data only: `AppState.deload` (`startDay`..`endDay`) stays saved after the week ends, so it is passed to `suggestNext` as a new context field `lastDeload` (next to the active-only `deload`). No saved-data change. Only the latest lighter week is known, because starting a new one replaces the record; the 28-day wait below keeps an older window out of reach of the plateau rule for most of its 8 weeks.
  - In the week, every session cuts from the lift's last session before `startDay` (×0.9 load, ×0.6 sets, the fixed P4 factors), so the target never compounds. Loads snap down on the gym's equipment as before (LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS.md row "deload"), else to the half kg. A lift first logged inside the week repeats that session (factor 1), since it has no pre-week level.
  - The first session after the week, for a lift trained in it, is a hold at the pre-week top load and reps (`mode: 'hold'`, "The lighter week is over. This is your level from before it."); bodyweight and carries match their pre-week best. After that, every rule reads the pre-week sessions plus the new ones; lighter-week sessions are never the base or the evidence. Time away (re-entry) still counts every session. Timed holds are untouched (D-A1 point 1).
  - Lighter-week sessions are left out of the plateau, stall and decline evidence in `suggestNext`, `deloadTrigger` and the coach's `progress.declining`, `progress.plateau` and `progress.plateau-lever` notes. BUG-14's rule and tests are unchanged.
  - A new offer from the lift triggers (a)-(c) waits 28 days after the week ends (`DELOAD_TRIGGER.cooldownDays`; BELL23 and ROGERSON24 put deloads about every 4-6 weeks, no source fixes the exact wait; 28 matches the 4-week history minimum of D-A1 point 6). Trigger (d), red readiness on 3 of 5 days, is a safety signal and still offers one.
  - Triggers (b) and (c) read completed weeks only (weeks 1-3 and 1-2 back); the current week is still being trained.
  - Set cuts reach Train: a suggestion whose set list is a deliberate cut (lighter week, red "reduce") carries `cutSets`; Train sets the planned rows past it aside while empty (dimmed, no placeholder, "Not today · lighter week" or "· readiness", not the next-up row) and the header counts the kept rows. A set-aside row the user fills counts as usual, and empty rows are already dropped on save, so the session store (`session.ts`) is unchanged.
  - Live autoregulation never says "add load" when the suggestion holds the load (`holdLoad`: an active lighter week, amber or red readiness, or an Escobar cut factor under 1). An easy first set then gets "Keep <target> for the next set." ("easy sets are the point today") rather than silence, so the lifter still hears that the easy set was right; the target is restated in the equipment's unit, never re-snapped. A missed first set at max effort still gets its ease-off advice. The gate's I14 contrast block (another task's, not edited) measures this same line on its amber-readiness seed.
  - Wiring outside the card's write scope, one line each, so every screen shows the same target: `Coach.tsx` and Escobar's `progressionCtxFor` pass `lastDeload`; Escobar's live view passes `holdLoad`. One existing test changed its assertion because it encoded the bug: `tests/escobar/read.test.ts` expected "Try …" (add load) after an easy set on its fixture's amber day; it now expects "Keep <target> kg for the next set.", and a new missed-set case keeps its "reads the first working set" check.
  **Why**: the audit's correct behaviour for each finding; checked by the BUG-15 tests in `tests/progression.test.ts`, `tests/deload.test.ts`, `tests/coach.test.ts`, `tests/live.test.ts`, `tests/workout/set-aside.test.ts` (each fails on `main` 8365d9e and passes after) and the gate block "BUG-15 set cut rows".
  - Back-to-back weeks (review round 1): red readiness on 3 of 5 days still offers a new week inside the 28-day wait, and Escobar's `propose_deload` can start one too; both stay, because a red run is a safety signal. So a new week can start before the pre-week level is back. `acceptDeload` then chains: when no session was logged after the previous week ended (up to and including the new start day), the saved window keeps the previous week's `startDay` and runs to the new week's end (`chainedStartDay`). Every rule above then reads the original pre-week level as the base (72.5 × 3, not week 1's 65.5 × 2), and the week after holds it. No new field: the chain lives in `startDay`. The day count ("day N of 7") comes from the window's last seven days (`lighterWeekDay`) in the coach card, Train's reason, Escobar's brief and its live read. A session logged between the weeks re-establishes the level, so the new week starts fresh; that is judged over all lifts, so a lift not trained in that gap still bases on its lighter session (named risk).
  - The weekly review's lift direction (`weeklyReview.ts`), Escobar's `get_exercise_history` (`read.ts`) and its `lift_trend` card (`show.ts`) leave lighter-week sessions out of the plateau and trend evidence too; the charts and records still show every session.
  **Open**: the explain_method text for the lighter week does not mention the 28-day wait (outside this card).

## Live session end time, pauses and rest (BUG-19, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-B19, BUG-19 builder)**: how a live session's end, duration, rest and timing trust are measured (DATES-F1, F2, F3, F5, F11).
  - **A forgotten Finish (A1)**: when Finish comes more than 12 min after the last committed set (`FORGOT_FINISH_SEC`, the plan's longest plausible gap between two live commits, 6.17.2 :815), the session ends 5 min after that set (`FINISH_MARGIN_SEC`: re-racking, a stretch, the walk out). `loggedAt` stays the real Finish time. Finishing within 12 min of the last set ends it at Finish, as before, with no new step. The finish sheet shows the same duration that is saved, and when it was trimmed one line says "Saved as ending at HH:MM, 5 min after your last set. The time since is not counted." Chosen over asking "when did the session end?" because: it never adds a step; a Finish tapped the next morning is exactly when a person is least able to say when they stopped, so the question would mostly be skipped or guessed; the last set's time is measured, not remembered; and apps that leave it to a manual edit (Hevy's help asks you to edit the duration afterwards) leave the wrong number in place when nobody edits. Risk: work after the last logged set (an unlogged cardio finisher longer than 12 min) is cut. It is named on the sheet, and the load it drops is the unlogged part, which the app cannot rate anyway.
  - **Duration (A2)**: the saved `durationSec` is the pause-free training time, the number the finish sheet shows (plan scenario 6: a Pause the person tapped is a confirmed pause). `endedAt - startedAt` can now be longer than `durationSec` by the paused time; that is intended, and recovery uses `durationSec` for load.
  - **Warm-ups (A3)**: warm-ups and sets that were never committed (no `at`) are left out of `liveShare`, the burst share and the compressed check's set count. They are still saved and shown.
  - **Rest (A4)**: `restSec` is now rest, not the commit gap: the gap minus any Pause inside it, minus the new set's own working time (its `durationSec` when timed, else 3 s a rep, the self-selected pace of about 1.5 s up and 1.5 s down measured in PeerJ 2020;8:e8697), capped at 0 to 600 s. This changes plan :511 ("seconds since the previous commit"). The fidelity class uses the gap minus the pause, so the first set after a Pause stays live.
    - The pause inside a gap is known from the session's paused total at each commit, kept in memory only, so no saved-data shape changes. After an app restart in a session that has paused, the next set's gap is unknown: it is marked `delayed` with no `restSec` (plan scenario 6, "rest metrics skip the gap"); from then on it is known again. A session that never paused is always known. If the owner later approves storing the paused total per commit, this fallback goes away.
  - **Burst share (A5)**: `burstShare` counts every commit inside a run of 3 or more commits within 15 s, and nothing else, so sets that were only late (long gaps) no longer count (plan :821). `timingTrusted` also needs no `burst` flag (plan :803).
  - **Heart summary (review finding 1; the supervisor widened the scope on 2026-09-28 to `src/slices/workout/heart.ts`, `finishHeartCapture` only)**: the saved heart series and summary keep only samples up to the session's end (`t <= endedAt - startedAt`, in seconds). So after a forgotten Finish, the hours the watch stayed on reach neither the zones, the energy, the coverage nor the stored series. Samples recorded during a Pause stay in, as they were before this card. The saved data shape is unchanged.
  - Two existing tests in `tests/session.test.ts` changed their expected `restSec` by the set's own time (210 → 180 for 10 reps, 120 → 96 for 8 reps), because the definition changed as the card asked; nothing else in them changed.
  **Why**: the audit's correct behaviour and the card's acceptance; checked in `tests/session-bug19.test.ts` (each test fails on `main` 39b26d7 and passes after; each fix was removed one at a time and a test failed each time) and the gate block "BUG-19 finish end time".
## Anonymous error reports, app side (item 7.5, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-C75, 7.5 app builder)**: how the app keeps to docs/ERROR-REPORTS.md and the shared `POST /errors` contract (batch of at most 20, at most 8 KB, allowlist).
  - Size: 8 KB = 8,192 bytes, as the doc says and as the Worker's `MAX_ERRORS_BODY_BYTES` now reads (`8 * 1024`, PR #34); both sides quote the same number (review of PR #35, finding 4). A single report too big for any request is dropped locally instead of being sent for a certain 413.
  - Batch: at most 20 reports per request, enforced in `packBatch` as well as by the 20-entry queue.
  - Count: a folded count stops at 100,000, the Worker's `MAX_COUNT`; above it the Worker answers 400 and the app would drop the whole batch (round-2 review).
  - One door out: `sendingAllowed()` is consent on and not automated. Automated means a Vitest run (`import.meta.env.MODE === 'test'`) or a WebDriver browser (`navigator.webdriver`, which Playwright sets), so neither the tests nor the gate can ever send. Online is checked in `trySend`; the 30 s poller, the `online` handler and each new report all go through this door.
  - Privacy beyond the doc's message cleaning: (1) a thrown or rejected value that is not an `Error` is reported only by its type ("non-Error string thrown"), since it can be any user content; (2) an error name must be a plain identifier of at most 80 characters, otherwise it becomes "Error"; (3) only real frame lines are parsed (V8's `at …`, Firefox/Safari's `fn@url`), never the message line at the top of a stack; a frame is kept only when its file is on this page's origin and is a bundle file by the Worker's `BUNDLE_PATH` rule (`/assets/<Name>-<8-char hash>.js` or `/sw.js`), and it is sent as that path; data:, blob:, extension, other-host, bare and any other paths never go, and query and hash are dropped (review finding 1); (4) the store-save report uses a fixed message and never reads the save error's text.
  - Message cleaning is the Worker's `scrubMessage`, ported as is so both sides use one rule (review finding 2): straight, curly and backtick quotes; a quote runs to the last quote of its kind, so nested quotes and apostrophes are covered; an opening quote with no closing one removes the rest of the message; then digits become #, cut to 300.
  - Error names: one rule on both sides, the Worker's `ERROR_NAME`. The app's own names carry no '-' (`SaveFailed`, `LoadRecovered`, `NonError`), so they pass whichever rule either side holds (review finding 3).
  - "Sent once a day": after a 204, each sent signature is remembered with its UTC day in the local queue record (`sent`), and the same signature stays quiet until the next UTC day. Before sending, repeats fold into one entry with a count, as before. Only today's entries are kept.
  - Boot: a load failure this boot (unreadable save quarantined, or restored from the backup copy) is reported once as kind `boot`, name `LoadRecovered`. A throw during boot itself was already reported.
  - Android version and device model (optional fields) are not filled yet: they need `@capacitor/device`, a new dependency that needs the supervisor's OK. The Worker accepts reports without them.
  - Known limit (review of PR #35, not blocking): the doc's message rule removes digits and quoted text only, so unquoted words in a message the app builds itself would pass. No reported trigger puts user text into a message today (store-save, boot and transport send fixed text; a non-Error is reported by type only). Rule for new trigger sites: never build an Error message from user data.
  - No theme-test block: the switch and the ask reuse existing classes (`.banner`, `.hint`, `.small`, Toggle) and add no CSS; the gate's `7.5-toggle` block checks their contrast in Silent Black and Paper instead.
  **Why**: every rule is the doc's or the Worker's; each is pinned by a test in `tests/errors.test.ts` that fails when its line is removed (mutation run in PR #35).

## Anonymous error reports, Worker side (item 7.5, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-C75W, 7.5 Worker builder; carried in PR #35 because the Worker PR #34 deploys and holds only escobar-worker/**)**: how `POST /errors` meets docs/ERROR-REPORTS.md "Server".
  - Storage is Cloudflare D1, one of the two the doc names (the earlier draft used a Durable Object, which the doc does not name). D1 over KV: the KV free tier allows 1,000 writes a day, and every accepted request writes at least 3 rows (report plus two counters); D1 allows 100,000, and SQL gives the 90-day delete and the summary directly. Tables are created on first use, so there is no migration command for the owner.
  - 8 KB means 8192 bytes, as the doc says, on both sides: the Worker's `MAX_ERRORS_BODY_BYTES` and the app's `MAX_BODY_BYTES` are both `8 * 1024`, and the app packs every batch to at most this. A batch holds at most 20 reports, and a report's count is at most 100,000 (`MAX_COUNT`; the app caps a folded count there, so a busy signature never gets its batch refused).
  - Message cleaning, one rule for app and Worker (review of 06efaba, finding 2): straight, curly (“ ” ‘ ’) and backtick quotes count; a quote runs to the LAST quote of its kind, so nested quotes and apostrophes (`"hello "Dave"`, `'O'Brien'`) are covered; an opening quote with no closing one removes the rest; an existing "…" is left alone so cleaning twice changes nothing. Chosen over "cut at the first quote" because it keeps useful text after the quote (for example "is not valid JSON"). Unquoted words are not removed, so no trigger may put user text into a message unquoted (the app's triggers use fixed text today).
  - Frame files (review of 06efaba, finding 1): only the shapes `vite build` produces are kept, `/assets/<Name>-<8-character hash>.js` and `/sw.js`, after the scheme, host, query and hash are removed. Any other path (a folder, extra words or digits outside the hash, file:, blob:, data:) is dropped, because a path can carry personal text. The app keeps the same shapes (`bundleFile` in `src/errors/scrub.ts`). Risk: a Vite upgrade that changes the hash length drops all frames (less detail, no leak); the W4 bundle test names the rule to update.
  - Error names: one rule on both sides, a code identifier (`-` and `.` allowed) of at most 80 characters; anything else is stored as `Error`. The app's own names have no `-` (`SaveFailed`, `LoadRecovered`, `NonError`), so they pass unchanged.
  - Unknown fields are rejected (400), as the doc says. "Drop anything not on the allowlist" is applied to values: the stored row is built from named fields only, the message is re-cleaned with the shared rule, frames outside the app bundle are dropped, and an unexpected route, error name, version, OS or device becomes a neutral value or is left out, so the report is still useful.
  - The IP limit needs a per-IP counter, and an IP is personal data. The counter key is an HMAC-SHA-256 of hour and IP keyed by the `ERRORS_SUMMARY_TOKEN` secret, and counters from earlier hours are deleted on the next request and by the daily cron. One secret serves both uses so the owner sets one value; `POST /errors` answers 503 until it is set, so no IP is ever hashed without a key.
  - A request counts against the limits even when it is then refused; the doc limits requests, not accepted reports.
  - "Daily summary" is a pull: `GET /errors/summary` returns the last 24 hours, and marks a signature new when it was first stored inside that window. No email or push service was added (that would be a new provider).
  - Risk: D1 unavailable. Mitigation: the Worker answers 503 without detail; the app keeps its queue and backs off. Risk: the owner merges before creating the database. Mitigation: `wrangler.toml` holds a placeholder id, so the deploy fails loudly and the running Worker stays as it was.
  **Why**: docs/ERROR-REPORTS.md and the shared contract between PR #34 and PR #35; checked in `escobar-worker/test/errors.test.ts` (W1-W9), and every rule there was shown to fail its test when broken (27 code mutations, all caught).

## Exercise-title sweep on open and while logging (UI-1, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-UI1, UI-1 builder)**: how the owner's sweep comes back without I3's always-on loop.
  - One keyframe, `exercise-shimmer`, holds every paint property (accent band over a `var(--text)` background, text clipped to it). Nothing is set on the title outside the keyframes, so at rest, after the run and under reduced motion the title is plain `var(--text)`. The band is `var(--accent)` only; no dim text tone, so it never reads as "disabled".
  - Opening: `2s linear 2` (two passes, 4 s) on `.exercise.active .exname`. Logging: the same animation switches to `infinite` while a `.set-grid` input has focus. Same name, so the switch never restarts the run; on blur the count drops back to 2, the run is already past its end, and the sweep stops at once.
  - Effort tap: a button keeps focus after a tap, so plain `:focus-within` would sweep for the whole rest. Instead Train.tsx sets `data-hold` on the card for 2 s after an effort tap (one delegated `onClick` on the card, away from the set rows); the sweep runs while `data-hold` and focus are both there. CSS alone cannot time a hold without a second animation, which would break "at most one exercise-* animation".
  - Two passes of 2 s: the card's "about 2 s, 2 passes". The band enters at the left edge and leaves at the right in each pass.
  - The F1 token lint allow list gains `exercise-shimmer` (a literal duration and one `infinite`), like the other decorative loops. The gate's idle check keeps it off its infinite-animation allow list, so an idle open card with a loop still fails.
  - Known risks: blurring within the first 4 s after opening lets the open run finish its two passes (at most 4 s). `:has()` needs WebView 105+; on older WebViews the logging sweep simply never shows (the open run still does). `background-position` repaints on the main thread while the sweep runs (4 s on open, and only while typing).
  **Why**: owner request 2026-09-27; checked by `tests/theme.test.ts` (UI-1 block) and the gate's `UI-1 shimmer` block, each failing on `main` 39b26d7 and passing after.

## Load menu (LT-1, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-LT1, LT-1 builder)**: points `docs/LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS.md` §2 leaves open.
  - `loggedKg` is a list of `LoggedLoad` (`sessionId`, `gymId?`, `kg`, `entered?`, `flags?`), built by `loggedLoads(sessions, exerciseId)`, so `loadMenu` itself applies the gym, flag, unit and two-session rules.
  - Learned loads join the menu only at ranks 3 and 4 (the card's "default united with"). A `known` profile is taken as it is: §1's 32 kg typed on a 25/30/32.5/35 ladder must not turn into a rung.
  - Any loaded, non-skipped set counts, warm-ups and drop sets included: a load someone lifted exists. Two sets in one session are one session.
  - A set without `entered` was typed in kg, as `Train.tsx` SuspectChip already reads it (`set.entered?.unit ?? 'kg'`).
  - "Flagged" (`isFlaggedLoad`) is BUG-18's read-time held rule (D-B18), read from `exerciseHistory`'s `held` sets, plus a stored `unit_suspect`. A stored `implausible_load` alone no longer counts: a repeat confirms the load (plan 6.17.4), and BUG-18's rule already covers it. `unit_suspect` stays because BUG-18 does not replace it. Under BUG-18 a load clean in two sessions is never held (the later one confirms the earlier), so the hold only bites when a set's reps are the typo.
  - `learned` needs two confirmed loads, and loads the default ladder already has count toward the two (they confirm the default).
  - "The default gym" is `gym_default`; when it was deleted, the first gym, the same fallback `resolveProfile` uses. A session whose gym was deleted counts for no gym.
  - The menu's unit: a default-source profile here (exercise, then group), else the most recent other gym's profile for this exercise (`source: 'other_gym'`), else the gym's default unit. That keeps today's unit for users who set it at another gym.
  - `jumpPct` is unrounded percent of `fromKg`; `Infinity` from 0 to a load, 0 from 0 to 0.
  **Why**: the spec is silent on these; each follows the card, the code it cites, or the §1 problem. Checked by the LT-1 block in `tests/plate-sense.test.ts`.
## Heart rules per the plan (BUG-21, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-B21, BUG-21 builder)**: D-A1 points 4, 5 and 7 (RECOVERY-F18, F34, F38, F39; COACHRULES-F24).
  - Rest end (point 4): heart rate may end an accessory's rest early (after its 60/90/120 s effort minimum, as before); a main lift's rest ends only when the user's timer runs out (`restDone`). The rest belongs to the set whose commit started it (supervisor decision, review r2). That set and its exercise are kept in memory only (`restStartedBy` in `session.ts`, beside the running rest; never in `RestState` or storage, so no saved-data change). Heart rate may end the rest early only when that record exists, the set is still there as a committed live working set of the same exercise, and the exercise is an accessory. In every other case the timer is the floor: no record (an app restart, or a rest started some other way), the set deleted, marked a warm-up, its exercise removed or substituted, or an unknown exercise. A warm-up or a delayed or retro set committed during the rest never starts a rest, so it never takes the rest over (review r1). Round 1 read the latest live working set instead, so deleting or re-kinding the set that started a main-lift rest fell back to an older accessory set and let heart rate end it early. In heart mode a main lift shows `bpm → target` until the heart rate settles, then the timer's countdown (with its 3-2-1 ticks). Nothing new is stored: the exercise is read from the session's own sets.
  - Pre-set heart rate (F18, still open after BUG-17): the rest target now starts from the heart rate before the set, not the bpm at the commit (the end-of-set peak). It is the lowest 15-second median (3 points) of the 5-second series between the previous commit, or the session start, and this commit: rest ends at the trough, and wrist HR lags the set's start by 5-15 s (App A.1). With fewer than 3 points in that window there is no pre-set bpm; heart mode still runs (the stream is LIVE) and the target is the plan's other term alone, resting + 0.35 × reserve, the upper bound of the plan's `min()`. The end-of-set bpm is never used as a stand-in. (`RestState.preSetBpm`'s comment in `models.ts`, a reserved file, still says it is absent when the stream was not LIVE; the field and its shape are unchanged.) RECOVERY-F17 (HRR60 never recorded) is not needed by the new drift and stays open. The live pre-set window starts at the previous commit of any kind (warm-ups included), while `sessionDrift`'s starts at the previous live working set, so the two can read a slightly different trough for the same set; both are troughs of the rest before it, and the live one only feeds the rest target (review r1, left as is).
  - Effort mismatch (point 5, P2): still 5+ rated sets with heart data in the session; within each exercise the reference is the set rated hardest (max, else ideal), its highest peak if several; an easy set at 90%+ of that peak is flagged. An exercise rated only easy has no reference. When the easy set reached or passed the reference the note says "reached" instead of a percentage over 100.
  - Drift (point 7, Appendix B): per session, across all live working sets with a real commit time (warm-ups, delayed and retro sets and sessions with untrusted timing are left out, plan 6.17.4). The session needs `durationSec` of 20+ min and 6+ sets with a pre-set HR. `drift% = 100 × (mean pre-set HR of the last 3 − the first 3) / the first 3`; flag above 8 %, or when the least-squares slope of `timeToReady` over set position is above +10 s per set. `timeToReady` is the time from a commit to the third of 3 consecutive points at or below that set's `restReadyBpm`, searched up to the next commit and the plan's 300 s cap (for the last set, the cap and the end of the stored series: the series is on wall-clock seconds, while `durationSec` is training time with pauses and a forgotten Finish taken out by BUG-19, so it only gates the 20 min); a rest that never got there is left out (the next set may simply have started early), and the slope needs 3+ known rests and a resting HR (else only drift% is judged). HRmax is `hrMax(profile)`, the same the live rest uses. The note says what rose and advises water and longer rests; it never cuts load. It reads the stored 5-second series, passed to the coach as `CoachContext.heartSeries` (the app and Escobar's loop both pass `getSeries`; Escobar's loop was not given it before, so `get_heart_session` had no series).
  - Escobar's `get_heart_session` reports the same drift and mismatch as the coach notes (fixes RECOVERY-F34: it ran the old drift over every set of every exercise). `intraSessionDrift` (the old F1.4 metric) is removed.
  - Tests changed because they encoded the old rules: `tests/heart.test.ts` loses the `intraSessionDrift` block and passes its effort-mismatch sets as one exercise (assertions unchanged); `tests/coach.test.ts` loses its two old `heart.drift` cases (replaced in `tests/heart-bug21.test.ts`, which also pins that the old 3-set pattern no longer fires); `tests/escobar/bug-20-privacy.test.ts` gives its fixture commit times and a heart series so both heart notes still fire, and matches the new copy. Wiring outside the card's write scope, one line each: `src/app/selectors.ts`, `src/escobar/session.ts`, `src/escobar/tools/context.ts`, `src/escobar/tools/read.ts`, `src/slices/workout/Train.tsx` (rest end).
  - Known risks: the pre-set trough and `timeToReady` rest on the 5-second series and the commit times; a set committed long after it ended (still "live" under 12 min) widens its window, which can only lower the trough. The 8 % and 10 s lines and the 0.35/+12 rest constants are the plan's heuristics, to be tuned on the GT6. Needs a real-watch check: heart-mode rest on a main lift and an accessory, and a 20+ min session that drifts.
  **Why**: owner decision D-A1 and COACHING-PLAN.md 6.4 and Appendix B; checked by `tests/heart-bug21.test.ts` (each test fails on `main` 6608f1a and passes after).
## Targets from straight working sets; implausible sets held (BUG-18, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-B18, BUG-18 builder)**: what targets are built from, when a set is held as implausible, and which sets can make a strength record (PROGRESSION-F3/F14/F23, COACHRULES-F3/F19; plan 6.13 records row, 6.17.4).
  - Targets use the straight working load: the load most working sets used, drop sets aside; a tie (a ramp or a pyramid with no repeated load) goes to the heavier load, which is what the app did before, so those users see no change. `topKg` stays the heaviest trusted load for records and history display; the new `workKg` / `workReps` / `workMinReps` feed targets.
  - Recovery calibration and held sets (review round 3): finish used to take the previous session's summary from the whole history (a typo there held), while the rebuild summarised each session on its own (the same typo counted), so a rebuild after any history edit learned a different model (BUG-17 A6). Chosen: finish, and `calibrateAfterSession`'s default, now summarise the previous session on its own too (`lastSummaryAlone`), so all three paths learn the same model. Not chosen: judging the rebuild's summaries against earlier sessions, because a held set's status depends on every later session up to the one being finished, so an exact match needs the whole prefix history at every step (about n³ work, past the 500 ms rebuild budget for 600 sessions). Cost: a typo on a max-effort set can nudge the saved recovery pace; left to BUG-24 with the other paths that still see held sets. Checked by the "finish and the rebuild learn the same recovery model" test in `tests/bug18.test.ts`, and BUG-17's own replay helper now mirrors finish with `lastSummaryAlone`.
  - The lighter week (BUG-15) and its first session back build on the working sets too: the week cuts from `workKg`, loaded carries included, and the back-to-level hold is `workKg` at `workReps`. A session whose sets are all held is skipped before the lighter-week filter, so when the only in-week session is held the lift reads as not trained in the week, and the first session after it gets the normal target ("keep the load and add a rep") rather than the back-to-level hold: the week's data is not trusted, and the gap is one rep.
  - An increase needs every set at the working load at the top of the range (`workMinReps`), twice. A lighter last set that is not marked as a drop set is treated like one for this: it is not at the working load, so it neither blocks nor earns the increase (100 × 12, 100 × 12, 80 × 10 counts as two sets at the top). The "add a rep" target still counts from the best set at the working load, so it never asks for fewer reps than were done (PROGRESSION-F24 stays with its own card).
  - A set is implausible by fidelity.ts's existing lines: load more than 25 % above the best trusted load so far (all earlier sessions, not only recent ones, so a return to an old load is not flagged), or above 500 kg; reps above 50, or 30 on a main lift above 70 % of its best e1RM. The load line is checked only for weighted and conditioning work (on bodyweight and assisted work the kg is added or helping weight, where big jumps are normal).
  - Plan 6.17.4 says a flagged set is held "until the load is repeated", so confirmation is read from set data, with no stored mark: another working set of the same exercise at that load or heavier (same session or any later one) confirms it; for reps, another set also past the rep limit. Three sets at a new load confirm each other. So no stored "confirmed" field is needed. The plan's in-session "Keep / Fix" question (6.17.3) would need one; not built here.
  - Which numbers leave held sets out: everything built on `exerciseHistory` with the earlier sessions in view, which is the e1RM and top load the app shows, trends and plateau (e1RM, top load and volume series), records (`recordsFor` / `allRecords`, the post-session debrief, the Train pill) and targets (`suggestNext`). A session whose only sets are held is skipped for targets.
  - Which still see them: content always counts (set counts, weekly sets and volume, the "last time" hint). Two number paths also still see held sets, because they read a session without its earlier sessions or read raw sets: recovery calibration (both the new session and the exercise's previous session are summarised on their own, at finish through `lastSummaryAlone` and in BUG-17's `replayRecoveryModel` rebuild alike, so only the 500 kg line applies there) and load snapping (`loggedLoads` in progression.ts counts every logged load as one the equipment has). The share card's per-exercise top set also shows what was typed. Follow-up card BUG-24.
  - Records: a record only a held set would set is returned by `recordsFor(..., { unconfirmed: true })`, marked `unconfirmed`; everything that celebrates or counts records (coach, History, weekly, share, Escobar) leaves it off. `recordsFor` judges which of the current sets are held against the prior sessions it is given, so the post-session debrief, which summarises the new session on its own, cannot praise a typo either. Once the load is repeated, the record appears on the day it was first lifted. On Train the pill reads "PR unconfirmed" (same pill, so the F9 pill checks still hold; its tip says it counts once lifted again) and does not buzz.
  - A strength (e1RM) record comes only from sets of 10 reps or fewer that are not rated easy (unrated reads as ideal, as e1rm.ts already does), and never from a load and rep count already done before: a new effort label on the same lift is not a stronger lift. The bar it must beat is still the best earlier e1RM from any effort.
  **Why**: the audit's correct behaviour and plan 6.13 / 6.17.4; checked in `tests/bug18.test.ts` (14 of 18 tests fail on `main` cfb7dc7 and all pass after) and the gate block "BUG-18 unconfirmed record".
## Form-guide player wiring (FG-4, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-FG4, FG-4 builder)**: what the player does where §6 and the card are silent.
  - Only exercises with a file in `src/formguide/exercises/` play an `ExerciseGuide` (today the lateral raise). The two GU-7a guides with no file yet (machine chest press, lat pulldown) keep the GU-7a stub until their files land (Batch V1), so the GU-7a gate block stays valid without editing it. `registry.ts` stays import-free for the main chunk; the loader is `import.meta.glob('../exercises/*.ts')` inside the player chunk (one chunk per file), reached through `playerFor(id)` so a failed exercise chunk takes GU-7a's "Demo could not load." path.
  - The three reps are one 12 s animation (`iterations: 1`) whose keyframes are rep 0, 1 and 2 of `sampleGuide` at offset `(rep + u) / 3`; runs of an unchanged value keep only their two ends (exact under linear interpolation). The mistake plays its one rep three times on the same timeline, in the correct rep's length (both are 4 s for the lateral raise). A mistake figure mounted mid-play takes the correct figure's `startTime`, so it starts in step (seeking and then `play()` lagged about 280 ms in the gate).
  - Effort → paint: tint opacity = 0.6 × effort, shimmer band opacity = (effort − 0.5) / 0.5 clamped. FG-1's band is a gradient fill, not a stroke, so there is no `dashoffset` to drive; the band's opacity carries the "load peaks here" signal. Keep-quiet tints are faint in the correct rep (< 0.2) and rise in the mistake (tested).
  - Chips: one "Zoom" chip (the file's `camera.zoom`; its bubble reads the cues) and one "Mistake" chip (`aria-pressed`). No muscle hotspots on the FG-1 figure yet. Compare mode doubles the camera width and places the mistake one camera to the right; the hint line shows the mistake name and its tells. Pictures with Mistake on shows the mistake's four moments ("Mistake: …"), so reduced-motion users can still see it.
  - Load: the live session's latest committed working set (session.ts `isCommitted`: `status: 'committed'` or an `at` stamp), else the last working set of the last session with the exercise (warm-ups and 0 kg never count); read only.
