FG-1's dumbbell is printed "KG", so it shows kg to 0.1; the camera label shows the load in the user's unit. `loadFrom: 'bodyweight'` draws no label until a bodyweight guide exists (F13 wiring comes with it). `fixed` uses the file's `kg`.
  - Captions come from the tempo ("Lift, 1 s", "Hold, 0.5 s", "Lower slowly, 2 s", "Reset, 0.5 s"; tempo line "1 s up · 2 s down"); the rep pill and caption read the rep's own slowed windows. A theme change while the sheet is open (playing, paused mid-run or ended) remounts the figure paused on the start pose, with the rep pill and caption reset, (its palette is resolved from the tokens) and regenerates the pictures.
  - Known risks: about 34 animations of up to ~600 keyframes per figure, two figures in compare mode; frame rate on the owner's phone is A6 (not measured here). The main chunk grows by 48 B raw / 26 B gzip (Node zlib default; 25 B with `gzip -6`), measured on the merged head against `main` 18056db: the `playerFor` call in `lazy.tsx` and the chunk export of session.ts `isCommitted`, no form-guide code.
  **Why**: §6 and the GU-7a kept pieces; checked by `tests/formguide/player-fg4.test.ts` and the gate's FG-4 block.
## Live retarget and autoregulation (LT-3, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-LT3, supervisor rulings on the LT-3 check-in, PR #83)**: how `liveRetarget` (`src/brain/retarget.ts`) applies `docs/LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS.md` §4.
  - **Scope.** The placeholders follow `liveRetarget` on every loaded (`weighted`), non-timed exercise, for a load the user lifted: kinds `keep` and `above`, and the changes to reps alone. The kinds that pick a load the user did not lift (`drop`, `up`, and "Back to …" when nothing clean is left) and every line run only where plan 6.13 allows a prompt: main lifts, not isolation, not timed. On isolation, a would-be `drop` or `up` stays at the lifted load and follows the reps rule. Timed sets get no retarget.
  - **Above the plan.** The reps left at the lifted load are `floor(R + rirObs − rirMid)`. At `lo` or more, the kind is `keep`: "Keep 32 kg for the rest: 8 reps a set." Under `lo`, the placeholders stay unclamped (32 × 3, as §7 says). §4 bullet 1 is edited to match; its `max(lo, …)` applied only inside the window. At 1 or fewer, the only line is "Back to 27.5 for 8.". Above the plan there is never "add load", on the legacy path too ("Keep 32 kg for the rest.").
  - **At the plan's load.** Ideal keeps `min(hi, R)`, max takes `R − 1`, easy adds one up to `hi`. An easy set at or above the planned reps gets a reps line, never a load line: "Keep 72.5 kg and do 11 reps for the next set: reps first, then load." This keeps the existing gate probes ("for the next set") true. A held day (`holdLoad`) keeps the plan's reps on an easy set, and its line stays "Keep 60 kg for the next set." Ideal, and a one-rep miss at max, change the reps with no line. A miss at max by 2 or more steps down from the lifted load (2.5 % after 3 sessions, else 2.5 kg). The step is snapped on the menu, never the same rung, with its reps re-solved at `rirMid`. If nothing is lighter, the kind is `stay`, one rep fewer.
  - **Below the plan.** The same rules apply at the lifted load. An easy set at or above the planned reps steps up from the lifted load, never past the planned rung.
  - **Stored target (D-A4 a).** Set 1's target is kept on the active entry when set 1 commits, once (`setEntryTarget`), and `finishSession` carries it onto the `LoggedExercise`. One normalize rule (`withTarget`, `store.ts`) keeps only a positive finite kg and a positive whole number of reps, for saved sessions and the active entry. `session.ts`, the active entry type and `progression.ts` are outside the card's write scope; the supervisor approved them on PR #83.
  - **Next session (§4 a).** An early branch in `suggestRaw`, after the return week, calls `history.ts` `restateOffPlan`. When the last session's working load differs from its stored target, the target is restated as mode `hold` on the rung nearest the planned load whose re-solved reps fall inside `[lo, hi]` ("27.5 kg for 8 to 9"). The window uses LT-2's rule: one number when the re-solve is within a quarter rep. When no rung qualifies, today's rule stands. A load chosen in each of the last two sessions becomes the base only when its own re-solved reps fall inside the window. Otherwise the plan holds and the reason says so. Older sessions (no target) are unchanged. A restatement up to the plan (the last session was lifted under it) is an increase. It runs only on a day that allows one: not when readiness says `no_increase`, and not with the muscle under `RECOVERY_HOLD_PCT` (60 %). On such a day it falls through to today's rule (bench 50 × 10 against 55 × 8 on an amber day → hold 50 kg). A restatement down (the last session was above the plan) runs on any day (review fix round 1, PR #83).
  - **Verdict.** One line per lift off plan: "Planned 27.5 × 8, did 32 × 5: estimated strength up about 2 %." It is under plan by 1 % or more: "…: below plan, the next target holds." (a comma, not a second colon). Within 1 %: "…: on plan in estimated strength." The plan is read at the goal's `rirMid` (RIR 2 without a goal, as in Escobar's debrief tool), set 1 at its own effort. Records are unchanged.
  - **Cue.** Mode `earn` maps to the `build_reps` reason cue.
  **Why**: the supervisor's rulings on PR #83 (go, 2026-09-29 06:42 UTC), §4 and §7. Checked by `tests/live-retarget.test.ts`, `tests/live-retarget-next.test.ts` and the gate's LT-3 block.

## fg:render and sceneOf (V1-05, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-V1-05, V1-05 builder)**: `npm run fg:render`, `sceneOf`, and D-FG7 (i)'s hold-tremor measurement.
  - **D-FG7 (i) resolved**: the lateral raise's hold tremor moves the loaded hand **1.86 px** at 360 px wide (`tremorPx` in `check/render.ts`, the figure-space range of `hand_r` across the correct figure's hold window, scaled by the render width; printed by every `fg:render` run in `renders/<id>/report.txt`). That is over 1 px, so per D-FG7 (i)'s own fallback it is kept, not left out: the tremor stays a real, visible part of the correct figure's hold (doc §1), unchanged from what `sample.ts`'s existing tremor already drew.
  - **`sceneOf` composes guideView.ts's own primitives, rather than guideView.ts calling `sceneOf`.** `player/scene.ts` and `check/render.ts` are this card's whole write scope; `guideView.ts` and `ExercisePlayer.tsx` are not (the shared-file order table gives `guideView.ts` to V1-04 → V1-06 → V1-09, and `ExercisePlayer.tsx` to V1-06 → V1-09 → V1-19). So `sceneOf` is a pure wrapper over `markupOf`, `momentFrame`, `frameFn` and `cameraOf` — the exact functions `ExercisePlayer.tsx` already calls directly for its own Pictures tiles and zoom-still snapshot (`guideView.ts:539,622-624`) — instead of the reverse. A2's spy test (`tests/formguide/render.test.ts`) spies on those guideView exports and shows `sceneOf` calls them with the same arguments the player's own call sites use, and that the two produce byte-identical markup and an equal frame for the same guide and moment: today, the player and `fg:render` are drawing from the identical two functions, never a second implementation of them. When V1-09 next touches `guideView.ts`, it can route `ExercisePlayer.tsx` through `sceneOf` directly with no behaviour change, since `sceneOf` already computes exactly what those call sites compute. Fallback: none needed, this is additive.
  - **The "12-frame filmstrip" is one composed image per rep, not 12 files.** A contact sheet, matching the CHECK-IN's own "contact sheet" wording; `stripSvg` (`check/render.ts`) composes any number of posed, fully token-resolved figures into one SVG, reused for the filmstrip (12), compare mode (2) and a plain moment (1). Every composite keeps each figure at the full 360/390 px render width (`RenderJob.slots`), never squeezed to fit the whole strip into that width, so a filmstrip frame reads the same as a standalone moment. Without this the first cut squeezed the whole strip (or the compare pair) into 360 px total, an unreadable ~30 px tall image; caught by hand on the first render and fixed before this card's check-in.
  - **"The mistake" and "compare mode" freeze each figure at its own peak** (`momentsOf(g, figure)[2]`, the "top" analogue index 2), not the same raw rep-fraction for both: the correct and mistake tempos differ (the lateral raise mistake is faster, `tempo: {lift:0.8,...}` against `{lift:1,...}`), so the same `u` would not land on the same phase in each. This also matches the reference PNG naming (`mistake-top-light.png`).
  - **Debug markers use theme tokens, not literal colours**: `--guide` (joint pivots), `--target` (grip/contact points) and `--mistake` (the centre-of-mass line), resolved through the render's own `TokenReader` at build time — never a hex literal in `check/render.ts`'s source, which `tests/theme.test.ts`'s whole-of-`src/formguide/**` colour lint enforces with no exemption for debug-only code. The centre-of-mass line is an unweighted mean of the drawn joint pivots (not the de Leva 1996 segment-mass model V1-07's `balance` check will use), enough for a debug picture, not a check.
  - **A6 (the debug marker within 1 px of the solved hand) is pending V1-04.** Nothing in the current rig solves a hand to a machine target yet (`solveFrontArm` exists but no exercise file drives it); `tests/formguide/render.test.ts` records this as a skipped test rather than a fabricated pass, per the card.
  - **The Chromium-level proof of A1 (byte-identical PNGs) is opt-in on `MARC_CHROMIUM`, like `npm run gate` already is**, not part of a bare `npm test`: it launches a real browser and runs the CLI twice end to end (~30 s combined). The SVG-string level (every theme, in-process, no browser) always runs and is what actually decides determinism — Chromium's own rasterization of static, font-free vector input is the well-established assumption `npm run gate`'s screenshots already rest on. Verified once with `MARC_CHROMIUM=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`: two full runs of the lateral raise gave byte-identical PNGs in all 5 themes × 2 widths × 9 pictures (90 files), each run 15-17 s (A4, ≤ 60 s).
  **Why**: the card's write scope (`scripts/fg-render.mjs`, `check/render.ts`, `player/scene.ts`, `tests/formguide/render.test.ts`, one `package.json` line, one `.gitignore` line) and D-FG7; checked by `tests/formguide/render.test.ts` (A1-A3 always run; A1's Chromium half and A4 run under `MARC_CHROMIUM`; A6 pending, skipped).
## Held sets out of recovery calibration and load snapping (BUG-24, 2026-09-29)

## The user's own week (ADAPT-4, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-ADAPT4-1, supervisor approval 2026-09-28)**: COACHING-PLAN §7 P2-C's "weekly review appears on the first open of a new week with ≥ 5 logged days" becomes "…once the week reached the user's planned sessions (at least 2)". The supervisor approved this line change on 2026-09-28 (ADAPT-4 `depends_on`, ADAPTIVE-COACH §5 item 2); the builder made the edit as instructed. The one existing test that asserted the old rule (`tests/weeklyReview.test.ts`, `weekHasEnoughData`) now asserts the new one; its "too little data → no review" half stays (2 sessions with nothing planned → false).
  **Why**: finding C-5: a 3-a-week lifter never reached 5 days, so never saw the review.
- **Decided (D-ADAPT4-2, builder)**: what "planned" means and where the plan is silent.
  - Planned sessions for a week = the schedule minus days off (`plannedThisWeek`), else `Profile.plannedDays`, else nothing set. A full week (volume `under`, the weekly review) = that count, 3 when nothing is set, never below 2 (the card's risk line). The week grade target keeps no floor: a 1-day plan with one session is a strong week for that user.
  - The finding's third fallback, the 8-week median of sessions per week, is **not** used. Card acceptance W7 says nothing set keeps today's behaviour (3), and a median learns from the very weeks it judges: a lifter who drops to one session a week would lower their own bar and never read "under". No new stored data either way.
  - The review covers this week once it reached the planned count, else the week just ended when that one did (so it appears on Monday, as P2-C intends). The week-bound rows (sets vs band, frequency, failure share, rep mix) read that week and say "last week" when it is the week just ended; the rolling rows (e1RM trend, staleness, adherence, weight) keep `today`. Dismissal stays keyed to the current week, so a week is reviewed once.
  - Gap note (E-6): days marked off are not counted, and the note waits for max(7, 2 × usual gap), where the usual gap is the median gap between training days in the last 8 weeks (needs 3 gaps). The title still says the real number of days.
  - Green-readiness note (E-9): the first scheduled day of the week not taken off; Monday with no schedule.
  - Profile shows "not set" when `plannedDays` is unset; − and + still start from 3. The old hint "Pre-fills your weekly schedule" was not true (nothing reads it for the schedule); it now says "Your weekly target when no days are scheduled."
  - Escobar's weekly-review method text (`src/escobar/knowledge/methods.ts`) named the removed 5-day constant; it now states the planned-sessions rule and gives the user's own count. One-line wiring outside the card's write_scope, called out in the PR.
  - Scope extension (supervisor, 2026-09-29): `src/slices/coach/Coach.tsx` now passes the plan to the weekly-review card gate (`weeklyReviewCardLine` → `reviewWeek`), so a 2-planned lifter sees the card and a 6-day schedule needs 6 sessions, matching the review inside it; the card line says "last week" when it covers the week just ended (review note on #69). Checked by `tests/adapt4-own-week.test.ts` (Coach card wiring) and the gate's ADAPT-4 G2 block.
  - Callers outside the card's write_scope still pass no plan and so keep the default 3: Body and Escobar volume reads (`Body.tsx`, `escobar/tools/show.ts`, `read.ts`), and the History / Escobar week grade (`History.tsx`, `show.ts`, `read.ts`, `brief.ts`). The coach notes, the Today week card and the review's own content use the plan. Wiring those callers is a one-argument change each; listed in the PR for the supervisor (ADAPT-5 V3 needs Body and the review to agree).
  **Why**: ADAPTIVE-COACH §3 C-3, C-5, C-8, E-6, E-9, F-1 and card ADAPT-4; checked by `tests/adapt4-own-week.test.ts` (W1-W7) and the gate's ADAPT-4 block (G1).
- **Decided (D-B24, BUG-24 builder)**: the two number paths D-B18 left seeing held sets now leave them out. This replaces D-B18's "Which still see them" points on calibration and snapping; the share card's typed top set is unchanged.
  - Calibration judges held sets as finish sees them: in the history up to and including the session being finished (`judgedPair` in recovery.ts). A typo BUG-18 holds then, in that session or in the exercise's previous one, never moves tauScale. A set later repeated counts from the finish that repeats it on, never backwards: the model learned at each finish stays as it was.
  - The rebuild (`replayRecoveryModel`) learns the same model by judging each step against its own prefix, not the whole history (a whole-history judgement would count a typo that a later session confirmed at the finish where it was still held). Judging the full prefix at every step took 22 s for 600 sessions all rated max (budget 500 ms), so `stepJudge` keeps each exercise's summaries from the previous step and re-judges only sessions that still hold a set (a new session may repeat the load) or whose reference moved (best load or e1RM before them); every other summary cannot change. A session dated before one already judged is placed in day order, as `exerciseHistory` does. The same 600-session worst case takes about 350 ms (main: about 250 ms); the budget test's history is unchanged.
  - Calibration still starts with the cheap check on every set (a set rated max in both sessions, the previous one inside the 7-day window); held-out sets can only rate lower, so no case that passes the full judgement is skipped. Finish's call site in session.ts is unchanged: its default now judges held sets.
  - Load snapping (`loggedLoads`): a load only a held set used is not one the gym is known to have, so it is not kept as loadable; it snaps like any other load. Once the load is repeated it counts.
  - Saved data shape unchanged; the stored tauScale of existing users changes only after a rebuild (history edit or delete) when a held typo had moved it.
  **Why**: the audit's correct behaviour (a held set feeds no number, plan 6.17.4) and BUG-17's rule that finish and a rebuild learn the same model. Checked in `tests/bug24.test.ts` (3 of its tests fail on `main` 5e416d8) through finishSession and the store, plus a random-history test that `stepJudge` equals judging each prefix.
## Recovery rebuild: previous session in day order (BUG-25, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-B25, BUG-25 builder)**: the rebuild (`replayRecoveryModel`) takes an exercise's previous session the way finish does: the last one by day among the sessions started before this one, a tie on the day going to the later start. Before, it kept the last one by start. Finish reads it through `lastSummaryAlone`, which is `exerciseHistory` and so orders by day, and BUG-24's `stepJudge` already took the numbers in day order.
  - How it went wrong: when a history's day order and start order disagree, the rebuild checked "rated max" and the 7-day window on the last-started session but took the numbers from the last-dated one (review note 1 on #77). Example: A dated day 10 and started first, B started after A but dated day 8, C on day 13. Finish of C compares C with A; the rebuild checked B, and when B was not rated max it learned nothing where finish had learned.
  - When the orders disagree: `startedAt` and `day` come from the same training time in the app, so they agree unless the day was saved in another time zone, or a backup or repaired save carries a `day` its start time does not give (`repairState` keeps a valid `day` as it is). Retro sessions logged later do not cause it: the rebuild then reads the history as it now is, by design.
  - Not chosen: moving finish to start order. `exerciseHistory` is day-ordered for every reader (progression, PRs, BUG-18's judgement); only the rebuild's own map differed, so the fix is there. Saved data shape unchanged; a stored tauScale changes only at the next rebuild (history edit or delete), and only for histories whose orders disagree.
  **Why**: BUG-17's rule that finish and a rebuild learn the same model. Checked by `tests/bug25.test.ts`: a store-driven backdated history (finish learns 1.1 for quads, the rebuild on `main` learned nothing) and 30 random histories with out-of-order days, each rebuild equal to finishing every session in start order.

## Rung choice and rep re-solve (LT-2, 2026-09-29)

## Abductors drawn on the back map, not front (BUG-23, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-B23, BUG-23 builder)**: `abductors` (label "Outer hips") declares `view: 'back'` in `src/data/muscles.ts`, matching the SVG that actually draws it — `gluteus-medius-left`/`-right` in `src/svg/bodyMuscles.ts`'s `BACK_PARTS`. It was `'front'`, so the muscle map, form-guide overlays and any future single-side view of a muscle disagreed with what the body SVG shows.
  - Guard against the whole bug class, not just this one id: `tests/muscleView.test.ts` derives, per muscle, the set of SVG sides (`FRONT_PARTS`/`BACK_PARTS`) that actually draw a path for it, and asserts the declared `view` is one of them. Muscles with no path of their own (`brachialis`, `rotator_cuff` — aliased onto a neighbour in `src/ui/MuscleMap.tsx`) are skipped, since they have no side to check against.
  - Checked every consumer of the `view` field first (`MuscleMap.tsx`, `Body.tsx`, `formguide/check/overlays.ts`, `formguide/parts/*`, `escobar/tools/executor.ts`, `brain/exposure.ts`): `MuscleMap.tsx` renders both the front and back SVG unconditionally from `FRONT_PARTS`/`BACK_PARTS` (so the on-screen bug is that the back map already showed "Outer hips" gluteus paths — the wrong metadata just mislabels which side owns the muscle for anything that reads `Muscle.view` itself); `formguide/check/overlays.ts` keeps its own separate front/back muscle lists and already had `abductors` correctly under `back`, so it was unaffected. No other code currently reads `Muscle.view` at runtime; the field is otherwise forward-looking metadata, which is exactly why the wrong value went unnoticed.
  - Added a gate block `BUG-23` (add-only, end of `scripts/screenshot-gate.mjs`) that opens the Body tab and asserts an "Outer hips" path title exists only in the back map's SVG, not the front one.
  **Why**: the SVG is the ground truth for what a view shows; `Muscle.view` must describe it, not contradict it. Checked by `tests/muscleView.test.ts` (fails on `main` before the fix, passes after) and the gate's BUG-23 block.
- **Decided (D-LT2, LT-2 builder)**: how `src/brain/retarget.ts` `chooseRung` applies `docs/LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS.md` §3 and §5b, where the text is silent or the §3 table disagrees with its own rules. The rules win over the table, and rows 5, 6 and 7 of the §3 table are corrected to match (supervisor, PR #73).
  - Integration: `suggestRaw` keeps every rule and its order. It only records, next to an `increase` or a step-down `reduce`, the anchor (the median working set at `workKg`, RIR from its effort, 2 when unrated), `rawKg` (today's `loadStep` with the 10 % cap, or today's `half(topKg − loadStep)`) and the load before the failed increase. `suggestNext` calls `chooseRung` only for a weighted lift with equipment and no lighter week or load factor. Everything else goes through `snapToEquipment` as before (holds, BUG-11 logged loads, carries, deloads, Escobar factors). With no equipment nothing changes, so the pinned 60 × 4 max-twice reduce stays at 57.5. `ProgressionContext.menu` (LT-1's `loadMenu`) is optional. Without it the rungs are `equipment`'s loads, and `menuConfidence` is left off.
  - Candidate rung: today's upward snap of `rawKg`, with `loadableNear(topKg + 0.02, 'up')` for the top-of-ladder check. It matches `up` in every §3 row and keeps the pinned "logged 61 kg → 65 kg" increase (A7). When that rung fails, a smaller real rung between the current load and the ideal step is tried before earn.
  - Row 5 (kettlebell 16/20/24, lean main, 16 × 12): the step 6 formula gives earn = ceil(14.25) = 15, not the table's 16. 15 = hi + 3 is allowed, so the verdict is earn ("Keep 16 kg and work up to 15 reps; then 20 kg for 6 is ready."), which also matches §5b's "double progression to 15, then a variation". The lever wording is tested on a 20.5 kg next bell (earn 16).
  - Row 6 (anchor 12 reps): step 1 shows "about" for any anchor over 10 reps, so the line is "55 lb for about 8."
  - Earn aims at the acceptance floor, so strength earns toward 3 reps, not lo = 1. Otherwise the rung it earns fails acceptance again. Row 7 then gives earn 8 > hi + 1, so lever. Earn is tried before the levers for every goal. For strength, §5b lists add-ons, an extra set and pauses first. Add-ons are already rungs on the menu (`loadableValues` includes `addOns`), and the "do you have add-on plates?" ask is LT-4's chip.
  - Strength lever order (supervisor, PR #73 fix round 1): §3 wins. Earn up to `hi + 1` comes first, then the §5b levers in their order, without earn. §5b's strength column is amended to match, and §3 step 6's formula now reads "`lo`, or 3 for strength".
  - Wording: "No smaller step here" appears only when the rung overshoots the ideal step by more than the step itself (rows 1, 2 and 5 yes; rows 3, 4 and 6 no). A re-solve within a quarter rep above a whole number shows that number alone ("about 8", "for 4"), because the extra fraction is under the formula's error. The window narrows with it, not only the words: row 2 is `[8, 8]` before widening, so `[7, 9]`, not the step 4 formula's `[8, 9]` → `[7, 10]`. RIR 1 as the shown effort adds ", close to max". A lever names "about N reps" only when N ≥ 1. The second lever is "a harder variation" (lean, growth), "pause reps" (strength and muscle), or for strength "move this lift to the barbell" on a ladder (dumbbells, kettlebells) and "pause or tempo reps" otherwise.
  - An anchor over 10 reps widens `repWindow` by one rep each way, inside `[lo, hi]`. The planned sets and the line use the unwidened centre.
  - Suggestion shapes: a rung keeps its mode (`increase` / `reduce`), with `reps` = `repWindow` and the verdict line after the existing reason. Earn is mode `earn`: the same load, `reps` = `[n, n]`, which may sit above `hi`. A lever is mode `hold`: the same load, `hi` reps and one more planned set (at most 6). A step-down with nothing lighter on the menu keeps the load at `lo` reps.
  - Re-solved reps are read to a hundredth, because rungs are stored to the gram and 55 lb re-solves to 7.9993.
  **Why**: §3 and §5b of the spec and the card's A1–A7, checked by `tests/retarget.test.ts` and the LT-2 block of `tests/progression.test.ts` (6 of its 7 tests fail on `main` 5e416d8; the seventh pins a behaviour `main` already has).

## V1-02 research validator (2026-09-29)

- **Decided (V1-02 builder)**: two points D-FG7 (l) leaves open for the research validator (`tests/formguide/research.test.ts`).
  - **Back view's drawable channels**, needed to check the rear delt fly's mistake tells (D-FG7 (d) gives it the back view). D-FG7 (l) publishes only the front and side lists; back is not drawn yet (`figureBack.ts` is a stub until V1-22). The validator treats back as the front list minus `wrist_pron`: horizontal abduction moves the arm in the same coronal plane as front abduction, just seen from behind, while a grip turn is not visible from behind (nothing in the plan suggests otherwise). Fallback: when V1-22 publishes the real back list, the validator's `BACK_CHANNELS` constant is updated to match, and `tests/formguide/research.test.ts` is the one place that needs it.
  - **Where a "contact fact" (curl grip, row handle line, pulldown grip width, bar-over-mid-foot, hanging swing) is recorded.** `Research` (`model.ts`) has no dedicated field for these yet (they feed V1-04's solver `contacts`, not built here). Each is written as a plain descriptive entry in the file's top-level `sources` array, e.g. `"Contact fact (D-FG7 (j)): grip stays supinated..."`, so it is cited but does not need a schema change in this card's write scope. Fallback: if a later card adds a `contacts` field to `Research`, these entries move there and the sources array keeps only citations.
  **Why**: neither point blocks V1-02's write scope (`research/*.json`, `research.test.ts`, `fixtures/research-bad/**`); both follow directly from the published plan and the current schema.
  **Source**: `docs/FORM-GUIDE-PRODUCTION.md` §10.5 V1-02 card; D-FG7 (d), (j), (l); `src/formguide/model.ts` `Research` type.
## Form guide Version 1 plan (V1-00, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-FG7, supervisor-approved plan of the 2026-09-29 planning workflow; recorded by V1-00)**: the decisions the Version 1 cards build on. The plan itself (approach, grafts, card defaults, lanes, merge order, the 25 cards and the owner's device checks) is `docs/FORM-GUIDE-PRODUCTION.md` §10. Each item gives its reason and its fallback.
  - **(a) Approach C, contact solver plus key poses, with the grafts in §10.2.** Why: both judges scored it highest (41 and 41, against 40/38 for templates and 29/30 for hand-keyed); the author writes coach-level contacts ("hands on the handle", "bar over mid-foot") plus a few key poses, and the solve sits in one place, the sampler's evaluator (`sample.ts:176-207`), so the hash, every check and the WAAPI keyframes read the same angles. It also fixes the front seated sway turning the pelvis about the floor (`rig/pose.ts:61`). Fallback: an exercise whose geometry the solver cannot hold (V1-04 A0) is left out of V1 and logged here; no check changes.
  - **(b) Drive rule, revised.** The dominant joint is the driver, as one minimum-jerk curve; one secondary channel is solved to keep the hand or foot on the part's path; the part's travel follows the body, always as a projection onto a one-dimensional path (line, arc or rotation), never a free 2-D point, so `handsOnHandle` still measures a real gap. `handsOnHandle` 0.5 and `machinePivot` 0.05 (`check/index.ts:48-50`) and what they measure are unchanged; the rubric row "moving parts follow the hand or foot" (doc §1) already matches. Why: driving the limb from the part's travel failed smoothness (c) (limit 3×, `check/smooth.ts:8`) in the judges' scratch runs, 3.46-12.7× against 2.08-2.89× with a joint driver, on a generic arm (unverified on our rig; V1-04 A0 re-measures on the real front and side arms and the leg press before any machine card). The doc's drive rule (doc:120) is rewritten by V1-00; the matching comment at `model.ts:56` is changed by V1-04, which owns `model.ts`. Fallback: a geometry that still fails on the real rig leaves that exercise out of V1.
  - **(c) Sway turns about the support.** Standing: about the sole, unchanged, so the lateral raise hash `c1ac61634cd68bd4` stays. Seated front: the trunk turns about the hips. Back-pad supports: head and neck only. Hanging: about the grip. Plus the add-only `swayDrawn` check (V1-11), because side seated poses draw no sway (FG-6 `pose.ts:221`) while `secondaryMotion` reads the channel value (`check/index.ts:387-397`), so a still figure passes today. Existing limits stay. Why: sway about the floor moves a thigh pad or back pad, which `bodyOnPad` (0.01) cannot pass (D-FG3, D-FG5 risks). Fallback: if a support cannot hold its pad within 0.01 (V1-11 A2, A4), that pose's sway is limited to the head and neck and the reason is logged here.
  - **(d) The rear delt fly uses the back view.** Why: `patterns.ts:20` gives horizontal abduction the back view; the back overlay list has `rear_delts` (`overlays.ts:11`) and the front list does not (`overlays.ts:8`). doc:125 and doc:226 are corrected by V1-00. Fallback: after two failed likeness rounds on V1-22, the side view with `viewWhy` (the side list has `rear_delts`, `overlays.ts:9`) and a judge motion-truth mark ≥ 4; otherwise the rear delt fly is left out of V1.
  - **(e) The triceps exercise is `lib_single_arm_triceps_pushdown`.** Why: it is the id in the owner's Version 1 cut (doc:205); doc:243 said `lib_triceps_pushdown` and is corrected. The owner is asked, without blocking work, because `templates.ts:23` uses the two-arm id. Fallback: if the owner picks the two-arm pushdown, V1-15 keys `lib_triceps_pushdown` instead (same station, both arms) and the switch-on list follows.
  - **(f) Leg press and incline press use the seated pose on a reclined seat support; hanging becomes a real pose** (the type already lists it, `model.ts:46`). Why: the fewest new poses; the reclined support is a support option, not a new leg part set. Fallback: if V1-11's sweep or renders show the seated pose cannot recline, V1-11 adds a `reclined` pose and records why here.
  - **(g) Switching on.** Only the supervisor edits `src/formguide/registry.ts` and the exact list in `tests/formguide/player.test.ts` (the "names exactly the N guides" test); an id switches on only after its judge pass (V1-24). `lib_lat_pulldown` is switched off by V1-00: it had no file, so `FormGuidePlayer.tsx:55-56` (`guideFor` returns `stubGuide` for any id) played the chest-press stub for it (the stub is the chest press, `stubGuide.ts:33`). `scripts/screenshot-gate.mjs` has no reference to the id (its three "Lat Pulldown" hits are logged-history fixtures, not the guide). New test "every GUIDE_IDS id has an exercise file or is the stub's id (V1-00 A2)" keeps any id from playing a wrong demo again; it failed on 1afc7ab with `['lib_lat_pulldown']` and passes after. Fallback: none needed; V1-17 re-adds the id after its judge pass.
  - **(h) The GU-7a stub is retired only after V1-19's parity block reproduces every assertion of the GU-7a gate block** (`screenshot-gate.mjs:5322-5611`); the chest-press file (V1-21) merges right after (V1-20 then V1-21). Why: the chest press is already in `GUIDE_IDS` and `playerFor` plays any file in `exercises/`, so its file cannot land earlier without replacing the stub mid-gate. Fallback: a gap in the mapping keeps the stub; V1-20 and V1-21 revert together.
  - **(i) Hold tremor** (doc:10) never reaches the stops, because no stop sits inside a hold (`check/index.ts:125-159`, D-FG2). Decided after V1-05 measures how many pixels it would move at 360 px; it does not block V1. Fallback: if it moves less than 1 px, it stays out and doc §1 says so.
  - **(j) Defaults, owner questions non-blocking:** the curl uses both arms together; the row uses a V-handle; the curl grip follows research. Why: the plan sets them so no card waits on an answer. Fallback: an owner answer that differs changes only that exercise's keys (and the handle part for the row).
  - **(k) Frame-rate protocol:** DC0 after V1-09 (80-path stand-in machine, compare mode, median and p95), DC1 after V1-14 (leg press), DC2 on the release candidate (median ≥ 30 fps, doc:114); steps in §10.6. Why: phone performance is the one thing no agent can measure. Fallback: a DC2 median below 30 fps opens a fallback card (V1-24 A3) and all 15 are re-checked.
  - **(l) Channels each view can draw** are published in doc §3 ("Channels each view can draw"), derived from `frontFrame` (main `rig/pose.ts:51-120`) and `sideFrame` (FG-6 `pose.ts:191-260`). Research tells and mistakes may use only channels their view draws (V1-02's validator); V1-07 adds a perturbation test so the list cannot drift. There is no spine-flexion, scapular-retraction or pelvic-tilt channel (`rig/joints.ts:28-45`). Why: `wrist_pron` is read only by the front frame and appears 0 times in `figureSide.ts`, so a side tell on it would show nothing. Fallback: a needed but undrawn motion is shown another way (the curl's grip turn by crossfading the dumbbell drawing, V1-06) or a new channel is a supervisor decision logged here.
  - **Package script line:** V1-05 may add the one `fg:render` script line to `package.json` (supervisor OK, per AGENTS.md's package.json ownership); no dependency changes.
  - **Reference PNGs.** `docs/design/form-guide-lab/**` was copied onto main from commit 6c5a1a0 (branch `claude/marc-form-guide-smoothness-fiuy2y`, not merged). sha256: `lateral-raise-lab.html` 0b6ecc94adf492be836e038fc5b66b0f52867c32f9bda59de8a42d3a7911bf42; `reference/close-top-dark.png` c6b41663f606fd5a6cee82a71adcca7fe2af61956822d192d9d51db648247db4; `reference/half-light.png` c034dd1c03782b5616fa26207cadf51b60c578240e3c347d03206592da17119f; `reference/mistake-top-light.png` 938824e7319283fe3f394e19e7a3387fbe95fa2c259a2f57a8d9347b7208d735; `reference/rest-dark.png` f3128749038cdb1d261214ed42a9344f7701dca263c4468ef4f86ddbb0f670f8; `reference/rest-light.png` f2ba701fae8046800dd0fb83bff244a769b0f86f376ad1791236dac241704204; `reference/top-dark.png` 085085c0726bf33a451707644961a1be5ebb527c5cd04e82aae3bdedde568840. Likeness check (V1-00, 2026-09-29): the gate's FG-4 screenshots of today's FG-1 figure at 390 px, pose for pose (`silent-black-fg4-guide-t0.png` against `rest-dark.png`, `silent-black-fg4-guide-top.png` against `top-dark.png`; `paper-fg4-guide-t0.png` against `rest-light.png`, and `paper-fg4-guide-top.png` against `half-light.png` for the light palette only, since there is no light top reference) still match the references: same masked head and eyes, cel-shaded accent body with the lab's muscle lines, dark trousers and shoes, hex dumbbells, same proportions and outline in both themes. The side-delt tint follows effort in both: at the top, app and `top-dark.png` show the same warm tan; `half-light.png` shows a blue translucent tint at 46°, lower effort, so the light top frame's paler, greyer tint is not a like-for-like difference (no light top reference exists to settle it; V1-08 probe 2 and the judge check it per exercise). The differences are not likeness: the app draws the figure smaller inside the sheet, and it has no angle arc, tag or dashed guide yet (a §1 item, V1-19). So the PNGs stay the primary likeness reference for the front view; side and back use the owner-approved turnaround sheet. Fallback: if a later FG change makes them drift, the owner-approved sheet becomes the primary likeness reference for every view and this entry is updated.
