## Progression without perfect ratings, and after breaks (ADAPT-3, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-A3, ADAPT-3 builder)**: how targets move when few sets are rated, when reps fall under the range without "max", and after a break (A-1, A-9, E-8, A-4; `src/brain/progression.ts`, `rules.ts` effort-missing rule).
  - **Reps earn a step (A-1).** Under 50 % of recent sets rated, the load no longer holds when the last two sessions at one load had every straight set at or above the range top, at most 1 rep lost from the first straight set to the last, and no max-rated set (`repsEarnIncrease`). That earns one standard step, capped as before; the fast track never applies, since it needs one session and the rep rule needs two. Anything else with few ratings (mid-range reps, reps falling 12/10/8, a max set) still returns "rate your sets" and holds the load, so today's `confirm_effort` test for mid-range reps is unchanged. An unrated session with flat reps at the top also counts as clean when ratings are otherwise present. Readiness, recovery, plateau and the biggest-jump cap run unchanged after the gate.
  - **Step-down without "max" (A-9).** A session counts as under the range when its best straight set is under the range bottom and either a set was rated max (unchanged) or the last straight set is unrated and no straight set was rated easy. Two such sessions in a row step down one step. The rule now runs before the effort gate, so a user who rates 1 of 3 sets gets the lighter target instead of "rate your sets". Every set rated "ideal" under the range is not a signal (the user says there were reps left). Strength ranges starting at 1-2 reps keep the e1RM-at-max rule (out of scope: e1RM is reserved).
  - **The rating nag (E-8).** "Rate your sets" leaves out the sets of lifts whose reps already earn the step, so it asks only where ratings would change the answer. The coach context has no goal yet (ADAPT-5 wires `goal` into `CoachContext`); an optional `goal` field is added for it, and until it is set a lift is left out only when its reps earn the step under every goal's range (so the nag never goes quiet while a target still holds). The "offer 'rate only your last set' after 2 snoozes" part of E-8 is an ASK item and is not built.
  - **Return after a break (A-4).** 14 or more days since the lift's last session (`REENTRY_DAYS` 13, "more than 13") repeats the last load once, no increase (was more than 28). Beyond 8 weeks (`RETURN_CUT_DAYS` 56, "more than 56") the return load is **10 % lighter** (`RETURN_CUT` 0.1), bounded to 70-100 % of the last working load, and with equipment it snaps to the nearest rung below, never back up. The 10 % is a design value: [Bosquet 2013] shows loss grows with break length, [Hwang 2017] shows 2 weeks keeps strength, but no study gives an exact return cut; it sits inside the card's 70-100 % bound. Learning the cut from the user's own past comebacks needs 2 comebacks and is later work. The same 14-day line applies to carries and sleds logged by distance or time (they share `REENTRY_DAYS`); bodyweight rep targets have no break rule and are unchanged.
  - **One existing test changed.** `tests/progression.test.ts` "re-entry after a long gap" pinned a 79-day return at the old 60 kg, the exact harm A-4 names; its expectation is now 54 kg (10 % lighter). No other existing test changed. Escobar's method text reads `REENTRY_DAYS`, so it now says "more than 13 days"; it does not mention the 8-week cut (`src/escobar/knowledge/methods.ts` is outside this card; follow-up).
  **Why**: ACSM 2009 "2-for-2", COACHING-PLAN App. D 2 (novices rate effort poorly), LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS §2, and the studies above; checked in `tests/adapt3.test.ts` (P1-P8, 23 tests; the P1, P4, P6, P7 and P8 cases fail on `main` 9a37b56).
## Readiness judges "normal" against the user (ADAPT-2, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-ADAPT2, ADAPT-2 builder)**: Version 1 of B-1 (step 1), B-3 and B-9.
  - **Check-in centre 0.75, slope z/3 (B-1 step 1).** Each check-in part (soreness, sleep quality, mood) is `clamp(0.75 ± z/3, 0, 1)`, with z the existing z-score against the user's own 30-day mean (n ≥ 3, SD floor 0.5). Below 3 past check-ins the raw rating is re-centred the same way: `0.75 ± (rating − 3)/4`, so a 3 reads normal. Why 0.75: the check-in carries 0.35 of the score and the green line is 67; at 0.5 an at-mean answer (0.35 × 0.5) pulls a day with 90 % recovery to 65 (amber, no increase), and with no load input it can never reach green. At 0.75 the same day reads 81, and 78 at 78 % recovery (ADAPTIVE-COACH B-1). The z/3 slope is kept as it was (the same slope as the HRV part), so one SD better or worse still moves a part by a third; 2 SD worse on two of three parts takes the check-in to about 0.31 and the day to amber or red (R5). The 67/33 bands, the weights, the 0.4 check-in driver line and the D-B16 "reduce needs corroboration" rule are unchanged. Because the centre moved, the 0.4 driver line now sits about 1 SD under the user's usual (before: 0.3 SD under), which is what "a genuinely worse day" means in the card.
  - The 14-day `calibrating` flag keeps its meaning and no longer decides whether an average day can be green (R3: a 2-3 check-ins-a-week user stays calibrating and reads green).
  - **Resting HR (B-3).** With 14+ days of resting HR in the last 28: z = (7-day mean − 28-day mean) / max(SD of the resting HR days before the last 7, 1.5 bpm); score `clamp(1 − z/3, 0, 1)`; driver and "low" at score ≤ 0.5, that is z ≥ 1.5. Under 14 days: the old fixed scale (`1 − delta/10`, driver at +5). Two points differ from the card's wording, and why:
    - The SD is taken from the days before the last 7, not `restingHr28dSd`. The 28-day SD includes the raised week itself, so for a clean step it always gives z = √3 ≈ 1.73 whatever the size of the rise (a 3 bpm and a 20 bpm week read the same), and R6's "+4 bpm with a personal SD of 1" cannot exist (a +4 week alone puts the 28-day SD above 2.3). The card's own "personal SD" is the user's usual day-to-day spread, which is what the days before the week measure.
    - The driver is at z ≥ 1.5, not z ≥ 1. The card's R6 requires "+6 bpm with SD 5 → no driver"; at z ≥ 1 that is 1.2 and fires. 1.5 satisfies both R6 cases, puts the driver line where the other personal parts' z/3 slope puts 0.5, and for a typical day-to-day SD of about 3 bpm lands at about 4.5 bpm, near the old fixed 5.
  - **Missed weeks are not a spike (B-9).** `acuteChronicRatio` keeps its 28-day ratio and, when 3+ of the 8 weeks before this one have training, also reads this week's load against the usual trained week (median of the last 4 non-empty weeks); the smaller of the two counts. Taking the smaller means nothing reads higher than before (a real jump above the usual week still counts: R8, 1.6 → load driver), steady training is unchanged, and the 1.3 threshold and 1.25 caps are unchanged. Fewer than 3 trained weeks: today's ratio. Both callers (whole-body factor, readiness load part) get the fix.
  - No new stored data: everything is read from `checkIns`, `healthDays` and `sessions`. `ReadinessBaselines` gains two computed fields (`restingHr28dCount`, `restingHrBaselineSd`), not stored.
  - Known risks: more green days means more increases; mitigated by the unchanged 67/33 bands, the per-muscle 60 % recovery hold, the goal caps, R5 and R8. A user whose resting HR is noisy (SD 5) now needs a larger rise before the driver shows; the 1.5 bpm floor keeps a very steady watch from flagging a 1-2 bpm drift.
  **Why**: docs/ADAPTIVE-COACH.md B-1, B-3, B-9 and card ADAPT-2; checked by `tests/readiness-adapt2.test.ts` (R1-R8 and the two fallbacks), each behaviour test failing on `main` a566381 and 10 code mutations each caught.
## Privacy policy draft (DOC-1, 2026-09-28)

- **Decided (D-DOC1, DOC-1 builder)**: what docs/PRIVACY-POLICY.md says where the card is silent.
  - Scope: the Android app and the web version, since error reports carry `platform: 'android' | 'web'` and the Worker allows the Netlify origin. Netlify is named only as the web host.
  - Everything real is listed, including what the docs don't mention: the coach's per-device and per-IP daily counters (raw IP or IPv6 /64, deleted after 3 days, `quotaDO.ts`), Anthropic's prompt cache (up to 1 h, `anthropic.ts`), and Android Auto Backup (Capacitor's template sets `allowBackup="true"` and `patch_manifest.py` does not change it). These are open risks for the owner in the PR, not decisions.
  - Error reports are described from the merged code, not the doc alone: "Android version and device model" are named as "not collected yet" (D-C75).
  - The Settings buttons are named as the UI shows them, "Reset workout data" then the confirm "Reset everything" (Settings.tsx), not the doc's "delete everything".
  - Anything only the owner can supply stays a `[OWNER: …]` placeholder: contact email, date, developer name as on Play, public URL of the policy, minimum age, applicable law.
  - Review round 1 (supervisor's rulings, 2026-09-28): (1) the readiness score and muscle recovery are sent whenever the coach is on and can use sleep and resting HR (`brief.ts:79-94`, `recovery.ts:131-140`); the policy says so plainly and no app code changes here. (2) Anthropic's API retention is a public fact, so the policy cites the Anthropic Privacy Center pages (30-day deletion, longer only for usage-policy or legal needs, no training by default) instead of a placeholder. (3) Android Auto Backup stays as is and is described from Android's documentation (private Google Drive folder, about daily when idle and on Wi-Fi, encrypted with the screen lock on Android 9+, turned off in the phone's backup settings); changing it is an open owner question. Added Play's required lines: HTTPS in transit, health data limited use, the developer's name. The coach's reachability check (`session.ts:147`) is disclosed. "Anonymous" is kept only as the switch's own label; the policy says "not linked to your name or account".
  **Why**: the card's risk note (a policy that says less than the app does is a store-review and legal risk); every statement is traced to a file and line in the DOC-1 PR.
## Volume that fits the lifter and one band everywhere (ADAPT-5, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-ADAPT5, ADAPT-5 builder)**: what the card left open for C-2 (seed), A-10, C-4, E-1..E-4 and F-2.
  - **Level seed (C-2).** `trainingLevels` takes an optional `{ trainingSince, today }`; the level is the lifetime score's level raised to the seed: 12-36 months → Developing, 36+ → Established (cap). Months = days since the 1st of the `trainingSince` month / 30.44, as `trainingAgeMonths`. Only `Profile.trainingSince` seeds, never the first logged session (V2: unset means today's level). No decay (out of scope).
  - **Wiring the seed is outside the card's write_scope, and touches the reserved `deload.ts`.** `trainingLevels(sessions, custom)` had no way to see the profile, so the seed can only reach the bands through its callers: `volume.ts` (`muscleVolumeStatus` gains a trailing `trainingSince`), `plan.ts` (`evaluatePlan` ctx gains `trainingSince`, V1), `deload.ts` (one trailing `trainingSince` argument passed to `trainingLevels`; the lighter-week rule itself is unchanged; V9 cannot pass otherwise), and the callers that pass the profile: `rules.ts`, `Body.tsx`, `escobar/tools/{show,read,actions,plan}.ts`, `escobar/knowledge/methods.ts`. Rejected: a module-level "current training age" inside `exposure.ts` (hidden state in the pure brain; no precedent). Each change is one argument; listed in the PR for the reviewer.
  - **One band (C-4).** The review's "sets vs band" row is judged by `muscleVolumeStatus` on the reviewed week (called as of the Monday after it), with the user's plan and seed, so it says what Body says: 'under' only after two full weeks under the band (plan 6.13 "low only after 2 weeks"), and it now also says 'over' when Body does (under wins). Body and Escobar's volume reads now pass the plan too (ADAPT-4 listed this for V3). Two existing tests encoded the old behaviour and were updated, not loosened: `weeklyReview.test.ts` "low chest volume" now has two under-band weeks (was one week, which V3 reverses); `coach.test.ts` "well over its band" uses 20 sets (the shared test profile trains since 2015, so its band is Established 10-18, not New 4-8). ADAPT-4's W3 still holds through the 'over' row (quads 9 sets over the New band).
  - **Plateau lever volume (E-1).** The usual week = median hard sets of the primary muscle over the last 4 completed weeks that had any session (a missed week is skipped). With 3+ such weeks: low when under the muscle's seeded band bottom (clamped 4-20), or, for a goal with `mainLiftWeeklySets` (strength 3-10), when the lift's own median hard sets are under its bottom ("or", because a strength lifter doing 2 sets of the lift is under-dosed on that lift even with the muscle in band). Fewer than 3: the median of what exists against the old 10.
  - **Failure-share lever (E-2).** `fShare > goal.failureShareCap`; the text states the share and the cap ("About 40% … above the 30% your goal allows") because "mostly max" was untrue at 40%. The goal reaches `CoachContext` as `goal?` (`selectors.ts`, `escobar/tools/context.ts`); missing → `DEFAULT_GOAL`.
  - **Effort mix (E-3).** Max line = `failureShareCap`; the easy line stays 0.6 (plan 6.13 "> 60 % easy"; no source gives a goal-specific number), and the easy tip names the goal's `rir`. The debrief passes the previous session of the same split; a flag fires only when that session had the same flag. One flagged session alone shows nothing (no "healthy spread" row, which would be untrue). A direct call without `opts` keeps judging one session (existing unit tests).
  - **Rest tip (E-4).** Threshold = the goal's `restDefaultSec` (lean/growth 90, strength_muscle 120, strength 150; strength was 120). Train and Escobar pass `goal` and, when auto-rest is on, the timer setting; below the threshold the action names it ("Your rest timer is set to 90s; try 120s before heavy sets."). The old boolean is still accepted (true = strength).
  - **Default goal (F-2).** Brief line `goal: default, not chosen` when the goal is `DEFAULT_GOAL` and `profileHistory` has no goal row from `user` or `onboarding` (a `migration` row does not count). Derived from stored data; no new kind of data sent. Known gap: `changeGoal` records nothing when the picked goal equals the current one, so someone who taps the pre-selected "Lean" in onboarding still reads "not chosen" (the line is advisory; Escobar can ask). Fixing it is a `profile.ts` change outside this card. The "Confirm your goal" note is not in the card's acceptance and is not built.
  - Risks: users who overstate training age get higher bands (capped at Established; the over-band note and the plan block still apply). A strength lifter's rest tip now fires below 150 s instead of 120 s.
  **Why**: docs/ADAPTIVE-COACH.md C-2, A-10, C-4, E-1..E-4, F-2 and card ADAPT-5; checked by `tests/adapt5-volume-band.test.ts` (V1-V9 with failure paths), each behaviour test failing on the ADAPT-4 base 642a20c.
## Side-view wiring and cameras (V1-06, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-V1-06, V1-06 builder)**: where card V1-06 (`docs/FORM-GUIDE-PRODUCTION.md` §10.5) is silent.
  - **Camera scale.** "At standingFront's scale" is read as: the figure draws at one size on screen. The stage is 358:276 (`styles.css` `.form-guide .stage`), so a box up to 778 units wide at 600 tall is height-bound; every full camera keeps height 600 and a width ≤ 778, and is centred on x = 200, because the checks read `VIEWBOXES` unreflected (`check/index.ts` mistakeSane, themes) while a mirrored figure is reflected about x = 200. `hangingSide` alone is 666 tall: the bar at 2.3 m (line 489 above) and a dead hang span about 658 units, so that camera draws the figure about 10 % smaller. The zooms keep `upperFront`'s 280 × 300.
  - **"Range ends" (A4)** is the table in `tests/formguide/sideWiring.test.ts` A4: per camera, the rest pose and the ends of the motions its Version 1 exercises draw, one entry at a time, both facings, plus FG-6's stills. Left out, with the reason: standing arms above 90° (no Version 1 standing exercise goes overhead; the overhead press gets a taller camera from this lane when it is built, per the card's risk line); seated knee and hip ends and the seated heel raise on the full camera (FG-6's seated rule puts the mid-foot on the floor, so a straight knee sinks the pelvis and plantar flexion sends the toes through the floor; the seat support is V1-11's and the machine cards'); raw one-channel AAOS ends in general (in the side rig a stance-leg channel pitches the whole body, so they draw poses no exercise has). Stand-ins until their views exist: the hanging pose is the straight-legged figure, arms overhead, grip on the bar (V1-11 re-measures on the real pose); `backFull` is measured on the front figure (V1-22 re-measures on the back figure); `gripFront` holds both hands and dumbbells of the standing front figure; `ankleSide` holds the near shin, calf and foot of the seated figure.
  - **Zoom cameras follow the mirror.** `cameraOf` reflects a camera about x = 200 for a mirrored file (a no-op for the centred full cameras), and the player's snapshot line uses `cameraOf` instead of `VIEWBOXES` so its tiles and zoom still agree. That is one line in `ExercisePlayer.tsx` beyond the card's label line (and its unused import), called out in the PR.
  - **`solveSideLeg`.** The target is the foot attachment point (the sole under the mid-foot) at the pose's ankle angle. Near a straight knee the foot's forward offset gives two bends that are both 0..180; the one nearer the pose's own knee is kept, so a solver driven along a path does not jump. When the support places the pelvis from that leg (the standing near leg, the seated near foot on the floor) it re-solves until the pelvis holds, and throws when the sole cannot reach the target.
  - **The side chain in the rig (after V1-04 merged).** V1-04's add-only `Rig.chain` (the solver's seed for a two-channel contact) is filled for the side view in `sideGuideRig`: a hand by `solveSideArm`, a foot by `solveSideLeg` with the rig's own mirror and lying surface; an unreachable foot gives null so the solver falls back to its grid rather than throwing. The side rig also carries `machine` from `MACHINES`, as the front rig does, so a side machine file's contacts find their parts. Merge rule kept: V1-04's `check/view.ts` (its `worldMat` cache, `frontReach`, `chain`) is taken whole; V1-06 only adds its side, back and `heldOf` lines.
  - **`tests/formguide/checks.test.ts` "a view or pose the rig does not draw yet"** asserted that a side file fails with "no side view figure yet", the exact behaviour A1 removes; it now asserts the same rule on the back view and its V1-22 reason. The one edit outside the card's write scope, called out in the PR.
  - **Hand parts (A6, supervisor go 2026-09-29).** `figureSide`'s `held` option draws the part in the wrist groups at the grip centre, so it turns with the forearm as a held dumbbell does. The dumbbell is FG-5's end-on drawing in both hands (the supinated curl grip; the curl research on `claude/v1-02-research` has no `wrist_pron` range, so there is no crossfade), with no load label, since a label would turn with the forearm and the camera label already carries the load. The barbell is FG-5's end-on bar at the near hand only; its plates follow the file's `equipment.kg`, the load the checks see, not the logged set, whose unit may be pounds. A bar on the back is placed by its caller. `FIGURE_PARTS` stays `['dumbbell']`, so a front barbell file still reads the library drawing.
  - **Review fix B1 (supervisor ruling 2026-09-29):** `snapshot.ts` `resolveVars` read the figure root's own variables only from `class="fg-fig"`, so the side root (`fg-fig fg-side`) left `--rim` unresolved and the Pictures path (and reduced motion) threw. The regex now accepts extra classes on the root; `fg-side` stays. Tests: sideWiring "B1" (both fixtures, both facings, both figures, Silent Black and Paper) and the A2 reduced-motion mount, both red before the fix.
  - **V1-05's `tests/formguide/render.test.ts`** (merged from main) asserted "no side view figure yet" in two tests written "until V1-06". Same move as `checks.test.ts`: the no-rig rule now runs on the back view and its V1-22 reason, and the sheet test asserts the side view has a rig. Outside the card's write scope, called out in the PR.
  - The FG-6 fixtures keep `zoom: 'upperFront'`: the card's camera list has no lying zoom and no check reads the zoom's bounds.
  **Why**: card V1-06 A1-A5 and F1; each is a test in `tests/formguide/sideWiring.test.ts` that failed on ed221a0 and is proven to bite by the mutations listed in the PR.

- **Decided (V1-04, solver core; supervisor rulings on PR #74, 2026-09-29)**: the D-FG7 (b) amendment, the D-FG3 extension and the solver's own choices.
  - **D-FG7 (b) amendment, driver per geometry.** The driver is the joint A0 names for the geometry: `elbow_flex` for the horizontal and the incline press, `shoulder_flex` (side) / `shoulder_abd` (front) for the overhead press and the front pulldown, `knee_flex` for the 45° sled. Measured on the real rig (PR #74 check-in table, `tests/formguide/driveSmooth.test.ts`): travel-driven solving fails (c) everywhere (7.6-29.9×); the named driver gives (c) 2.38-2.92 with a contact gap about 2e-4. The elbow fails when it drives the overhead press (front 3.29×) although its excursion is larger, so "dominant" means the joint A0 names, not the larger excursion.
  - **D-FG7 (b) amendment, soft-lockout envelope.** The front pulldown and the overhead press keep an elbow bend of at least 15° at the straight end and start at 0.45 of the reach or more; outside it the pulldown reaches (c) 3.81×. The test asserts the envelope and its failing corner. The V1-17/V1-18 card text is carried over by the supervisor.
  - **D-FG3 extension (smoothness in the body's frame).** `smoothness` reads a solved file's channels and grip from the same solve with the sway held at 0 (`sampleGuide(..., { still: true })`); `handsOnHandle`, `bodyOnPad`, `machinePivot`, `secondaryMotion` and the hash read the drawn pose, sway included; the sway channel keeps its value, so (d) still holds it. Why: the 0.2° drift crosses every phase edge by design (D-FG3) and a held hand makes the solved arm carry it, which failed (a) by 1.2-2.6 % on the fixture; the drift belongs to `secondaryMotion`. No limit changes. Guard: a solved file with a real edge jerk in its driver still fails (a) in the solved arm and the hand path (`solve.test.ts`).
  - **Solver choices.** One solved channel holds a point on a part's one-dimensional path (a slide's line, a lever's arc); two hold it on a point (a pad, or a travel part's anchor). Each rep is solved over its stops by continuation (seeded from the last three stops, extrapolated; Broyden-updated Jacobian), cached per file, rig, figure and rep; any other u starts from its nearest stop. A contact holds to 1e-8 units and throws above 1e-7, naming the contact, u and the distance. A solved channel stays inside its range (default its AAOS row; `elbow_lead` has none, so a contact on it names one); the first stop must find exactly one root in the range, so a range holding both bends is refused. A solved channel is never keyed; a mistake delta on one needs its point released.
  - **D-FG7 (b) amendment, second forward-kinematics path.** The front rig's `reach(pose, point)` computes a hand without drawing the whole frame: the chest's figure matrix comes from one frame drawn with the sway at 0, kept while no other trunk channel changes, the sway turned onto it about the floor point (pose.ts:61), and the elbow and shoulder turns and the shoulder's rise applied in closed form from `frontFrame`'s own ops (pose.ts:77-79, not edited). Why: the solver moves only arm channels while it holds a hand, and drawing every joint for two points was most of the A7 cost. Held equal: `reach` equals `point(frame(pose))` to 1e-9 over a 500-pose sweep per pose (seated, standing, mirrored file), both arms, every channel moving (measured 2.3e-13), with the sway, breath and arms moving on a kept trunk; mutations: 1e-6° added to the elbow's rotation in `reach` only fails the guard (1.62e-6 units), and the sway pivot moved 1 unit fails it (3.5e-2).
  - **A7 cost.** 3 slowed reps plus the mistake of the two-hand front fixture (812 stops, 2 contacts) solve in **29.4 ms on CI** (15486cc, scale 1.00) against the 50 ms budget and the supervisor's 40 ms target; about 15-19 ms locally. The way there: first build 149 ms locally; CI 88 ms (Broyden, table reads), 54.4 ms (first `reach`), 42.1 ms (closed-form arm, scratch reuse, chest kept per trunk pose), 26.4 ms (the solve's own points reused for followed travel, cubic seed). Re-calibrating the budget was refused. The test is `tests/perf/formguideSolve.test.ts`, because `MARC_PERF=1` runs only `tests/perf/**` (vite.config.ts).
  - **Risk:** the fixture's seated front pose is solved with the sway about the floor (pose.ts:61); V1-11 moves it about the support. The front solve reuses each frame's joint matrices (`check/view.ts` worldMat memo, the same products in the same order).
  **Why**: V1-04 card and the supervisor's rulings on PR #74 (check-in "go", sway option 1, A7 go).
## Ask once and Escobar (LT-4, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-LT4, LT-4 builder)**: how the Train card's one-time ask and the tool JSON apply §2/§9 item 4 where the card is silent, without touching `src/brain/retarget.ts` or `src/brain/progression.ts` (LT-2's, reserved).
  - **Wiring the menu into Train.** `EntryCard`'s `suggestNext` call never passed `ctx.menu`, so `menuConfidence`/`repWindow` were always undefined there (LT-2 only fills them when a caller passes a menu). `EntryCard` now builds LT-1's `loadMenu` itself (`equipMenu`, memoized like `next`) and passes it in — the one behaviour change beyond the chip: increases now see the "learned" rungs union too, same as `read.ts` already could. `src/escobar/tools/read.ts`'s `getNextTarget`/`getEquipment` build the same menu locally (a `menuFor` helper) rather than adding a `menu` field to `context.ts`'s `progressionCtxFor`, since that file is outside the card's write scope.
  - **When the chip fires (LT4-A1).** `chooseRung` doesn't return a first-class "cap broke" flag, and `progression.ts` is reserved, so `shouldAskWeight` (`Train.tsx`, exported for tests) reads it off the `Suggestion` it already has: `menuConfidence === 'assumed'` and either mode `earn`, or mode `hold` whose `reason` contains "is too big a jump" (the lever's cap-break text, `retarget.ts`'s `chooseRung`). The other `hold` lever text, "Nothing heavier here…" (top of the ladder, not a broken cap), is deliberately excluded, and so is every other `hold` (readiness, a lighter week). This is a text-coupling to `retarget.ts`'s wording; a future LT-2 wording change would need `shouldAskWeight` updated alongside it — flagged here rather than adding a field to a reserved file.
  - **Candidates.** `askCandidates` takes the next two rungs already on `equipMenu.rungsKg` above the current load, converted to the menu's display unit — this covers both a ladder (Dumbbells) and a stack (Machine/Cable/plain step), since `loadMenu` already resolves either shape to `rungsKg`. A barbell profile (`plates`/`barKg`) is never asked about; §2's merge rule only covers a stack and a ladder.
  - **The merge rule (LT4-A2).** `mergeAskAnswer` (`src/slices/workout/units.ts`): a ladder profile drops the rungs strictly between the current load and the answer, adds the answer, and keeps the rest; when that would leave fewer than three rungs, nothing is dropped (the "never a two-rung ladder" guard, §2). Any other profile (step, add-ons, or the plain default) becomes `step = answer − current`. Both set `source: 'user'`, which is what flips `loadMenu`'s confidence to `known` next render — there is no separate "stop asking" flag to maintain.
  - **Once per exercise per gym, dismiss and Other (LT4-A1/A3).** A per-run signal (`askDismissed`, a `Set<"gym|exercise">`, same pattern as `checkInDismissed`) is set on both a dismiss and a confirmed answer; nothing is saved on dismiss or an abandoned "Other". An answered pair also becomes `known` through `mergeAskAnswer`, so it would not ask again even across a reload — the per-run signal alone covers the same-session "Other"/dismiss case the owner did not approve a stored `askedAt` field for (§6, D-A4b).
  - **Escobar (LT4-A4).** `get_next_target` adds `repWindow`/`menuConfidence` (only present when LT-2's rung choice ran, i.e. an `increase`/`reduce` with a menu — a `start`/`hold`/etc. suggestion carries neither); `get_equipment` adds `confidence` from the same `loadMenu` call.
  - **Gate probe (§7, fix round 1).** Added, not skipped: `scripts/screenshot-gate.mjs`'s "LT-4 ask chip" block (add-only, appended before `browser.close()`) seeds two sessions of `lib_dumbbell_lateral_raise` at 4 kg × 15 (the fixture already proven in `tests/escobar/read.test.ts` to read `assumed` with a cap-breaking jump), starts a session, and checks the chip shows via `[data-testid="ask-weight-chip"]`, that "Not now" hides it, and that it stays hidden after navigating away (Today) and back to Live in the same run. The original call to skip it was wrong: the file already carries dozens of per-task-ID add-only blocks (BUG-8, BUG-9, BUG-12, BUG-18, UI-1, FG-5, …) and the card names no such qualifier.
  - **Fix round 1 (review r1, both blocking).**
    1. An "Other" answer at or below the current load is now rejected in two places: `mergeAskAnswer` itself returns the profile unchanged when `!(answer > current)` (so a caller that skips the UI guard can't corrupt a stack's `step` towards `0.01`, the failure the review reproduced — `mergeAskAnswer(stack, 40, 35)` used to give `step: 0.01`), and `AskWeightChip`'s Save button is `disabled` for the same condition, with a one-line reason shown. `Train.tsx`'s `askAnswer` also short-circuits before calling `saveProfile`, so a bad value never reaches storage from any of the three layers.
    2. `shouldAskWeight` now takes the profile too and returns `false` whenever it has neither a `ladder` nor a `step` (a barbell/Smith-machine profile, `plates`/`barKg` only) — turning "the default plate set makes this unreachable in practice" into an actual invariant, per the review's note.
  **Why**: keeps every reserved file (`models.ts`, `store.ts`, `retarget.ts`, `progression.ts`) untouched per the card's write scope, while still proving each acceptance criterion with a test that fails before and passes after; fix round 1 items from the supervisor's review of PR #82.
## LT-5 split from the verdict line (D-LT5, supervisor, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided**: §9 item 5 ("LT-5 Verdict and substitution estimate") originally paired the plan-vs-done verdict line (`post.ts`) with the substitution carry-over estimate (`substitute.ts`). The verdict line needs the planned target LT-3 stores (`LoggedExercise.target`, D-A4, acceptance LT3-A5) and so moves to LT-3's card (§9 item 3); LT-5 (§9 item 5) is the substitution estimate only, unchanged from what the LT-5 PR (#76) already built.
  **Why**: LT-3 was already building `LoggedExercise.target` when review found the verdict line unbuilt in the LT-5 PR; splitting the card to match what each branch actually owns avoids one PR blocking on the other's dependency.

## Privacy policy live and Play forms (DOC-2, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-DOC2, supervisor decision, owner facts 2026-09-29)**: M/ARC's minimum age is **18+**, not a lower age with parental-consent handling.
  **Why**: (1) Anthropic's Usage Policy defines a minor as under 18 and requires extra safeguards for products serving them, and this app has no such safeguards built; (2) the app handles health data (heart rate, resting heart rate via Health Connect) and gives strength-training advice through Escobar, both inappropriate to hand to a minor unsupervised. Setting 18+ avoids both problems without building anything new, versus supporting a lower age with parental consent, which this app has no mechanism for.
  **Source**: Anthropic's Usage Policy (minors defined as under 18); `docs/PRIVACY-POLICY.md` "Online coach" and "On-device data" (health data, coaching advice); owner facts for DOC-2, 2026-09-29.

- **Decided**: applicable law is stated as Australia's Privacy Act 1988 (Cth) plus, for Philippine users, the Philippines' Data Privacy Act of 2012 (Republic Act No. 10173) — both names and years confirmed from their official sources (`legislation.gov.au` for the Privacy Act 1988; `officialgazette.gov.ph`/`privacy.gov.ph` for RA 10173, signed 15 August 2012), not assumed from training.
  **Why**: the owner lives in Australia and the app is available in all countries, especially the Philippines and Australia (owner facts); both acts are the correct, current official names as verified by web search, not similarly-named older or draft bills.
  **Source**: WebSearch results against `legislation.gov.au`, `officialgazette.gov.ph` and `privacy.gov.ph`, 2026-09-29.

- **Decided**: `docs/PRIVACY-POLICY.md`'s "Last updated" date is set to the DOC-2 build date (2026-09-29), not left as a placeholder, since the owner facts gave a real developer name/contact/URL to fill in now rather than at merge time.
  **Why**: the card asked to set it "to the merge date"; the exact merge date isn't knowable at build time, so the build date is the closest verifiable stand-in. If the PR merges on a different date, the supervisor or owner should bump this one line before or as part of merging — no other content in the policy depends on the date.
  **Source**: DOC-2 task card.

- **Decided**: the GitHub Pages page (`.github/workflows/pages.yml`, `scripts/build-privacy-page.mjs`) renders `docs/PRIVACY-POLICY.md` to HTML **at deploy time**, rather than committing a static `site/privacy/index.html` that a test checks for drift.
  **Why**: a render-at-build-time page can't go stale — there's nothing to fall out of sync, so no drift-detection test is needed at all, and one less generated file needs reviewing in every future privacy-policy PR. The renderer itself (`renderPrivacyPolicy`) is still unit-tested (`tests/privacy-page.test.ts`) against escaping, bold/link handling, lists and "no leftover `[OWNER:`" regressions. `site/` is gitignored; the workflow generates it fresh on every push to `main` that touches the policy or the renderer.
  **Source**: DOC-2 task card (offered both options); `docs/AGENT-RULES.md`-style "no speculative abstraction" reasoning (a static copy would be a second source of truth with no reader that needs it).

  **Superseded (DOC-3, 2026-09-30)**: `pages.yml`, `scripts/build-privacy-page.mjs` (with its `.d.mts`) and `tests/privacy-page.test.ts` are removed. The repo already deploys the M/ARC website to GitHub Pages from `.github/workflows/website.yml`, and a repo has one Pages site: a second deployment would replace the whole website with this one page. The policy is now rendered from the same `docs/PRIVACY-POLICY.md` into the website's own /privacy/ page at every site build, and the site gate checks its required sections (DOC-3).

- **Decided**: the Data Safety form's "Anthropic receives the request" case is drafted as **collected and transmitted for app functionality**, not flagged as data "sold" or used for advertising, and Anthropic's role is documented as a data processor fulfilling the user's own coach request.
  **Why**: `docs/PRIVACY-POLICY.md`'s existing, reviewed wording (D-DOC1) already establishes this framing — no training by default, 30-day deletion, server-side requests so Anthropic never sees the phone's IP. `docs/PLAY-SUBMISSION.md` carries the same framing forward with file:line citations rather than re-deciding it.
  **Source**: `docs/COACHING-DECISIONS.md` D-DOC1 (review round 1, Anthropic retention); `docs/PRIVACY-POLICY.md` "The online coach".

- **Decided**: the content rating questionnaire's user-generated-content question is answered "no shared/public user-generated content" (marked unknown/conservative in the doc), rather than asserting a specific IARC answer key.
  **Why**: nothing in the codebase names an IARC question ID or exact wording the current questionnaire uses (Google can reword it), and coach messages/exercise notes are never shown to other users — verified by grep, no multiplayer/social/sharing feature exists in `src/`. Guessing a specific rating board's checkbox label would risk misdeclaring; the doc says what the code does and flags the mapping as the owner's to confirm against the live form.
  **Source**: grep across `src/` for user-to-user sharing (none found); DOC-2 card's "say unknown where the code can't answer, never guess" instruction.
## Carry-over start wired into the no-history target (LT-6, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided (D-LT6, LT-6 builder)**: points the card and §5 leave open, wiring LT-5's unused `carryOverStart` into `suggestRaw`'s no-history branch (`src/brain/progression.ts:341`).
  - The "replaced lift" comes from `ActiveSession.entries[].plannedId` (already saved by `substituteEntry`, `session.ts`), never a new saved field: `plannedId` is the slot's exerciseId before the swap, so a value that differs from the entry's own `exerciseId` means a substitution happened and names what it replaced. No `models.ts`/`store.ts` change (matches the card's reserved-paths stop condition, which does not fire).
  - The replaced lift's "strength estimate" is its last session's `workKg` (the canonical top load already used everywhere else in `progression.ts`, e.g. the increase/step-down `SEEDS`), not a separate e1RM computation — the doc says "e1RM or top kg" and the file has no other estimate to reuse.
  - Gated on `mode === 'weighted'`: `carryOverStart` already returns `null` for a pattern mismatch or no ratio, but a weighted-only gate keeps duration/bodyweight/conditioning substitutes on their existing branches untouched, matching A5 (no behaviour change outside the new case).
  - `carryOverStart`'s `menu` parameter is narrowed from `LoadMenu` to `Pick<LoadMenu, 'profile' | 'rungsKg'>` (`substitute.ts`), so it accepts either a real `LoadMenu` or the bare `{ profile, rungsKg }` the no-history branch builds via `menuFromProfile(ctx.equipment)` when no menu is passed. Written before LT-4 (#82) merged into `main`; after the LT-4/LT-3 merge (2026-09-29, this PR) `Train.tsx`'s `EntryCard` always passes `ctx.menu` (`equipMenu`, LT-1's `loadMenu()`), so the carry-over path now normally uses the real per-exercise, per-gym menu with learned rungs — the `menuFromProfile` fallback still guards any other caller of `suggestNext` that only passes `ctx.equipment`.
  - Wired only into the live entry card (`Train.tsx` `EntryCard`, the target shown during an active session): the split preview and the pre-session brief build their target from a `Split`/`plannedExercises`, before any session (and so any substitution) exists, so they have no `plannedId` to read.
  - No new `scripts/screenshot-gate.mjs` block: the card allows one "if the text shows in Train", and none of LT-1 through LT-5 added a gate block for their own new suggestion text (rung choice, earn/lever wording) either — the existing `See substitutes` flow already exercises substitution end to end, and this is coaching copy, not a rendering change, so it is left to `tests/substitute-carry-over.test.ts`.
  **Why**: the card is silent on all of the above; each follows `docs/LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS.md` §5, the existing `progression.ts`/`substitute.ts` code, or the LT-4/LT-5 merge state. Checked by `tests/substitute-carry-over.test.ts` (fails on `main` before this change, passes after) plus the untouched `tests/substitute.test.ts` and `tests/progression.test.ts`.
## GU-7a Play measured under 44 px, a probe-timing race not a layout bug (D-B26, BUG-26 builder, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided**: the `GU-7a` gate failure ("Play is 44.0 x 44.0 px, under 44" at 360 px) is `fitProbe` (scripts/screenshot-gate.mjs) measuring `getBoundingClientRect()` while the sheet's entrance transform (`sheet-in`, `--dur-sheet`) is still in flight, not a CSS rule that lets Play shrink. `openGuide` only waits for the player to become **visible**, which the sheet already is at frame 1 (its `sheet-in` keyframe animates `transform: translateY` only; opacity is 1 throughout in every non-reduced-motion theme), so `fitProbe` can run on the very next frame, while `.sheet-panel`'s translateY is only a few percent progressed. Reproduced directly: sampling Play's rect every animation frame from click shows `height: 43.99993896484375` (displays as "44.0" under `toFixed(1)`, the exact symptom) at ~5% into the transform. Running the real click → wait-for-visible → measure path under 6x CPU throttle (Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate, closer to a loaded CI runner) reproduced it directly too: **4 / 50** runs failed with the identical `h: 43.99993896484375` fingerprint. No layout rule (`.btn-icon { width: 44px; min-height: 44px }`, `.form-guide .controls { height: 44px }`, `flex: none` on Play) was at fault — width was invariant at 44 in every run and every ancestor's `transform` outside of the animating `.sheet`/`.sheet-panel` was `none`.
  - **Fix**: `fitProbe` now calls the gate's own `settle(page)` (races running short animations to completion, capped at 1000 ms) and awaits `document.fonts.ready` before it reads any rect. This is the GU-7a block's probe-timing change the task allows; no assertion, bar or tolerance changed, and the 44 px floor is untouched.
  - **Evidence**: before the fix, 4/50 under 6x throttle (`h: 43.99993896484375` each time); after the fix, 0/60 under the same 6x throttle, same viewport, same DSF. The unthrottled gate path (DSF 1/2/3, 30-60 runs each) never reproduced it locally even before the fix — CI's failure needed enough load to let the measurement land in the ~5%-progress window, which is why "flake" looked plausible but the mechanism is deterministic and load-dependent, not random.
  **Why**: task BUG-26 — root-cause CI run 36550949553 (job `visual-gate-tz`, GU-7a A7 360 px); no test, guard or the 44 px bar was loosened.

## Status bar gets its own fixed backdrop, painted at the source (D-B27, BUG-27 builder, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided**: added one new element, `<div class="status-bar-backdrop" />` (`src/app/App.tsx`, first child of `.app`), styled by a single new rule (`src/ui/styles.css`, marked BUG-27): `position: fixed; top/left/right: 0; height: var(--safe-area-inset-top, env(safe-area-inset-top, 0px)); background: var(--bg);`. This is the "fix at the source": one element covers the status-bar strip for every tab, sheet and scroll position, instead of patching each page's own top padding (the app's existing `--safe-area-inset-top` handling, `.app`'s padding-top and `.live-top`'s sticky offset, only reserves space at the very top of the document — it does nothing once the user scrolls, because that reserved space scrolls away with everything else and page content is free to move up under the real (physical) status bar).
  - **Solid, not blurred**: the inset strip is short (~24-32dp) — a blur radius large enough to fully hide text at that height would need to be disproportionately large next to `.nav`/`.live-top`'s 14px blur, and a solid `var(--bg)` fill is simplest and unconditionally hides anything behind it in every theme, since every theme defines `--bg` (`src/theme/themes.ts`).
  - **No layout shift**: `position: fixed` takes it out of flow entirely; it adds no padding/margin to `.app` or any page, so nothing before it changes size.
  - **No native/manifest change needed**: the repo has no `@capacitor/status-bar` plugin and no `StatusBar` config in `capacitor.config.json` — Capacitor's default WebView already exposes the real inset through `env(safe-area-inset-top)` (see `--safe-area-inset-top` used throughout `styles.css`), so this is a pure CSS/component fix; `scripts/prepare-android.sh` and `native/patch_manifest.py` are untouched.
  - **z-index 35**: above `--z-toast` (30, the highest existing token) so the backdrop always wins at the very top of the screen; `pointer-events: none` so it can never intercept a tap (nothing interactive lives in the inset strip anyway).
  - **Gate probe (BUG-27, add-only, before `browser.close()`)**: seeds the existing `legacy` fixture, opens the Escobar tab (where the bug was seen, "Escobar's notes"), sets a simulated 32px `--safe-area-inset-top` (env() itself can't be faked in headless Chromium, so this mirrors the existing `--safe-area-inset-bottom` fake used by the F12/UI-19 blocks), scrolls the page, then checks the CSS painting-order guarantee directly at the mid-inset point: the backdrop covers it, is `position: fixed`, is painted the theme's exact `--bg`, and no other positioned, z-indexed element covering the same point has a z-index at or above the backdrop's — in Silent Black and Paper. Not `elementFromPoint`: the backdrop is deliberately `pointer-events: none` (so it can never steal a tap), which also makes hit-testing skip straight through it to whatever's behind — the first draft of this probe used `elementFromPoint` and always found the scrolled page content instead, which would have false-failed even with the fix in place (caught by the mutation test below).
  **Why**: task BUG-27; root-caused via `grep` for `safe-area-inset-top`/`StatusBar` (no JS-side inset handling existed at all) and confirmed no Capacitor StatusBar plugin is installed, so nothing native needed changing.
  - **Full-screen elements below the top layer**: checked whether the backdrop (z-index 35) would wrongly paint over the top of something that isn't a `<dialog>`. The launch overlay (`#launch`, `index.html`) uses `z-index: 2147483000`, so it always wins over the backdrop while present, and removes itself once the app boots — no conflict. `OnboardingSheet` (the welcome/profile-setup flow) renders through `Sheet`, which is a native `<dialog>` via `showModal()` — browser top-layer, always above any `z-index` on ordinary content, backdrop included — no conflict. The one non-dialog full-screen fallback, `ErrorBoundary`'s crash card, renders its own `<div class="app">` without this backdrop (it isn't inside `App()`'s JSX) — but its content is one short, fixed-height card that never grows past the viewport, so it never scrolls and the strip is never visible over it; left as is rather than duplicating the backdrop into a screen that can't exhibit the bug.

## Empty finish behaves like a discard, not a save (D-B28, BUG-28 builder, 2026-09-29)

- **Decided**: finishing with 0 logged sets (`finishSession`, `src/slices/workout/session.ts`) now calls the existing `discardSession()` and returns `null`, instead of building and returning a `FinishSummary` for a session it never adds to `sessions`. This was the actual bug: `sessions: exercises.length ? […] : s.sessions` already skipped the save, but `escobar.todayOverride` was cleared unconditionally, and `Train.tsx` showed the "Session saved" screen off the returned (but unsaved) `session` object regardless of `exercises.length`, so the user saw "0 exercises · 0 sets" for a session that was in fact discarded from history.
  - **Why reuse `discardSession` instead of a parallel branch**: an empty finish and a discard are the same outcome (`active: null`, history untouched, `todayOverride` kept, heart capture torn down via `discardHeartCapture`) — `discardSession` already does exactly this and is already covered by its own tests, so reusing it needs no new state-shape code to review.
  - **UI**: `Train.tsx`'s finish handler shows a toast ("Nothing logged, so nothing was saved") when `finishSession` returns `null`, then falls through to the normal Train screen (no active session) — the smallest change, reusing the app's existing `showToast` pattern rather than a new sheet/screen.
  **Why**: task BUG-28. No change to a normal finish (`exercises.length > 0` still takes the original path); `sessions`, `recoveryModel` and `todayOverride` are untouched on an empty finish.

## Push hook, deny rules and resume settings (D-WF2, WF-2 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Decided**: `.claude/settings.json` gains the resume env vars (change 4), seven `permissions.deny` rules (changes 5 and 7) and a `PreToolUse` hook on `Bash` that runs `.claude/hooks/guard-before-push.sh`. The two owner-rules hooks are unchanged. `.claude/owner-rules.md` rule 1 gains "(Opus 5.5 / Sonnet 5; never Haiku or Fable)". Where the plan was silent:
  - **The watch-file rules are anchored with `/`** (`Edit(/native/wear/**)` and so on). The docs say `/path` is relative to the settings file's project, while a bare `path` is relative to the session's current directory.
  - **The fetch uses `+main:refs/remotes/origin/main`**, like git's default tracking refspec. Without the `+`, a changed `origin/main` is not updated, and the merge-base check reads the old ref. A test on the scratch copy caught this.
  - **`git push --all` is also refused**, because it pushes a local `main`.
  - **No `if` filter on the hook.** The script does the matching itself, so compound commands (`cd x && git push`) and `git -C dir push` are covered. A non-push command costs about 10 ms.
  - **Each push is checked in its own directory** (review of 01f46b2). The directory comes from the hook input's `cwd`, then any `cd X` or `git -C X`, so pushes from a git worktree are checked against that worktree's branch and files. The guard runs only for a real `git push` segment; text that only mentions "git push" does not trigger it. If python3 is missing, or the command can't be parsed, the agent gets a note (exit 0) rather than a silent pass.
  - **The "check skipped" note goes to the agent as `additionalContext`.** Stderr on exit 0 reaches only the debug log (hooks docs).
  - **`askUserQuestionTimeout` and `CLAUDE_AFK_TIMEOUT_MS` are not set** (owner decision: the question timeout is dropped).
  **Why**: every item was checked against code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, /permissions, /settings-reference and /env-vars, and each was tested on a scratch copy (PR body).

## D-HT3: golden procedure, the L3 rule, the capture conditions, tripwires and gate time (HT-3 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Decided** (supervisor, plan 2.8, 2.9 and section 3): a plate changes only through the golden update procedure (plan 2.8, with the commit-range guard). The L3 pass rule is fixed at no channel off by more than 1/255 and at most 0.02 % of pixels off by exactly 1. The performance tripwires are those of plan 2.9, including shimmer ≤ 1.2 × golden B. Golden B is pinned only after the S-2 entry conditions hold. The font-scale pass rule is O9's.
- **Capture conditions found by HT-3** (recorded on PR #106, 2026-09-30; the threshold is unchanged):
  - **What happened**: the first app-vs-gallery capture differed in 19,283 px (max 115/255), while L2b found every computed property of all 231 elements equal.
  - **Cause 1: LCD text.** The gallery card paints in the page's root layer, where headless Chromium uses LCD sub-pixel text. The app's `Sheet` is a modal `<dialog>` in its own composited layer (`FixedPosition`), which gets grayscale text. A phone never uses LCD text. **Condition:** gate block HT-3 runs in its own Chromium with `--disable-lcd-text`, for both pages alike.
  - **Cause 2: raster tile phase.** Inside a composited layer, path anti-aliasing depends on where the content sits against the layer's 128 CSS px (256 device px) raster tiles. The golden alone shows it: in a dialog padded 0, 1, 50, 89, 128 and 200 px, it differs from pad 0 by 0 / 701 / 1,327 / 712 / 0 / 1,013 px. **Condition:** see *Capture mode* below. The two controls keep the parts in the dialog's layer, where the phase shows.
  - **Cause 4: which element gets a layer depends on the Chromium build** (CI red on 869d89f, PR #106). Playwright 1.63's `chromium_headless_shell-1243` in CI composites every `overflow:auto` scroller, so the app's sheet panel (layer dump: `388x612 OverflowScrolling`), and the plate rasterises from the panel's padding box (1, 232), not the dialog (0, 231); the first presentation, a plain dialog, was then 1 px off in both directions and failed every pair (about 1,500 px, max 73-93/255). Build 1194 composites only the dialog. Reproduced locally on 1194 by making the panel overflow by 1 px: the plain dialog then differs by 1,257 px; the dialog-plus-scroll-box mirror above gives 0 px. Reproduced locally on 1194 by making the panel overflow by 1 px. A mirror of the app's panel (dialog, scroll box, sticky header) was tried on a10cf0c and replaced: each Chromium build decided its layers differently again (transparent mirror: 1/255 rounding; opaque mirror: no layer of its own), which the layer-kind check caught every time.
  - **Capture mode** (the supervisor's fallback, PR #106, 2026-09-30; replaces the mirror): for pixel captures only, every part of the golden block (plate-fit, cue line, controls, tells, tempo) gets its own compositing layer (`will-change: transform`, inline, removed after the capture) in both pages. Each part then rasterises from its own box, whatever layer Chromium gives the sheet or the dialog around it, so both pages rasterise alike in every build. The golden card is moved, untouched, into a modal `<dialog>` (it inherits `#sheets`' colour and font) placed so its plate-fit is at exactly the app's viewport position, sub-pixel included, measured on every capture. Each capture reads back from Chromium's layer tree (CDP `LayerTree`, read after two painted frames until two readings agree) that every visible part owns its layer on both pages; otherwise it fails. The golden HTML bytes never change; L2b, F3 and the L4 animation lists read the golden card in place. Measured on 1194: the full matrix is 0 px, and the forced-scroller case (CI's layer layout) is 0 px on 2 plates × 2 themes.
  - **Authority** (supervisor, PR #106, 2026-09-30): CI's full Chromium 1243 is the authority; local 1194 is for development. There is no Playwright bump.
  - **Cause 5: the headless shell.** Playwright's default `chromium.launch()` is the headless shell, which CI ran. On the local shell 1194, the squat's sheet in mistake mode with the chest tell lays out 22 px taller than in full Chromium 1194 (the sheet then scrolls at 844 px), and even at the taller viewport the two pages differ by 6,732 px (max 215/255) around the tell's highlight, while full Chromium 1194 gives 0 px at 844, 1100, 1200 and 1300 px for the same state. **Condition:** gate block HT-3 launches full Chromium: `MARC_CHROMIUM` when it is set (local: `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`, build 1194), else Playwright's `channel: 'chromium'` (CI: the full build that `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium` already installs next to the shell). The gate prints the version it ran. The other gate blocks keep their browser.
    - Two controls run in every gate: phase p against p + 128 must be 0 px (the period), and p against p + 1 must fail the rule (the phase matters, so the check is not blind).
  - **Cause 3: animation layers.** A running or paused opacity animation gets its own compositor layer. Its bounds depend on the page around the plate, which moved anti-aliasing by up to 6/255 on the pull-up's leaders in Trace frames. **Condition:** a Trace frame at time t is frozen in both pages the same way: every Trace animation is set to t, its value is written into the element (`commitStyles`), and the animation is cancelled. The L4 animation lists (target, keyframes, timing) are compared before freezing, and the natural end of a Trace is checked in Silent Black and Paper: once the Trace animations report `finished` (their own clock, so a loaded runner cannot fake a failure), `.tracing` and aria-pressed must be cleared within 1 s. A fixed 3.7 s wall clock from the tap failed on a loaded local run (pull-up) and was replaced.
  - **Supervisor conditions** (PR #106, 2026-09-30): the position is measured from the app's plate-fit on every capture, never hard-coded; the width asserts run on the golden-in-dialog before any pixel compare; every L3 and L4 pixel capture uses the same presentation (no per-case exceptions); both controls run in Silent Black and Paper, and HT-1's golden self-check in its original root layer stays at 0 px. Phones render text with greyscale anti-aliasing, so the dialog capture with LCD text off is the closer match to the device; the owner's M1a device check still covers real rendering.
  - With these conditions, all measured pairs are 0 px off (see PR #106 for the builds and runs).
  - **Taller viewport** (supervisor, PR #106): when the app's sheet would scroll, or the region does not fit 844 px, both pages are captured at the same width × 1300 CSS px (under the Train page's own 1,363 px, so the app page still scrolls and its dialog keeps its own layer like the golden's; at 1400 the app page stops scrolling and headless shell 1194 paints the sheet into the root layer, which the layer-kind check caught). A scrolling sheet sits at the fractional 92dvh line, where HTML text snaps up to half a pixel differently in the two pages (measured: 5,909 px on the squat's chest tell in headless shell 1194). Each such capture is listed in the gate's HT-3 line. The clip is never cut.
- **Gate time** (HT3-A9): recorded on the PR with the measured seconds, and the proposed budget is set there.
- **HT-10 amendment: the final A3 numbers** (D-HT10-A3m, supervisor ruling on #166, 2026-10-03). Measured with everything mounted (the squat's sheet after one full HT-10 script run), at 4x CPU throttle, 5 opens per median.
  - **Tap-to-plate limit: 317 ms** = ceil(1.25 × the highest median), capped at plan 2.9's 400 ms (`ht10TapLimit` in `tools/plates/fidelity/ht10.mjs`, which lists every median below). It catches a real regression on either machine without flaking on the slower one. A probe in the gate checks that the verdict trips with the limit 1 ms under the measured median.
  - **Medians, CI** (Chrome 153.0.8010.12): 154 ms (`source-gate`, ca313c7), 147 ms (`visual-gate-tz`, ca313c7), 154 ms (`source-gate`, 3139d28), 140 ms (`visual-gate-tz`, 3139d28).
  - **Medians, agent containers** (Chromium 141.0.7390.37): reviewer 251 (full gate), 241, 236, 253 ms (ca313c7); builder 171, 168, 160 ms (3139d28). Highest: 253 ms → limit 317 ms.
  - **Long tasks during the 5 opens:** limit 100 ms (unchanged). CI: none. Containers: none over 100 (largest 68, 85, 88, 76 ms in the reviewer's runs; none in the builder's). Only tasks that start inside the window count (D-HT10-8). The synthetic 150 ms control was caught every time.
  - **S0 element count:** limit 700. Measured 569 everywhere (CI and both containers).
  - **Shimmer ratio** (app / golden B, TaskDuration): limit 1.2 (D-HT3, unchanged). Chrome 153: CI 0.60, 0.62, 0.86, 0.88; reviewer 0.50. Chromium 141: reviewer 0.85, 0.94, 0.90, 0.95; builder 0.60, 0.73, 0.58, 0.84, 0.85, 0.67.
  - **Early How-to requests** before Train is idle: limit 0. Measured 0 everywhere.
  **Why**: card HT-3 (HT3-A7, HT3-A9) and the plan's rule that noise above the threshold is investigated, never absorbed.

## D-HT3-sections: the sections below the plate during capture (HT-3 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Decided** (supervisor ruling on PR #106, after HT-6 found it): once any section registers below the tempo (HT-6 on), the sheet is taller than the 1300 px viewport, so gate block HT-3's tall fallback reported "the sheet still scrolls or the region does not fit" for every plate and state.
  - **The change:** around each check (L2b, F3 and the capture), `hideSections()` in `tools/plates/fidelity/harness.mjs` sets `display: none` inline on every `dialog.sheet.ht [data-section]` and restores the old inline value after. `display: none` keeps the DOM and every stylesheet, so section CSS that leaks into the golden block (plain, `:has()` or sibling selectors) still reaches it; only the sections' layout boxes go. The captured region is plate top to tempo bottom, so no captured pixel changes.
  - **Kept strict:** "the sheet still scrolls / the region does not fit" stays an error, and the L3 rule is unchanged.
  - **Guards in gate block HT-3** (`ht3SectionGuards`), each with a mutation that fails it:
    1. After each check, the same number of sections must be displayed as before. The run with a 900 px fixture section registered passes (sections hidden, the sheet fits). Mutation "restore skipped": "1 sections were displayed before the capture, 0 after (not restored)". Without the hiding, the same run gives the HT-6 failure ("the sheet still scrolls or the region does not fit at 390x1300").
    2. A fixture section carrying `.ht p { word-spacing: 1px }` must still fail L2b while the sections are hidden. Mutation "hiding also disables the section's stylesheet": the leak is no longer caught, and the guard fails.
    3. One S0 capture with the sections visible, on the normal 390 × 844 path: the sheet scrolls, the region is scrolled into view below the sticky header, and L3 must pass. Mutation "the section overlaps the tempo by 40 px": 56,248 px off, and the guard fails.
  - **Sheet-wide section bleed** (supervisor ruling on PR #106, from HT-6's measurement in #112): the sheet panel's content box is 356 px against golden B's 358, the same 2 px `.ht-golden` bleeds for, so every section was 2 px narrower than golden B and every transform-origin 1 px off. `sheet.css` now has `.ht [data-section] { margin-inline: -1px; }` for all sections; the lanes drop their own copies.
    - **Guard 4** (`sectionWidthProblems`): every displayed section's box is exactly golden B's section width at 390, 360 and 340 px. Golden B's `.hw-sec` sections sit in the gallery card's content box; measured on `docs/howto/golden-b/artifact/technical-plates.html` (claude/howto-options) they are 358 / 328 / 308 px, equal to golden A's card content box, which the gate reads. It runs on the fixture section at the three widths, and on every registered section after each open in the full matrix.
    - Mutation "the rule removed": the fixture section is 356 / 326 / 306 px, and guard 4 fails at all three widths (and the matrix check at 390).
  **Why**: the supervisor's ruling on PR #106 (HT-6's report); layer cards add sections without editing a merged gate block.

## HT-3 sheet: choices the plan left open (HT-3 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Decided**:
  - **The golden block is one string, inserted once.** PlateView builds the block exactly as the gallery's `card()` does (plate-fit to tempo, the same whitespace), from the generated golden strings, and inserts it with `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` from a memoised value. The content is our own generated, hash-locked content, never user input. The only differences are the 2 mapped wrapper classes and one empty `div.ht-zoom-slot[hidden]` after the plate box. `plate-state.test` proves byte equality with the fixture for all 8 plates.
  - **The mistake figure** is inserted on the first Mistake tap (plan 2.5). Its `--gd`, listeners and fit run then. The gallery sets them at load, and the result is the same markup (F3).
  - **Zoom slot API** as posted in the design note. The slot is the plate box's next sibling, because hiding the plate box must not hide the slot. `setPlateHidden(true)` ends a running Trace first, because a hidden figure's animations are cancelled, not ended. `clearMistake()` always ends in normal mode with the default callout. The API object is also a non-enumerable, read-only expando `htPlateApi` on `.ht-golden`, for gate probes only; no app code reads it.
  - **App leaks fixed inside `.ht-golden` only** (L2b finds them): the app's `.sr-only` adds `margin: -1px` (reset to 0), and the app's dialog does not set the text colour and font the gallery's band sets (set on `.ht-golden` with the band's values).
  - **`.ht` is the dialog's class**, so the header rules can live under `.ht` like every How-to rule (css.test requires it). `sheet.css` has an empty `ht-tokens` block for the same lint.
  - **Two dynamic imports.** HowToSheet loads `@/howto/generated` and then the plate module, because A4 requires that generated/** is reached only through `import()`. The sheet renders only when the content is in, so it slides in full. An id without content closes with the load-failed toast; there is no fallback.
  - **Focus** goes back to the entry through the modal dialog's own focus restore (`Sheet` closes its `<dialog>`), so no code is added for it. Gate block HT-3 checks it after Escape. A focus-restore effect in HowToSheet was tried and removed: a mutation that dropped it changed nothing, so it was dead code.
  **Why**: card HT-3 and the design note on PR #106 (the supervisor's "go").

## HT-2 generator: choices the plan left open (HT-2 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Decided**:
  - **Generator reads the engine, not the fixture.** `gen/plates.mjs` rebuilds the gallery from `tools/plates/vendor` through HT-1's `golden.mjs` and extracts the fragments with HT-1's `extractPlates`, so there is one extractor. It refuses to write unless the page sha256 is the latest GOLDEN page entry (printing the first differing byte offset against the fixture) and every fragment matches its latest GOLDEN plate entry. The golden can therefore never be "fixed" from the generator side.
  - **Strings are emitted as `JSON.stringify` literals.** The parsed value is the golden byte string; nothing is re-serialized. `tests/howto/generate.test.ts` compares them with `===`, and gate block HT-2 re-hashes them after vite bundling.
  - **`view` comes from the plate's own label** (`<span class="plate-meta">Front view</span>` in the normal overlay), because HT-1's extractor has no view field and the golden is the only source.
  - **`hashes.golden`** is the sha256 of `JSON.stringify(latest GOLDEN plate entry)`, which names exactly which approval the module was built from. `hashes.inputsSha256` equals the file's own header.
  - **Layer fields are typed `?: never`** until each layer card defines its type. That is stricter than `unknown`: nothing can be set by accident.
  - **Plugin contract:** `inputs()`, optional `after` and `outputs(ctx)`. A file written by more than one plugin (plan: `ht-<slug>.ts` and `ids.ts`, plates then content) hashes the union of its writers and their inputs, and `after` orders them, so HT-5 needs no core change.
  - **The inputs of the plates plugin:** every MANIFEST file, `MANIFEST.json`, the font, `golden.mjs`, `css.mjs`, `plates.json`, `GOLDEN.json` and the fixture (read for the failure offset). These are listed in full rather than trimmed to what build-page happens to import, so no read file is missed.
  - **css.mjs drop list.** It drops the page-only rules where the app has an equal rule or the rule styles gallery chrome. From the reset `h1, h2, h3, p, figure { margin: 0; }` it keeps only `.ht figure { margin: 0; }`, because the app's reset lacks `figure` and the plate is a `<figure>`. `#sheets .plate-callout:focus-visible` is a plate rule, so it becomes `.ht .plate-callout:focus-visible`. Keyframe names (`plate-trace/-ghost/-fade`) stay verbatim; they clash with no app keyframes.
  - **ht-tokens names:** `--ht-trace: 2.4s`, `--ht-arrow-at: 2.3s`, `--ht-arrow-dur: 160ms`, `--ht-ghost-step: 80ms`, `--ht-radius-tick: 1px`. css.mjs throws on any banned literal it has no name for.
  - **Theme parity is stricter than the plan:** besides the per-theme tokens, the non-theme tokens plate.css reads (sizes, timings) must equal the vendored `engine/tokens.css` in styles.css's token block.
  - **A9 chunks are measured in gate block HT-2 with the app's own vite config**, using `src/howto/generated/index.ts` as an extra entry. Until HT-3 wires `lazy.tsx`, nothing in the app imports the loaders, so `npm run build` alone cannot emit the chunks yet.
  - **A4 is also checked from `ids.ts` itself:** its static graph is just itself, and no src file outside `generated/` statically reaches `generated/**`. This catches the card's failure path (ids.ts importing `generated/index.ts`) before HT-3 connects ids.ts to main.
  - **Gate PASS phrase:** printed as its own line after the shared PASS line, so the shared line is not edited.
  **Why**: card HT-2 and plan 2.2, 2.6, 2.7; the supervisor's "go" on PR #105.
## Golden lock details the card left open (HT-1 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Decided**: where card HT-1 and the plan are silent:
  - **`build-page.mjs` is vendored at `tools/plates/vendor/artifact/build-page.mjs`.** Its own header says it runs as `artifact/build-page.mjs` and it imports `../engine` and `../ref-src`, so the mirror keeps that layout. The MANIFEST still names its bc0f378 source path (`docs/howto/technical-plate/build-page.mjs`) and git blob.
  - **The MANIFEST also pins each file's git blob id**, so the source claim ("this is the bc0f378 blob") is checked from the bytes without needing bc0f378 in a shallow CI clone.
  - **All 12 engine files are vendored**, including the mockup helpers (`gallery.mjs`, `render.mjs`, `zoom.mjs`, `measure-chars.mjs`, `sheet.mjs`), because `index.mjs` re-exports `sheet.mjs` and the card says "the engine" verbatim.
  - **GOLDEN.json holds one `page` entry plus one `plate` entry per exercise in one hash chain** (`prev` = sha256 of `JSON.stringify` of all earlier entries). A later golden update to one plate would otherwise leave the other plates' `pageSha256` stale. `cues` hashes both figures' `firstKey` and cue list.
  - **Each plate's `src` is proven, not inferred**: the probe renders the candidate source with the gallery's id and records it only when both the normal SVG (guides removed) and the mistake SVG equal the fixture byte for byte.
  - **PRIMITIVES coverage is measured**: the probe wraps each `PRIMITIVES` entry in a counter while it renders each fixture (the vendored bytes are untouched). Only `_test_front` draws `dumbbell`.
  - **Speed**: the L1 rebuild and the fixture renders run in 4 parallel Node processes (about 6 s instead of 24 s). The harness's 10 (load, theme) captures run in parallel contexts (about 17 s instead of 35 s).
  - **The harness self-check has a built-in negative control** that runs on every gate: the same block shifted 1 px must fail the L3 rule, so a blind comparison cannot pass.
  - **Commits that touch the golden paths carry `[golden update]`**, so they pass the planned agent-guard check (plan 2.8) if it lands before HT-1 merges.
  **Why**: the owner rule ("Dont lower quality and output of the technical plates") needs byte-level proof that does not depend on git history or on names; the rest keeps the checks fast.

## Correction to "HT-2 generator": the gate PASS phrase (HT-2 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Correction**: the "HT-2 generator" entry says gate block HT-2's PASS phrase "is printed as its own line after the shared PASS line, so the shared line is not edited". That is wrong. `scripts/screenshot-gate.mjs` appends `, and HT-2 (…) verified` to the shared PASS line in place, which follows the convention HT-1 set. The edit is allowed, because the card grants the PASS phrase.
  **Why**: HT-2 review on PR #105 (medium finding). The older entry is left unchanged, because this file is append-only.

## LIB-25 poly primitive (LIB-25 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Decided**: the new primitive is `poly({ pts, curve = false, cls = 'eq', z = 'back' })`: world points like `line`, straight edges through geom's `polygon`, or a closed Catmull-Rom curve through geom's `smooth` with `curve: true`. It throws on fewer than 3 points.
  **Why**: the card asks for a closed filled polygon in the engine's line style. A rope strand needs a rounded outline, and `smooth` already exists in the vendored geom.mjs, so the curve costs one import and no new geometry. Both forms return `poly`, the field plate.mjs:213-215 already uses to outline moving parts in the Mistake view, so plate.mjs is left untouched.
  **Source**: library plan 2.5; equipment.mjs:301-302; plate.mjs:213-215.

- **Decided**: the golden-A source gets a new engine test, `exercises/_test_poly.mjs` (claude/howto-options de00174). golden.mjs's probe renders it next to `_test_front` and `_test_side`, and its two new reference fixtures are committed.
  **Why**: HT1-A4 fails when a PRIMITIVES key is drawn by no committed fixture. That check must not be loosened, and the 8 approved specs must not change. An additive engine test covers `poly`, including its Mistake outline, and leaves every existing fixture byte unchanged.
  **Source**: golden.mjs `fixtureProblems`; golden.test.ts HT1-A4.

- **Decided**: golden.mjs accepts the two LIB-25 commits by name (`7859292`, `de00174`) next to `bc0f378` and `1a1e33b`. vendor.test.ts pins the exact source commit of each changed file. The pin is not a wildcard.
  **Why**: L0 names the commit each vendored file came from. A golden update adds exactly its own commits, so any other source still fails.
  **Source**: plan 2.8 step 4.

- **Decided**: there is no new GOLDEN.json entry.
  **Why**: plan 2.8 step 5 adds an entry when a plate changes. No plate or page byte changed here: L1 still gives `e2bea90c…` (860,766 B), and golden B still gives `f39137e1…` with the new engine.
  **Source**: plan 2.8.

- **Decided**: golden B's own copy of `engine/equipment.mjs` on claude/howto-options is not changed. Golden B was proven only in a temporary copy with its engine swapped for the new one.
  **Why**: golden B is pinned by its own README, and HT-4 vendors it. Changing it would be a golden-B update, which this card does not ask for.
  **Source**: docs/howto/golden-b/README.md.

- **Decided**: the poly rope composer lives in `tools/plates/library/eq/rope.mjs` on this branch. It keeps the pilot's API (`rope`, `ropeGeometry`, `ROPE_ITEMS`), so `specs/rope_triceps_pushdown.mjs` runs unchanged. `eq/parts.mjs` is copied verbatim from claude/lib-8-pilot-a, so the two branches merge it cleanly. `ROPE_ITEMS` is now 5: the cable, 2 strands, the ferrule and the Mistake cable twin.
  **Why**: the card says the arms-machines drawer adopts this rope.
  **Source**: LIB-25 card, step 4.

## LIB-26 flat palm (LIB-26 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Decided**: the option is a spec field, `hand: 'flat'` for both hands or `{ l, r }` for one. plate.mjs passes it to `bodyShapes`, next to `armsFront`. The shape is `palm.<side>`, and plate.mjs's `partOf` maps it to `arm.<side>`, so it is part of the arm's outline union and of the Mistake mask. When the option is not set, nothing in plate.mjs or body.mjs changes.
  **Why**: the hand is a body shape, not equipment. A spec-level field is the smallest additive switch: the same pattern as `armsFront`, with no change to pose data or to the saved shape of anything.
  **Source**: LIB-26 card; body.mjs `bodyShapes`; plate.mjs `drawPose`, `partOf`.

- **Decided**: the palm is one closed outline from the wrist to the fingertips. The fingertips are `WINTER.hand` from the wrist, along the wrist→grip axis. The outline starts on the forearm's distal circle (`RADII.fore[2]`) and reaches a little behind the wrist, so the union has no gap. The palm side (screen-down) is straight at `-RADII.fore[2]` up to the knuckles, and the back of the hand drops toward the fingertips. A hand pointing at the camera falls back to the forearm's end-on circle.
  **Why**: a flat run on the palm side is what makes the hand read as flat on the floor. Using the forearm's own distal radius means a spec only has to place the wrist `RADII.fore[2] × H` above a surface (`_test_flat` does exactly that). The join test checks three things: the wrist lies inside both the palm and the forearm, and the palm starts on the forearm's distal circle. An offset palm fails it, both in the test and as an engine mutation.
  **Source**: the card ("palm capsule joined to the forearm … no gap at the wrist"); the 2.6 cm gap and 8.7 cm fist offset measured on #109.

- **Decided**: the engine test `_test_flat` is a high plank on flat palms. Its end pose rocks the body forward over fixed hands. Its Mistake puts the hands ahead of the shoulders, so the moving palm is outlined. Each pose is solved with a short Newton loop inside the spec. The engine test cannot import the library solver, because vendored specs import only from the engine.
  **Why**: this keeps the palm on the floor in every pose, and the checks read 0 cm. A knee-drive end pose was tried first and dropped: its interpolated ghosts pushed the foot below the floor line.
  **Source**: render and report of `_test_flat` (the issues list is empty in the normal and Mistake views).

- **Decided**: the branch is built on LIB-25 (claude/lib-25-poly-primitive) and the draft PR targets that branch.
  **Why**: the card merges LIB-26 after LIB-25, and both edit the same MANIFEST pin and the same source list in golden.mjs. Stacking them avoids a conflict on the vendor lock. After LIB-25 merges, GitHub moves the PR's base to `main`.
  **Source**: LIB-26 card; plan 2.8.
## HT-4 golden-B lock, content types and the derivation test: choices the card left open (HT-4 builder, 2026-09-30)

- **Decided**: `content-types.ts`'s `PointRef` uses the plate engine's real convention (`{ at, pose?, off? }` or `{ along, t, off? }`), not GA section 4.1's literal `{ landmark, pose?, dx?, dy? }` spelling.
  **Why**: every vendored golden-B file (`dumbbell_lateral_raise.howto.mjs`'s own comment: "Point references use the plate engine's form ({ at, pose, off }), SPEC.md 3") uses `at`/`off`, never `landmark`/`dx`/`dy`. GA's prose predates the engine's actual field names; the card's risk_and_recovery line ("add it to the types... never drop a mockup field") points at following what golden-B really does.
- **Decided**: `content-types.ts` keeps every mockup-only field golden-B's files use (`chipCaption`, `panelHeight`, `notes`, `cameraLabel`, `callout`/`callouts` — both spellings appear — `feelPrompt`, `insetRight`/`insetWrong`, `rollDeg`, `crop.pose`, `HandPose.thumb: 'loose'`) as optional, rather than stripping them in a mapping step.
  **Why**: same risk_and_recovery line, taken literally ("never drop a mockup field"). HT4-A2's "mapped" compile check only strips `plate` (the criterion's own words: "There is no plate field").
- **Decided**: C4 (thumb rule) fails only when `overBody`/`hang` gets neither a `wrapped` default nor an offered `over` option; offering `over` alongside a non-wrapped default does not itself fail.
  **Why**: the architecture table's "Fails when" phrasing ("the thumb default is not wrapped or over is offered") is genuinely ambiguous — read literally it would also fail `hang`'s real content whenever a thumb-page shows non-wrapped options for information only. The permissive reading (always leaves a safe thumb configuration available) is the one that doesn't contradict `push`'s own "Required when overBody" note (a requirement, not a ban on offering alternatives elsewhere). Revisit if a future card's review disagrees.
- **Decided (HT4-A5)**: the "crop key" allowance a still-driven `renderPlate` call may vary from golden-A is a named field set, not a per-exercise path list: `poses`, `camera`, `seatDrop`, `datum`, `viewLabel`, `equipment`, `ghosts`, `startParts`, `marks` (all plate-engine drawing fields, SPEC.md's schema, not GA's `HowToContent`), the spec's own embedded `mistake` sub-pose, and the labels `id`/`name`/`view`/`facing`. `tempo`, `alt`, `checks` and `callouts` are held to zero tolerance (byte-identical, or empty for the last two). Derived empirically: a real module-hook capture of a real `build-page.mjs` run (48 calls across the 7 non-ref-src exercises) was diffed field-by-field against each exercise's own golden-A vendor spec, grouped by the call's own stable `spec.id`, before naming the list — never assumed from the plan's four-word summary ("id, mistake flag, crop window, zoom labels").
