## D-BUG38: next split follows the sessions done (BUG-38 builder, 2026-10-03)

- **Rule (D-BUG38, from the card in docs/supervisor/verify/BUG-38.md).** Weekdays decide when you train. Your sessions decide which planned days are already covered. A split you skipped by training another one moves into the next day an early session freed up. `src/brain/splitPlan.ts` holds the one pure `splitPlan()`. It reads only `schedule`, `splits`, `sessions` and `daysOff`, so nothing new is saved.
  1. **Sessions used.** A session's plan day is today when it is in `trainedTodaySessions` (QA8-4); otherwise it is its stored day. Only plan days from T-17 to T count. Each (splitId, plan day) keeps its earliest session. A past day off has no slot.
  2. **On-day, then early.** A session counts for its own day when that day's split is its split. Otherwise, early: the first planned day within 3 days after it, if that day has its split. There is no late step (D-BUG38-2). A day counts at most one session.
  3. **Owed.** A split is owed only when another session took its day. It stays owed until a later session of that split, or until its weekday comes round again.
  4. **Walk** T-14..T+7. A day whose split was done early goes to the oldest owed split, never the day before that split's own day. Without an owed split the day is done early. Placing a split on a future day uses it up; so does placing it on today when nothing was trained today.
  5. **Outputs.** `today` keeps its split on a day off, with `off`. Also `doneEarly`, `next` (the first split in (T, T+7]) and `days` (T..T+7).
- **Consumers.** All of them read this one function:
  - selectors: `todayPlan` is built from per-field computeds, never from `state.value` whole. `scheduledSplit` is undefined on a day off (UI-R03).
  - The coach: `derive`, `readinessSeries` (one plan per day, now = 23:59:59) and the scheduled-conflict rule.
  - Escobar: `splitPlanOf`, `readinessToday`, the brief and `get_overview` (`movedFrom`).
  - Reminders (`planned` map) and the Today card (`sessionCardState`).
  - `nextScheduledSplitOf`, `nextScheduledSplitFor` and `scheduledSplitId` are deleted.
- **D-BUG38-coach.** No `scheduled-conflict` on a day off (UI-R03). After any session today, the coach names what was done ("Done today: A + B", "A + B are done for today.") and gives the plan's next split. It never warns about today's own plan, which revises QA8-1 point 3 (LIVE-QA-8.md:20, :23). The "Below 60%" warning lines stay byte-identical; the AUD-20 follow-up owns them.
- **D-BUG38-pins.** The card's exact edits are made and nothing else changed:
  - coach.test.ts:275 is renamed "BUG-38: after any session today …". `priorHamSession` becomes `split_push`, and the test expects `recovery.done-today:split_chest` with "Done today: Push" and "Next: Upper on Mon".
  - coach.test.ts:284 changes only `priorHamSession` to `split_push`.
  - The old fixture (SPLIT 2 trained Friday) is pinned as done early by the new AC13 test.
- **D-BUG38-1 (supervisor ruling on #199, 10-03).** Deviation from the card. The coach's plan is memoised per CoachContext (a WeakMap, `todayPlanOf`), not kept as a `Derived` field. tests/aud20.test.ts:76-78 (AUD-20's pin, outside this card) runs the rule with a hand-built `Derived`. The plan is still built once per context.
- **D-BUG38-2 (supervisor ruling on #199, 10-03).** The card's late step is removed because it was unobservable:
  - A late count only stops its past day from being displaced.
  - That debt is paid by the same session in rule 3, at a walk day ≤ T.
  - A placement on a past day is never used up.
  - So `today`, `doneEarly`, `next` and `days` (T..T+7) cannot change. The same holds for `readinessSeries`: for a past day D, a late count lands before D.
  - AC10a's test is dropped. "a session after a missed day clears the debt" pins the outcome the late step was meant to give: dropping the paid clause turns it red.
- **D-BUG38-3 (supervisor ruling on #199, 10-03).** A session of a deleted split, or of a split on no weekday, counts for no slot, but it still takes that day from its own split (edge 5, the revised coach.test fixture).
- **Known limits.**
  - The 6-hour midnight switch (QA8-4) is pinned on both sides by the QA8-1 fixture test.
  - Past days are matched against the current schedule, the same limit as streak and adherence.
  - A snooze on the old `recovery.done-today:<scheduled id>` may not match the new id, which is keyed by the split done. The note lasts one day.
## PLAY-PREP (2026-10-03)
- **D-PLAY-PREP-1.** The Play forms in `docs/PLAY-SUBMISSION.md` follow one data-flow inventory, traced to file:line on `000918e`. Where code cannot show something (Anthropic's and Google's retention, Cloudflare platform logs), the inventory says "not verified". The earlier draft's stale citations and two wrong claims are fixed: height is never sent to the coach (`src/escobar/context/brief.ts:104-113`), and diagnostics are "Crash logs" plus "Diagnostics", not one type.
- **D-PLAY-PREP-2.** Data safety "Shared": No for every type. Play's definition (answer/10787469, read 2026-10-03) excludes transfers to a service provider that processes data for the developer on his instructions; Cloudflare hosts the developer's Worker and Anthropic answers requests the Worker makes with the developer's key. A custom coach server is a transfer the user starts, also excluded. Risk: if Play's review reads Anthropic as a third party, the answer for the coach rows flips to Shared: Yes with the same purposes; the form can be updated without a new release.
- **D-PLAY-PREP-3.** The 16 KB check fails only on ELF `PT_LOAD` alignment below 16384 for arm64-v8a and x86_64 libraries. The zip offset of an uncompressed library inside the `.aab` is reported, not enforced: devices install APKs that Play builds from the bundle with bundletool, which page-aligns uncompressed libraries itself, so the `.aab` offset never reaches a phone. Enforcing it could fail a valid bundle. 32-bit ABIs are out of the requirement's scope.
- **D-PLAY-PREP-4.** Declarations err on the side of more: App interactions (the screen name in the coach summary and in error reports), Other user-generated content (exercise and memory notes), Diagnostics, and Health info for reply reports (a reported reply can repeat health details). Over-declaring costs nothing at review; under-declaring is a policy violation.
- **D-PLAY-PREP-5.** The 16 KB self-test is `scripts/check-play-bundle.py --self-test` with committed fixtures in `scripts/fixtures/play-bundle/`, because the existing self-test lives in `.github/workflows/play-bundle.yml`, which builders do not edit. The supervisor adds a CI step that runs it.
- **D-PLAY-PREP-6.** The store listing claims 8 exercise guides, the number shipped (`src/howto/generated/ht-*.ts`), and is updated as library batches ship. No contacts, links or sources in the listing (LR-23).
- **Source**: card PLAY-PREP (supervisor, 2026-10-03); audit 3 "Google Play readiness" (PLAY 01).
- **D-PLAY-HR-1 (supervisor, 2026-10-03).** `READ_HEART_RATE` is removed by card PLAY-HR (branch `claude/play-hr`): no user-facing feature used it (session heart rate comes from the Bluetooth watch; the Health Connect reading reached only the sync Details sheet), and Play's Health Connect guidance (answer/12991134) forbids requesting data types the app does not need. The Health apps declaration names 4 permissions, category "Activity and Fitness", use case "Fitness, wellness and coaching". The owner may tick the category now; the Health Connect permission declaration and the closed-test release wait for the first Play bundle built after PLAY-HR merges. Bundle 37.1.0.47 and earlier still declare `READ_HEART_RATE` and must not go to testers.
- **PLAY-PREP review round 1** (#198 @ 48e72e1): fixed every finding as the reviewer wrote it: false permission reasons, missing Data safety details (height, BMI, body fat, safety flags, steps and calories gated, the reply-report purpose on Other info), listing claims, and the timing of the Health apps step.
- **D-PLAY-HR-1, correction (delta review on #198 @ f381135).** The cutoff "bundle 37.1.0.47 and earlier" in D-PLAY-HR-1 is wrong: later bundles (runs 48 to 52, including the first upload-signed one) still declare `READ_HEART_RATE`. The rule is: only a bundle built from a `main` commit that contains PLAY-HR's merge may be uploaded, and its manifest is checked first for no `android.permission.health.READ_HEART_RATE`.
- **D-LIB7-13 (replaces D-LIB7-9), rope.** The rope is drawn as a plain section with its 46 mm knob dashed over the fist, coaxial, from the plate composer. It sits in the fingers (contactAt 1.15, GA 3.1.1 drawing value). Check G7 tests it (calibrated critic, 2026-10-03).
- **D-LIB7-14, push Right.** The handle sits low in the heel, as on golden B's approved squat Right (contactAt -0.1, wrist 8°). The Right force line is re-aimed through the wrist pivot. G4 and G6 test it.
- **D-LIB7-15, EZ angled grip.** ez_bar_curl's claims (#c1 angled sections, #c2 semi-supinated) name the angled grip. It is drawn with orientation `angled`, labelled "Seen along the angled grip", in its own tile. preacher_curl names no angle and stays underhand.
- **D-LIB7-16, delta review on #193 @ bfacafb.** ez_bar_curl's angled grip is drawn palm up: the camera looks along the angled section, so the hand shows as an underhand grip does; the camera words carry the angle, and G8 checks it. The rope fist is curl 2's squared fist (contactAt .6) instead of GA 3.1.1's load in the fingers, which golden B draws as a point; G7 measures the fist's front. The push drawing values (-0.1, 8°) are flagged on the sheet.
- **D-LIB7-16a, rope contact (ruling on #193).** Case 1 applied: no rope_triceps_pushdown claim names where the load sits (shared/rope-rule.json c1-c6; gap 1 says no source does). The .6 square-fist contact is cited as the flagged drawing value `ga:rope-fist-mid`, and the unused `ga:rope-fingers` is removed.
- **D-LIB7-17 (ruling D-LIB7-17a), rope label.** Case 1 by claim (shared/rope-rule.json#c1, hand against the knob), but no one view shows both #c1 and the #c6 wrist curl, which turns about the rope's own axis; #c6 decides. The top view stays, the Right note is the drawn contact "Middle of palm" (ga:rope-fist-mid), the knob wording is dropped, #c1 stays cited. The knob rim lies wholly inside the fist outline (at least 2.6 mm in), so it stays dashed; G7 checks this.
- **D-LIB7-18, push Right load line.** Drawn straight down the forearm axis through the wrist pivot, from the level of the contact (G6: within 2° of the axis). The handle stays at the squat pose: golden B's palm keeps the contact 12 px off the axis, and putting it on the axis needs wrist 15° with the handle back at the finger fold.
- **D-LIB7-18a, push Right without a force line (replaces D-LIB7-18).** As on golden B's squat Right, the push Rights (single_arm_triceps_pushdown, skull_crusher) draw no force line; the contact dot in the heel, the pivot and the reference wrist tick stay. G6 checks it.
- **D-LIB7-18b (amends D-LIB7-18a).** The push Right follows golden B's squat Right in full: no force line and no wrist tick; the contact dot in the heel and the pivot stay. G6 fails a push Right with no force line that draws a tick.
- **D-LIB7-19 / D-LIB7-19a, push Rights parked.** Golden B's hand cannot seat a heel push low and over the forearm (golden-B follow-up 9; measured grid on #193), so single_arm_triceps_pushdown and skull_crusher are gaps until plan 2.8 fixes the engine: LIB-7 draws 12 ids, 18 gaps. The push design and its checks are parked, not deleted; a test fixture keeps their failure paths.
## A3-6 history and weekly numbers (2026-10-04)
- **D-A3-6-1 (UI-R02).** `exerciseHistory` trusts a known exercise id (library or custom). The name fallback runs only for an id that resolves to nothing, so two custom exercises with one name keep separate history and a legacy record with a deleted id still resolves by name.
- **D-A3-6-2 (UI-R06, ruling R8).** `deleteGym` does nothing for the live session's gym, and the Gyms sheet shows no Delete on that gym. No new text. D-AUD10-3 already fixes a running session's gym context; this keeps that gym alive until the workout ends.
- **D-A3-6-3 (ENG-03).** The weekly review passes `muscleVolumeStatus` only the sessions before the reviewed week ends, so a session in the next week cannot change last week's verdict.
- **D-A3-6-4 (ENG-04).** A session with no working set (warm-ups only) does not count as a workout in `weekSummary`, `reviewWeek`, the review's week sessions or `muscleVolumeStatus`'s full-week test, matching `trainingStreak`. It is still saved and shown in history.
- **D-A3-6-5 (ENG-06, 6.17.4).** `durationDriftInsight` builds its 5-session baseline from prior sessions with trusted timing only (`logging?.timingTrusted ?? true`, the default `postSessionInsights` uses).
- **D-A3-6-6 (ENG-07).** `postSessionInsights` treats only sessions that started earlier as prior, as Escobar's `getSession` does (`read.ts:174`), so a back-logged workout keeps its records on Finish. The input array is reused when nothing is later, so the history cache still hits.
- **Source**: card A3-6 (audit 3, `docs/supervisor/verify/audit-3-cards.md`).

## GATE-SPLIT build (GATE-SPLIT builder, 2026-10-04; HT-10 merged in train 13, main c542ae9)

- **D-GATESPLIT-KEYS Decided**: 99 groups. Blocks whose header names no task (they predate task IDs, and `git log -S` reaches only a re-indent commit) get the noun of their header: `BASE` (the first theme walk), `Onboarding`, `Weekly-review`, `Resting-HR`, `Watch-stub`, `Plate-sense`, `Palace`, `Heart-line`, `Hotfix` (3 statements), and `ITEM-7.5` (the two 7.5 blocks, one task). `ESC-NC` holds its pattern check and the Escobar loop, because both read `ESC_NC_RE`. Repeated IDs in separate places get `.1`/`.2` (I6, F5, O1).
- **D-GATESPLIT-R3 Decided** (refines D-GATESPLIT-COUPLING): R3 applies to bindings declared after `errors` that a group mutates. Such a binding belongs to one group (`bug22Runs`: BUG-22). Read-only ones may be shared, because their unguarded declaration runs in every job and R4 keeps it free of checks.
  - `ESC_NC_RE` is read by ESC-NC and HT-9. HT-9 reads only `.source`/`.flags`, and no pattern has the `g` flag, so there is no shared `lastIndex`.
  - An ALWAYS group may mutate such a binding: `HT-10.clock` pushes to `ht10Clock.lines`.
- **D-GATESPLIT-REPLAY Decided**: HT-3 sets `process.env.HT3_DUMP ??= <screenshots>/ht3-diffs` (where the harness writes diff images on an L3 failure; read again by HT-4, HT-6, HT-7, HT-8 and HT-9). In a job that does not run HT-3, `gate-split.mjs` replays exactly that write at HT-3's file position, so every later group sees what it saw in one serial run.
  - R5 allows only the writes listed in `REPLAYED`, and a test pins the list to what the parser finds.
  - **Why**: HT-3's block is another task's code and stays byte-identical. Without the replay, a split job would lose failure evidence (not a check) for those groups.
- **D-GATESPLIT-HT10JOB Decided**: the verdict proves HT-10's tuples once per time zone. `ht10-proof.json` has no time zone, so the supervisor's add-only YAML adds one step to `ht10-gate` that writes `screenshots/ht10-job.json` (`{tz, shard}`) before the upload. HT-10's code is not changed.
## LIB-2 build: decisions taken while building (library enabler builder, 2026-10-04)

Built on main c542ae9 (HT-10 merged) from the design note that passed at 2b8f52e plus the addendum 56d0c2e (#189).
- **D-LIB2-loaders.** LOADERS lives in `src/howto/generated/loaders.ts` (chunk `loaders-*.js`), not `ht-index.ts` as the design named it. **Why**: `ht-*.ts` is the plate-module pattern in five tests (ids, sections-risks, plate-state, generate, no-form-guide) and in HT-10's "one ht- chunk per id" count; `ht-index` would have broken all of them. Before LIB-2 the map was already a lazy chunk, but Vite named it `index-*.js`, which no budget could target.
- **D-LIB2-index.** `generated/index.ts` stays, as `export { LOADERS } from './loaders'`. **Why**: `@/howto/generated` keeps its meaning, and HT-2's generate.test keeps index.ts as plates.mjs's fixed single-writer file.
- **D-LIB2-hints.** `HOWTO_HINTS` keeps its one-line-per-id object format in ids.ts, typed `Readonly<Record<string, string>>`, and `HowToId` is a branded string. **Why**: HT-6's gate block A8 parses those exact lines from ids.ts and may not be edited; ids.ts may not import types (HT2-A5), so it cannot name `LibId`. The plan's deduplicated hint encoding waits for LIB-1's ids.ts numbers.
- **D-LIB2-ids-list.** The shipped id list (`HOWTO_IDS`) moved from ids.ts to `generated/loaders.ts`. Five tests that imported it from `@/howto/ids` (ids, hint, css, content-gen, no-form-guide) now import it from there; no assertion changed.
- **D-LIB2-bodies.** The identity list holds 54 files, not the design's 45: the generator also writes 9 research JSON files (`docs/research/howto/*.json`) that the design count missed. After LIB-2 the generator writes 60 files: those 54, the 2 it rewrites (ids.ts, generated/index.ts) and 4 new ones (ids-node.mjs, lib-id.ts, loaders.ts, coverage.ts).
- **D-LIB2-core-tests.** HT-2's generate.test.ts gets three edits, each forced by the design: plates.mjs's output list drops ids.ts (it moved to gen/ids.mjs); the multi-writer rule allows ids.ts = ids.mjs then content.mjs; the no-render recomputation uses a plugin's `inputsFor(path)` when it has one (per-output inputs). No check is weakened: each still compares exact values.
- **D-LIB2-builder.** `tools/plates/library/build-page.mjs` and `engine.mjs` are LIB-8's blobs unchanged (fb4f074 for build-page). So the builder has no empty-list refusal of its own; L2-A2 asserts the 8-row count instead. LIB-8's `node:test` file stays on LIB-8; the proof runs in vitest.
- **D-LIB2-browser.** The A10 request probe runs on Chromium only: it is the only browser installed in this environment (/opt/pw-browsers holds chromium-1194 and its headless shell).

## LIB-2 review fixes @ ee14df8 (library enabler builder, 2026-10-04)

Source: "REVIEW LIB-2 @ ee14df8: FAIL" on #189.
- **D-LIB2-layers.** The layer generators (plates, content, feel, zooms, hands) now read the 8 through the registry (`goldenRows()`, `sourceOf(id)`) and give every output its own inputs (`inputsFor`). content.mjs narrows them per exercise: a module and its research card are hashed over that exercise's source only. plates, feel, zooms and hands cut every output from one shared source (golden A's gallery, golden B's page), so each output's inputs are that whole shared set; per-id narrowing is not possible there. They read plates.json only, never a batch file, so a new batch row stales none of the 8's files (L2-A4, now tested with an injected batch row). Library rows' layer outputs come from LIB-6's renderer and are written when it lands; until then these plugins emit the 8 only.
- **D-LIB2-builder (correction).** The plates page builder gets its registry input through a new `tools/plates/library/plates-page.mjs`: a page file (`pages/golden-a.json`) names groups by library id, and each id's spec comes from its registry row. `build-page.mjs` itself stays LIB-8's blob.
- **D-LIB2-feel-test.** HT-8's feel.test.ts copies the feel plugin's inputs into a temp root by a fixed list; the registry module joins that list (one path added, no assertion changed), because the registry is now a feel input.
- **D-LIB2-loaders-serial.** The loaders chunk still loads after the sheet chunk, not in parallel (plan 5.2). **Why**: starting it from `lazy.tsx` makes HT-3b's footprint test (`tests/howto/footprint.test.ts`, whose esbuild bundle externalises only `./HowToSheet`) inline every plate chunk, so it would need another task's test edited; the hop count is the same as before LIB-2 and the gate's tap-to-plate median is 184 ms of 400. A later card that needs the saving changes both together.
## GATE-FLAKE-2: the HT-10 C10 probe measures at rest (2026-10-04)

- **D-GF2-1 Decided** (cause and fix): main's run 37163451459 failed `emerald machine-chest-press [wrist line]: C10: #machine-chest-press-zoom-hand-close is 41.934 x 41.934`. A close-up opens only after its chunk loads (`ZoomHost.open` awaits `load(k)`), then shows the panel and starts its WAAPI entry `scale(.9) → none` (240 ms) in the same task. The wrist-line step in `ht10Script` tapped and ran `settle()` at once, which saw no animation yet; when the chunk landed after that settle and before the probe measured, the probe read the panel mid-entry. It is an animation, not a layout shift or a font swap. Fix, in the harness only: `ht10DomProbe` first waits, on the page clock and at most 3 s, for every finite running animation or transition whose target is a control or holds one; one still running at the bound is the named problem "C10: n animation(s) on controls still running after 3000 ms …" and the probe still measures. An endless animation is not waited for (C12 reports it); Trace's paths and the feel bands hold no control, so "Trace running" still probes mid-Trace. The wrist-line step now also waits for its close-up to show, as the chip and "Show me" steps do, so it measures the close-up instead of racing it. No size, tolerance, control or exemption changed.
- **D-GF2-1 Reproduction** (deterministic; the reviewer's method on #212, rerun by the builder): with the service worker on, the hand chunk is requested about 9 s before the wrist-line tap, so delaying its fetch does not reach the tap (the builder's first "150 ms delay" note held only with the service worker blocked, where the chunk is fetched on the tap, and it reproduced in 2 runs of 3). Instead, in a scratch copy of the harness: `ctx.route(/\/assets\/hand-machine-chest-press-/)` holds every matching response (2 requests) until the sweep's `__ht10Probe` starts for label "wrist line", then releases them and waits 120 ms before measuring (the CI order: the chunk lands after `settle()` and before the read). Sweep `ht10Sweep(browser, port, 'emerald', { ids: ['machine-chest-press'] })`, Chromium 141, 3 runs each: c542ae9's harness reports `C10: #machine-chest-press-zoom-hand-close is 43.666 / 43.534 / 43.666 x … (< 44 x 44)` in 3 of 3; this PR's probe with c542ae9's wrist-line step reports no C10 problem in 3 of 3, so the probe wait alone closes the race.
- **D-GF2-2 Decided** (fixtures, gate block HT-10 "fixtures"; the probe now also returns the ids it measured): three cases on the machine chest press hand close-up, each failing as "not measured" if its close button is not in the probe's measured ids. Slowed (the page's animations at 1/10 speed through CDP `Animation.setPlaybackRate`, so the entry runs 2.4 s and is running when the probe starts): at 44 px no problem on the close button; shrunk to 40 px exactly "40.000 x 40.000". Settled (normal speed, entry finished, then shrunk to 40 px, visible and still): exactly "40.000 x 40.000" from any probe. Plus a 60 s transform on a section must be named within the bound, and an endless opacity animation on a control must not be counted. Mutations: the wait removed fails both slowed cases (on the builder's machine the old probe read 40.012 and 36.374 mid-entry; the reviewer's runs had the button still at opacity 0, which now fails as "not measured") and the bound case, while the settled case still passes; the button hidden (opacity 0) fails the slowed 40 px and settled cases as "not measured"; endless animations counted fails with "2 animation(s)".
## D-A3-2: data safety (A3-2 builder, 2026-10-04)

- **R3, DATA 01.** `repairState` keeps a custom exercise that has an id and a name, and gives `primary`, `secondary`, `stabilizers` and `aliases` a list of strings each (`[]` when missing or invalid, non-strings dropped from a list). Dropping the record would cut its sessions off from their exercise.
- **R4, DATA 02.** Only the deferred save (`persistSoon`) and the flushed save (`flushSave`) check first whether storage still holds what this tab last read or wrote (`storageMovedOn`). If another tab wrote or cleared it and the storage event has not arrived (frozen or back-forward cached tab), this tab takes what storage holds and does not write. `replaceState`, `resetState` and the boot write stay unconditional: an explicit replace wins. With nothing read or written yet (`lastGoodRaw` null, e.g. the boot write failed) the save goes ahead, so a failed boot write never blocks the next edit.
- **CSV rule, QA CSV 01.** In the sessions CSV, the split, exercise, exercise-note and session-note cells get a leading `'` when they start with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a CR (OWASP CSV injection), then are quoted as before. Numeric columns are never touched. A leading `'` shows in plain-text viewers; accepted as the standard mitigation.
- **Cache rule, QA CACHE 01.** Both resets (Settings "Reset everything" and the crash card) remove the app-cache folders `MARC Exports` and `MARC Share` on native (`clearExportCache`), as `PRIVACY-POLICY.md` promises. On the web there is no Filesystem call. The crash card clears storage synchronously first, then reloads when the cache call settles or after 2 s, whichever comes first.
- **Residual risk.** The pre-load crash reset in `index.html` runs before the bundle and cannot reach the Filesystem plugin; logged for the supervisor as a follow-up.
- **AC3 mutation M5.** The card expected M5 (guard also when `lastGoodRaw` is null) to turn case (a) red. Case (a) cannot catch it: a boot from backup writes main at once, so `lastGoodRaw` is never null there. Case (d), a failed boot write followed by a recovered edit, is the legitimate save M5 blocks, and it turns red under M5.
- **D-A3-1 (card A3-1, 2026-10-04; rulings R1, R2 confirmed by the supervisor).**
  - **COACH 01:** every request attempt, the busy or timeout retry included, is built from the state as it is at that moment, so a sharing switch turned off during the back-off holds for the retry.
  - **Undo toast (IMP-E02):** the toast carries its own conversation's id. When that conversation is not the visible one, the decision is saved to it quietly (`updateConversationQuietly`), the running loop's copy follows, and the visible chat does not change. A conversation reset since makes the tap a no-op ("No conversation.").
  - **Profile Undo (IMP-E03, R1):** Apply records the field value, today's weigh-in row and the history entry it wrote. Undo restores the field only while it still holds Apply's value, removes the row only while that row is still in the log, and drops the history entry. When neither the field nor the row is still Apply's, Undo is unavailable and changes nothing, so the history entry stays true.
  - **Equipment proposals (IMP-E04, R2):** the fingerprint includes whether the proposal's gym still exists, so Apply after the gym is deleted reports stale. A change of active gym alone does not change it. A proposal pending across the update reads stale once.
  - **Haptic feedback (IMP-E06):** the coach's Apply and Undo of the setting set the runtime switch, as the Settings toggle does.
- **D-A3-4 (A3-4 builder, 2026-10-04; rulings R5 and R6 confirmed by the supervisor).** Workout timing and history edits (ENG-05, ENG-02, UI-R01, UI-R04).
  - **ENG-05:** `resolveSessionTiming` and `logPastSession` rebuild the recovery model inside their `update`, as History's `withSessions` does (UI-12). A retime or a back-logged session changes what later sessions were compared against.
  - **ENG-02 (R5, revises the sets half of D-B15 above):** a red day suggests today's planned rows − 1 (minimum 1), in every mode. A lighter week suggests round(planned × setFactor) when a pre-week session exists; a lift first logged inside the week still repeats its own count (BUG-15 A1, `tests/progression.test.ts:455-460`). Carries and sleds take the same lighter-week cut (`cutSets`); only timed holds are exempt (D-A1 (1)). Loads and the pre-week base stay as D-B15 says.
  - **UI-R01 (R6, revises the substitution entry above; D-AUD10-1 kept):** with no committed set on the entry, Substitute swaps in place exactly as before (gate AUD-10). Otherwise the original keeps only its committed sets and is marked Done (a skipped entry stays skipped and not Done, so the two flags never both hold), and the substitute is inserted right after it with blank rows = the original's working rows − its committed working rows (minimum 1), not Done, not skipped, `plannedId` = the original lineage, no target, and the slot's `loadFactor`. "Save for future" then saves the original with its committed count and the substitute as an addition after it (QA3-6b's "what you actually did"). The set that started a running rest stays on the original, so a split substitute clears the rest's starter record: substituting still keeps the timer as the floor (BUG-21, `tests/heart-bug21.test.ts`).
  - **UI-R04 (plan 6.17.2):** History's Save marks a set `edited` when its kg, reps, effort or `durationSec` differs from the saved set, matched by set id; a legacy set with no id is matched by position only while its exercise kept its set count, otherwise it is marked edited. `logging.liveShare` is recomputed over the working sets finishSession rated (committed, `fidelity ?? 'live'`) plus any now edited, and `timingTrusted` becomes `old && liveShare >= 0.7`, never raised (plan :868). The plan's `editedAt` stamp is a new saved field, so it waits for the owner; this fix does not need it.
  - **Source**: card A3-4 (`docs/supervisor/verify/audit-3-cards.md`), repros in `docs/supervisor/verify/a3-tests/timing/`.
- **D-A3-4a (supervisor ruling on #214, 2026-10-04).** `tests/live-retarget-next.test.ts` "a substitution drops the replaced lift's target" looked entries up by index 0. Under R6, index 0 is the original lift, which keeps its committed set and that set's target (D-A4 (a)). The test now finds the entries by `exerciseId`, and it also asserts that the original keeps its target. Its intent (the new lift starts with no target and stores its own) is unchanged.
- **D-A3-5 (card A3-5, supervisor ruling R7, 2026-10-04).** Escobar's live facts come from the same sources as Train.
  - **Load factor (IMP-E01, R7).** During a live workout, `progressionCtxFor` uses the live entry's own `loadFactor` (set from the override when the workout started) and does not read today's override, so neither midnight nor another split's override changes the coach's target. With no live workout it reads today's override as before. Accepted: an override applied later the same day does not reach running entries, which is Train's behaviour.
  - **Clock (IMP-E05, pause and rest).** `get_live_session` reports `elapsedMin` and `restSecLeft` from Train's own `elapsedSec` and `restRemainingSec` (`session.ts`), so a running pause stops both.
  - **Profile (ENG-01, LOAD-AWARE-TARGETS.md §2).** One profile source: `menuProfile` = the profile of the gym's load menu (`loadMenu`), where another gym's exercise profile gives only the unit. `profileFor`, `progressionCtxFor`, `loadOf`, the brief's entry units and `plate_breakdown` use it; `resolveProfile` stays as it is and is called only inside `src/brain/units.ts` (pinned by `tests/escobar/a3-5-menu.test.ts`). `get_live_session` passes the gym's menu to `suggestNext` like `get_next_target`, so its live advice is measured against Train's target (a learned rung).
  - **Open (not decided here).** IMP-E05's done count: `setsDone` counts working sets as the exercise card does (`Train.tsx:727`, typed sets included), while the header's progress (`:457`) and the returned set list count committed ones; which count is right needs the supervisor or the owner. Its repro case stays in `docs/supervisor/verify/a3-tests/coach-facts/imp-e05.test.ts`.
- **D-GATESPLIT-FIX1 Decided** (review of #194 at 0df9648, FAIL 0/0/2/2; supervisor 10-16):
  - **Files rewritten count.** A group is credited with every file it writes, new or rewritten: the snapshot is name → mtime and size, not names only. A stale `screenshots/`, or a name an earlier group in the same job wrote, no longer changes a group's `files` or `gate-theme` rows. The reviewer reproduced 305 false differences on a stale folder.
  - **Proof sha.** The proof's sha is always `git rev-parse HEAD`. When `GITHUB_SHA` is set and differs, the job pushes an error and the verdict's wrong-sha check turns red.
  - **Required jobs.** The verdict requires all four needed jobs to be present in `MARC_NEEDS`.
  - **Duplicate uploads.** `collect` drops byte-identical proof copies (a failed `visual-gate-tz` uploads its proof twice; two jobs never write equal bytes).
  - **Budget scope.** The 1,800 s job budget (and the 900 s re-pick note) apply to split shards only. The serial arrangement is E8's reference, today's one-job layout under its 60 min timeout; the first serial dispatch (run 37182075671) was red on the budget alone, at 2,728 s and 2,747 s.
  - Each fix has a test that fails on 0df9648 and passes after.
- **D-GATESPLIT-HT6 Noted**: the same serial dispatch (37182075671, UTC, Chrome 153) failed `HT-6: paper lat-pulldown C11 close animation: app [] vs golden [...]`: the close animation was not captured. The block's code is unchanged (E7), and the split run on the same sha passed HT-6. This is a timing probe in another task's block, so it goes to GATE-FLAKE-1 (#179); GATE-SPLIT changes no probe. E8's 5 pairs show whether it repeats.

## LIB-2 reopened: the How-to wiring at scale (LIB-2 builder, 2026-10-16)

From the LIB-1 rehearsal (#209, report 5977306108) and the supervisor's reopening on #189 (library plan 5.2 lines 514, 518, 527, 528). Built on main 4180799.
- **D-LIB2-sheet-maps.** The three `import.meta.glob` maps that sat in the sheet chunk (`zoom/registry.ts` hand, `sections/Posture.tsx`, `sections/Feel.tsx`) are gone. `gen/ids.mjs` writes per-chromeId `HAND`, `POSTURE` and `FEEL` maps into `generated/loaders.ts`, in shipped-row order. `HAND` is an exercise's own `hand-<chromeId>` chunk, else its shared `handpair-<key>` chunk, so it replaces `PAIR_OF`/`PAIR_LOADERS`. An entry exists only for a chunk a layer plugin writes: `ids.mjs` now runs after hands, zooms, feel and handpairs (`after`), reads their outputs from `ctx.prev`, and lists their modules as loaders.ts inputs. HowToSheet passes the loaders module it already awaits down through `LoadersContext` (`src/slices/howto/loadersContext.ts`). Feel reads `FEEL` from it synchronously, so "no chunk, render nothing" is kept. The hand and posture loaders keep their fixed signatures (HT-6's API, and HT-7's test pins the registry line), so they `import()` the same loaders module, which the sheet has already loaded: no new request. **Effect**: HowToSheet-*.js is 24,014 B raw / 8,943 B gz at both N = 8 and N = 153 (it was 28,294 B at 8 and 108,411 B at 153). Its budget stays 30,884 / 10,728.
- **D-LIB2-loaders-budget.** The `loaders-*.js` ceiling becomes the per-id rule `ceil(measured8 × 11 × N / 80)`, N = the registry's shipped ids (`perIdCeiling` in `tools/plates/library/budgets/total.mjs`, the same form as the total). It sits in its own `perId` list in `tests/howto/budgets.json`, not in `budgets`, because `budgets.test.ts` holds every `budgets` entry to measured + 10 % and a per-id ceiling passes that bound at N > 8. Measured on the 8 with the new maps: 4,561 B raw / 1,088 B gz, so 5,018 / 1,197 at N = 8. The 1,152 / 475 entry goes: the maps it did not hold moved in from the sheet chunk. At 153 the chunk is 86,831 / 17,981, within 95,953 / 22,890. A batch re-sets `n`, `rawMax` and `gzMax` together (L2-A23, gate A10).
- **D-LIB2-synthetic.** The synthetic-N build (`tools/plates/library/scale/synthetic.mjs`, test L2-A24) works in a temporary copy of the repo. It clones the 8's generated modules into the next library ids, as LIB-1's `clone.mjs` does, registers them through one synthetic batch file whose rows ship, and runs the repo's own `gen/ids.mjs` in the copy. Then it runs `vite build`, with no NODE_ENV or VITEST* variables, since a test runner's environment gives a dev build. "Identical bytes" means identical after content-hashed file names (`-<8 chars>.js|css`) are made neutral. The loaders chunk's hash changes with N, and the sheet chunk names it. Both builds take about 13 s together.
- **D-LIB2-ht-tests.** Two other cards' tests pinned the globs and were edited to say the same thing about the new map, with nothing loosened. HT-2's `ids.test.ts` now counts `import('./ht-` per shipped id and refuses any other import kind, where it counted every `import(`. HT-7's `zooms.test.ts` now says that only `generated/loaders.ts` imports the posture chunks (8) and that `Posture.tsx` reads its `POSTURE`, where it said `Posture.tsx` holds the glob.
- **D-LIB2-A4-block.** Ruling D-LIB2-A4: `ht10.mjs` is untouched. LIB-2's gate block adds A25 and A26. A25 is the superset How-to total over every HowToSheet-, ht-, hand-, handpair-, feel-, posture-, zoom- and loaders- asset, within the total ceiling at the shipped N; it is red on no How-to assets and on no loaders chunk. A26 runs C17 (the shared checker, plus no `<a>`, no `target=`, and only `#` hrefs) over the loaders-* and handpair-* chunks. The count change inside HT-10 A4 goes to LIB-4 before LIB-9.
- **D-LIB2-hints-held.** Item 4 (the deduplicated hint encoding) is stopped for a ruling, as the card says to do when HT-6's A8 parser cannot read the encoding. Any encoding without id strings cannot be read by HT-6's A8 parser, which needs a `"lib_…": "<text>",` line (`scripts/screenshot-gate.mjs:6568`). Nor can such an encoding back `Object.keys(HOWTO_HINTS)` (HT-5 `content-gen.test.ts:192`, HT-6 `hint.test.ts:26`) or HT-6's pinned Train line `HOWTO_HINTS[ex.id]` (`Train.tsx:831`, `hint.test.ts:14`). It is also over 2,048 B in the worst case: measured at 153 ids with all 26 push ids hinted, the base (no hints) is 1,758 B, today's per-id lines 3,489 B, a one-character index per shipped id 2,280 B, and a 3-character index per push id 2,218 B. Details on #189. Nothing for item 4 is pushed.
- **D-LIB2-hints (rulings D-LIB2-hints and D-LIB2-hints-2, supervisor 2026-10-16; replaces D-LIB2-hints-held and the old D-LIB2-hints).** `ids.ts` uses plan 5.2's deduplicated encoding, with the plan's 4-character fallback hash:
  - **Encoding.**
    - `S` holds 4-character, 24-bit FNV-1a hashes (char code 48 + 6 bits, so no alphabet literal), with the hinted ids first.
    - `H` holds the distinct hint texts.
    - `O` holds one letter per hinted id.
    - Two functions read them: `hasHowTo(id)` and `howToHint(id) = H[O.charCodeAt(at(id)) - 65]`.
    - The encoding lives in `tools/plates/library/ids-encoding.mjs`.
  - **Writers.** `gen/ids.mjs` writes `ids.ts` and its Node twin with the hints, taken from `content.mjs`'s `pushHints()`. `content.mjs` stays `ids.ts`'s second writer and refuses a file without the hints it renders. That keeps HT-2's two-writer rule and the header the budget was measured with. `ids-node.mjs` keeps one writer.
  - **Budget.** The generator refuses an `ids.ts` over 2,048 B raw, its GENERATED header included. The message names the size, the number of distinct texts and the fix.
  - **Moved checks.** Same checks or more:
    - `Train.tsx:831` calls `howToHint(ex.id)`.
    - HT-6's `hint.test.ts`, HT-5's `content-gen.test.ts` HT5-A4 and HT-6's gate A8 now read the hints through the decoder over every `exercises.json` id. The gate reads them through the Node twin, whose literals and decoder L2-A17 proves equal to `ids.ts`'s on all 153 ids.
    - Mutations: dropping the chest press hint, or changing its text, turns HT6-A8 and HT5-A4 red, as the old checks went red on d97edeb. Dropping it also makes the gate's A8 report "no hint for the chest press".
  - **Known constraint for LB batches: 3 B of margin.** Measured at 153 shipped ids with all 26 push ids hinted:

    | Hint texts | Size | Result |
    |---|---|---|
    | 1 shared | 1,693 B | fits |
    | 12 distinct, 29 characters each | 2,045 B | fits, 3 B under the limit |
    | 13 distinct | 2,077 B | refused |
    | 26 distinct | 2,493 B | refused |

    Each extra distinct 29-character text costs 32 B. A batch that adds its own hint text must share it per archetype or shorten it, and the generator says so (L2-A9 (i) to (iii); dropping the check turns (iii) red).
  - **Side effect.** `content.mjs`'s source changed, so the 8 `ht-*.ts` header and `hashes:` lines change too. Their bodies otherwise stay the same (L2-A3).
- **D-OA1b-1 (OLD-ANDROID-1b builder, 2026-10-16).** The S3 CSS scan allows one site above the Chrome 111 floor: `grid-template-rows: subgrid` (Chrome 117) in `src/slices/howto/css/zoom-pull-up.css` `.th-cell` (HT lane). It only lines up caption heights; on Chrome 111-116 the declaration is dropped and the plate still shows. Listed in `ALLOW` in `scripts/old-android-floor.mjs` with that reason; any other site above the floor fails the gate.
- **D-A3-5b (card A3-5b, supervisor ruling R11, 2026-10-04).** Escobar's live done count is Train's card count.
  - **D-A3-5b-1, one rule.** `doneSets` (`src/slices/workout/doneSets.ts`, `countsAsDone` = `isWorkingSet`) is the exercise card's count (`Train.tsx` `logged`): filled-in working sets, typed or saved; warm-ups and blank rows never. Train imports it for the card; `get_live_session` builds both `current.setsDone` and `current.sets` from one call to it, so `setsDone: 1` with `sets: []` cannot happen. This settles the open IMP-E05 count question in D-A3-5.
  - **D-A3-5b-2, what changed.** `setsDone` already used this rule; the drift was the list, which held saved sets (`at`), including saved warm-ups, and left out typed ones. The list now holds the same sets the count counts. Train's card count is unchanged (`countsAsDone` is `isWorkingSet` itself). The header's hairline (`Train.tsx:457`, committed working sets) is a separate progress measure and stays as is.
  - **D-A3-5b-3, evidence.** `tests/escobar/a3-imp-e05-live-clock.test.ts` (IMP-E05 case 2, moved and adjusted to R11: typed, saved, mixed, and count = list at every step) and `tests/escobar/a3-5b-train-count.test.ts` (the rule's behaviour and that Train and the live read both use it). No new saved or sent data.
## D-A3-2b: pre-load reset clears every cache (A3-2b builder, 2026-10-16)

- **D-A3-2b-1, native copies.** The start-up crash screen's reset in `index.html` runs before the bundle, so it cannot import `clearExportCache`. On Android, Capacitor injects its bridge right after `<head>` (`JSInjector.java`), so `window.Capacitor.nativePromise('Filesystem', 'rmdir', { path, directory: 'CACHE', recursive: true })` reaches the same plugin call for `MARC Exports` and `MARC Share`. It runs only when `Capacitor.isNativePlatform()` is true; the web bundle's own Capacitor global reports false, so the web makes no Filesystem call.
- **D-A3-2b-2, Cache Storage.** The start-up reset also deletes every Cache Storage entry (the service worker's copy of the app). That reset follows a crash before the app mounted, which a bad cached build can cause, so the next load fetches a fresh copy. Trade-off: an offline reset shows the browser's offline page until the network returns, instead of reloading the same crashing build. The in-app resets keep Cache Storage: it holds only the app's own files, and the running app needs it offline.
- **D-A3-2b-3, no drift.** `src/native/share.ts` exports `EXPORT_CACHE_DIRS`, which `clearExportCache` uses; `tests/a3-2b-preload-cache.test.ts` fails when `index.html` lists other folders or another directory. The start-up reset reloads once the clear settles or after 2 s, as the crash card's `confirmReset` does. A failing or missing cache call never blocks the reset.
- **Supersedes** the "Residual risk" line of D-A3-2.
- **Device check (optional, owner):** export a backup on the phone, force the crash screen, tap Reset, then check that the app cache holds no `MARC Exports` folder.

## A3-7b: onboarding hold on replace (2026-10-16)

- **D-A3-7b-1, one clear point.** The store calls its replace listeners where it bumps the replace generation (IMP-N02): `replaceState` (Restore, Reset, Undo) and both branches of the other-tab listener. `selectors.ts` registers one listener that clears `heldOnboardingTrigger`. The store does not import app code, and a new replace path that bumps the generation clears the hold with no extra wiring. `App.tsx` is unchanged. The hold stays in memory only.
- **D-A3-7b-2, catch-up reads stay out.** `storageMovedOn` (A3-2, a frozen tab catching up on a deferred save) does not bump the generation today, so it does not clear the hold either. Changing that is IMP-N02's call, not this card's; the residual case needs a tab frozen with the form open while another tab writes.
- **D-OA1a (card OLD-ANDROID-1a, 2026-10-16).** No native crash at launch on Android 8.0-13.
  - **Api34 holder.** Every `android.health.connect.*` and `OutcomeReceiver` use in `HealthConnectNativePlugin` lives in the nested `static final class Api34`, so Capacitor's `getDeclaredMethods()` on the plugin at launch sees no Android 14 type. Api34 members the outer class calls, and outer members Api34 calls (`platformAvailable`, `hasHealthPermission`, `missingPermissions`, `callbackExecutor`), are package-private so D8 adds no nest bridges. Logic on API 34+ is unchanged: the moved methods take the plugin as `p` and are otherwise the same lines.
  - **Watch notification icon.** The small icon is the plain vector `drawable/ic_stat_marc` instead of the adaptive `mipmap/ic_launcher`. That an adaptive icon as a small icon crashes Android 8.0 ("Bad notification posted") could not be confirmed from sources or a device; the change is made because a status-bar icon should be a monochrome image either way.
  - **Guards.** `tests/old-android-native.test.ts` (sources) and the android-gate step "OLD-ANDROID-1 native API floor" (compiled classes of MainActivity and every registered plugin, checked against the SDK's `api-versions.xml`).

## GATE-FLAKE-3: HT-6's C11 animation reads race the animation (2026-10-16)

- **D-GF3-1 Decided** (cause and fix): main 41807999's run 38939522305 (visual-gate-tz, Pacific/Auckland) failed `HT-6: silent-black lat-pulldown C11 close animation: app [one 160 ms close] vs golden []`. C11 tapped through Playwright and then read `panel.getAnimations()` in a second round trip; a finished animation with no fill leaves that list, so on a loaded runner the golden page's 160 ms close was gone before the read. The open read (240 ms) and the reduced-motion reads had the same shape. Fix, in the harness only (`scripts/c11-anims.mjs`, used by HT-6's C11 code): before each tap the page records every animation on the panel where it is created (`Element.prototype.animate`, `Animation.prototype.play`, and the CSS `animationstart` / `transitionrun` events, one entry each, nothing filtered); the log is read once the panel is shown (open) or hidden (close), so no delay can lose an entry. The compared data and the rule (exactly one animation, equal to golden B's) are unchanged; under reduced motion "closed at once" is now read in the same task as the tap, which is stricter than the old read. `MARC_GF3_LAG` (ms) adds a delay between each tap and its read, the repro's probe mode.
- **D-GF3-2 Reproduction and proof** (`tests/gate/gf3-c11-race.mjs`, lat pulldown, Silent Black, app against golden B, needs `npm run build`): `--old` runs main's read. With no lag it is green; with `--lag 200` the close is red (`app [] vs golden []`), with `--lag 400` open and close are red, every run. The new capture with `--lag 400` is green 20 of 20 (`--runs 20`, the count asserted by the exit code). Seeded defects, each red with the new capture at `--lag 400`: close duration 200 ms, no close animation, two close animations, open keyframes `scale(.8)`, a CSS animation added to the close, and an animation under reduced motion.
- **D-GF3-3 Not fixed here** (other `getAnimations()` reads that expect an animation still present after a tap and a round trip or a fixed wait, so a loaded runner can turn them red the same way; listed for the supervisor, main 41807999 `scripts/screenshot-gate.mjs`): `:2854` (Escobar sheet-in, 320 ms, read after `waitForSelector` + 60 ms), `:2923` (esc-panel running after a click), `:3119` (rest-in, 240 ms, after 60 ms), `:3384` (pr-pop, 460 ms, after blur + 80 ms), `:2572`/`:2556` (exercise-shimmer A1 on open, 2 × 2 s, wide margin), `:7953`/`:7926` (feel-band lists read after a tap, about 5.5 s of sweep, wide margin). Reads that wait (`:50`, `:1338`, `:6707`, `:7495`, `:7832`, `:7874`, `:8111`), that look for endless or long animations (`:2013`, `:2446`, `:2496`, `:3126`, `:3142`, `:7930`, `:7931`), or that expect none (`:5222`, `:5523`, `:7994`, `:8019`) cannot turn red this way.
- **D-GATESPLIT-LIB2 Decided** (main 21d8797, train 19): LIB-2's two new blocks form one group, `LIB-2` (contiguous, one task), with two guard lines. That makes 100 groups. It was measured alone at 9.6 s locally, and its `gate-times.json` entry is 8.9 s (× 0.93, the measured local → CI ratio). The merge kept both sides; E7 against 21d8797 is empty.
- **D-GATESPLIT-FLAKES Noted** (local K = 4 pair on e9f46f2, Chromium 141): two timing probes missed once each, in split jobs, at 4× CPU, and passed on the one re-run of the same job. Both are in other tasks' blocks with unchanged bytes (E7), and both are positioned mid- to late-job, so a cold browser does not explain them.
  - BUG-34 launch first frame, UTC 2/4: offset 45.42, want ≤ 45.04 (position 17 of 25).
  - BUG-37 Settings settle, Pacific/Auckland 3/4 (position 24 of 25).
  - With the re-run proofs, `gate-compare` passes in both time zones. Both probes go to GATE-FLAKE-1 (#179). The CI E8 pairs show whether either repeats.
- **D-A3-7b-3, a listener cannot break a replace** (supervisor ruling on #229, issuecomment-6229501272). `bumpGeneration` runs each replace listener in its own try/catch and logs a failure with `console.warn`, so a throwing listener never aborts the replace (Reset, Restore, another tab) or the listeners after it. `tests/a3-7b-replace-listener-throws.test.ts` registers a throwing listener ahead of the hold's clear; without the try/catch, Reset throws and another tab's state never lands.

## BUG-41: Exercise progress line and trend use one series (2026-10-17)

- **D-BUG41-1, one series** (owner report 2026-10-17: "says improving but chart showing down. Says slipping but chart rising"). The card drew `bestE1rm || topKg || bestReps` per session over the last 12, while its tile judged `bestE1rm || volume` per session over all history; a session over 10 reps has no e1RM (E1RM_MAX_REPS), so both series mixed units from session to session and broke BUG-14's "never mixed with top load" and O4's "the same last 12". `progressSeries(history, mode)` in `src/brain/trend.ts` now picks one metric over the last `LIFT_TREND_SESSIONS` (12) sessions, and the line, the label directly under it (`progressHint(mode, metric)`), the trend tile, the readout, the effort bars and Escobar's `liftTrend` all read it. Picking: bodyweight best reps; duration longest hold; assisted keeps `liftTrend`'s decision and plots what it judged (least help negated, AUD-8, or best reps when help is flat); conditioning the farthest distance when any of the 12 has one (D-AUD11-4); weighted e1RM when every session has one, else top load over the sessions with a load, with volume deciding only upward when top load is neither up nor down (BR-04's tie rule), else best reps. Each metric keeps only values above 0 (assist aside, where 0 help is real), and the trend is `trend()` of exactly the plotted points. The line is drawn in the user's unit. This replaces QA2-FC-1's per-session mix and keeps its intent: more reps at the same load reads Improving (`tests/progress-trend.test.ts`, unchanged). No threshold changes. Tests: `tests/bug-41-progress-trend.test.ts` A1-A7, A10; gate block BUG-41.
- **D-BUG41-2, known costs** (pinned in `tests/bug-41-progress-trend.test.ts` A9). C1: ten weeks of 100×8, then 105×3, then 50×15 reads top load 'down' (the light day counts at its load). C2: eleven rising 5-rep weeks, then 60×12 reads volume 'up', since top load is not up or down and volume decides upward; its last point (2160) is the highest. C3: 20 kg with reps falling 15 to 12 reads top load 'flat' (main read 'down'), as BR-04 reads it 'plateaued'. Not BR-04's E1RM_MIN_SESSIONS pick: it drops a latest session over 10 reps, so the owner's lateral line would end on 17 Oct (10 to 9.3) while the header shows 18 Oct. Lighter-week sessions stay on the card while Escobar's evidence drops them (BUG-15).
- **D-BUG41-3, not fixed here** (for the supervisor to card): the chart points of Escobar's `show.ts` lift_trend still use `bestE1rm || topKg` per session; the assisted line's y labels are negative numbers (the least help negated).
- **D-GATESPLIT-OA1 Decided** (main 49a2853, train 20): the new top-level `OLD-ANDROID-1` block gets its one guard line (key `OLD-ANDROID-1`), making 101 groups. It was measured alone at 17.4 s locally, and its `gate-times.json` entry is 16.2 s (× 0.93). E7 against 49a2853 is empty.

## LIB-4b: per-id library runner (2026-10-17)

- **D-LIB4b-FIXTURE Blocked, waiting for a supervisor ruling** (D-LIB4-FIXTURE day-1 check, on main c14212af + #189 54d37f2d + #180 4540d827). Both questions are answered no, checked by running the repo generator, not by reading:
  1. *Can LIB-2's generator emit a library plate chunk from a spec and How-to source?* No. A scratch batch row (`lib_cable_lateral_raise`, mode H, stage `shipped`, `src: library/specs/cable_lateral_raise.mjs`) with the pull-up spec copied to `tools/plates/library/specs/` and its golden-B How-to copied to `tools/plates/library/howto/lib_cable_lateral_raise.howto.mjs`: `node tools/plates/generate.mjs` wrote only `ids.ts`, `loaders.ts`, `coverage.ts` and `ids-node.mjs` changes. No `ht-cable-lateral-raise.ts`, and no feel-, hand-, posture- or zoom- file. `loaders.ts` then imports `./ht-cable-lateral-raise`, which does not exist, and `vite build` fails ("Could not resolve"). Cause: `gen/plates.mjs`, `gen/content.mjs` (`rowsOf = () => goldenRows()`), `gen/feel.mjs` and `gen/hands.mjs` read `goldenRows()` (plates.json's 8) only; only `gen/ids.mjs` and `gen/handpairs.mjs` read library rows. LIB-2-DESIGN.md line 42 says the layer generators read a library id's source; the code on 54d37f2d does not.
  2. *Do its per-output inputs list shared composers?* No. For the 8's `ht-pull-up.ts` the union of `plates.mjs` and `content.mjs` `inputsFor` lists `layers/howto/shared.mjs` and `layers/exercises/pull_up.howto.mjs`, but not what that How-to imports: `layers/exercises/pull_up.mjs`, `layers/engine/hand.mjs`, `layers/engine/body.mjs`. For library rows no per-output list exists, since no output is emitted.
  - Effect: B5 and LIB-4c C11-C14 have no source-level scratch ids to run on, and LIB-9 cannot ship a library id. Per the card, LIB-4b stops before bulk build until the supervisor rules.
- **D-LIB4b-FIXTURE-2 Ruled by the supervisor** (D-LIB4-FIXTURE-2, #232 issuecomment-6228337559; lib4-cards.md @ af874cc, section 1 and card LIB-2b in section 10): (a) a new card LIB-2b (LIB-2 builder, branch `claude/lib-2b-library-rows`) adds library-row emission to the layer generators and composer-closure per-output inputs; LIB-2 merges without it. (b) LIB-4b builds the tuples, runner, twins, B1-B4, B6-B10 and the D-LIB4-PAR measurement on the 8 now; B5 waits for LIB-2b's READY head, merged here with a merge commit. LIB-4b's READY needs B5.
- **D-LIB4b-RULINGS Applied** (supervisor rulings on the design note, lib4-cards.md section 11 @ f20a0b4; 11.1 wins over 11.3). As built on this branch:
  - **D-LIB4b-KEYS:** `plateKeys` reads `ht-<slug>.ts` through esbuild, recomputes the `cues` fragment and requires it to equal the pinned entry (GOLDEN.json for the 8; for a library id the one `tests/howto/golden/library/*.json` file that holds it, every file through `verifyBatchChain`); `cues[0].key === firstKey`; else it throws, never returns fewer tuples. Theme `*` exactly when width `*` on per-id rows (`libraryTuples` and `rowProblems` assert it).
  - **D-LIB4b-SIZE:** `L2/chunk-size@*`, `HT7/A5-size@*`, `HT8/size@*` per id from `www/assets`, with HT-2 A9's limits, HT-7 A5's 24 KB gz (level 9) and the budgets.json entry; none allowed when the source has none.
  - **D-LIB4b-X3:** `entry/open@390` also asserts HT3-A3's S0 facts (title = the exercises.json name, eyebrow, one pressed callout = the module's firstKey, no Mistake figure, Mistake off, ≤ 700 elements).
  - **D-LIB4b-COUNTS:** `HT7/A2@390` per theme and `HT7/A2:reduced@390` (paper). The 8 now have 2,947 tuples, not the ruling's 2,979: HT-6 A6 and HT-9 A5 are one row per (id, theme) (`HT6/A6`, `HT9/A5`, each covering N, M, T@2400 and R:end, 4 pairs, so 16 × 4 = 64 = the blocks' printed pairs), not two, which the note's 2,907 still counted. 2,907 − 32 + 24 + 48 = 2,947.

X3b: `ht3Behaviour`'s A1 negatives (no library-id subject; HT-3 and the LIB-4a block every run). X9a: `runHt10` `golden` C10/C18 exemption checks; library ids inherit no exemption, and a named one is checked on that id's own page (the row stays red until then). X9b: `runHt10` `fixtures`, guards of the imported `ht10Sweep`/`ht10DomProbe`, never copied (B3's pin proves it). X9c: HT-10's own-job time budget, LIB-4c's 1,800 s verdict for the library. HowToSheet budgets in HT-3b A2: X4. G7 is a per-id check in `entry/open`. 2,947 tuples on the 8 accepted. The map test now fails on any entry left as a gap.

## LIB-2b: library rows in the layer generators (LIB-2b builder, 2026-10-17)

Card: `docs/supervisor/verify/lib4-cards.md` section 10; rulings section 11 (11.1 wins). Design: LIB-2-DESIGN.md 13.1.
- **D-LIB2b-PAGES.** Layers pages are committed (`tests/howto/golden/library/<batch>.layers.html`) and pinned by one `layers-page` entry in the batch golden file; plates pages are rebuilt at generate time and pinned by a `page` entry. The generator checks the pin before any cut (`tools/plates/library/batch-golden.mjs`). `layersConfig` holds LIB-6's spec as data. A vitest guard keeps the folder to the batch golden files and their pinned pages. `approvedBy` stays `'owner'` until the stamps wording is ruled.
- **D-LIB2b-FEEL.** A library feel chunk is cut from its pinned layers page with `feelSectionOf`, after the pin check. The 8 stay on `renderAll()`. A test runs the HT8-A1 checks on every shipped library row (rest map, each row's marks, legend, no playing state).
- **D-LIB2b-FIXTURE.** The E1 row `lib_cable_lateral_raise` is golden B's pull-up spec and How-to under one deterministic transform (`tests/howto/fixtures/lib2b/fixture.mjs`): the id is swapped, every relative import is re-pointed at its original target, and research.card names the scratch QA card. Its layers page was built by LIB-6 (#181 9b8f76e6) through `layersSpecOf` on a scratch worktree, from two checkouts, byte-equal; `provenance.json` records the command and shas. Where LIB-6's builder exists the test rebuilds the page and compares; where it does not, `render/` must be absent too.
- **D-LIB2b-LAYOUT.** Library specs and How-to sources keep in-repo relative imports. `layersSpecOf` stages each row's whole data closure into LIB-6's mirror, maps layers files to their mirror paths and everything else under `library/`, and throws on a specifier that leaves the mirror.
- **D-LIB2b-PINS.** The plugin-output and file-count tests D-LIB4-PINS-2 assigns here take their counts from `tests/howto/shipped-rows.ts` (the registry's shipped rows, floor: the 8 first). Each keeps its matcher; the 8's subtotals stay exact. `hands.test.ts` is not edited.
- **D-LIB2b-CSS.** The four shared CSS files stay built from the 8's pages. Each library page must rebuild them byte for byte. Its style, without comments, blank lines and its own `.hx-` rules, must equal golden B's, and it must hold ZDOTS once.
- **D-LIB2b-LOADERS.** `gen/ids.mjs` runs after `plates.mjs` and throws (`missingChunks`) before writing any line, unless every shipped row has its chunks. `hands.mjs` writes only `zoom-<chromeId>.css` for a library row; it throws when a non-`none` row has no pair, or a `none` row's page has a hand close-up.
- **D-LIB2b-CLOSURE.** `closureOf` builds the graph from esbuild's metafile. It throws on a computed or template `import()`, `require`, a bare specifier, a file read outside the MANIFEST-locked trees, and an unresolved import. A locked tree's MANIFEST.json joins the inputs. Template literals are found in the source text, because esbuild prints a constant template as a plain string.
- **D-LIB2b-QA.** `tools/plates/library/candidate.mjs` `candidateOf(id, { root, platesHtml, engine })` is the one QA candidate builder. The generator gates the node half and ignores only `browser:not-run` in PQ-H1/H4/H5 and F2/F5 "browser checks not run".
  - mistakeFault is the plate's selected mistake tell (its first tell key). This is the one source that names it; LIB-3's mutations use the same key.
  - research.card is a repo-relative path. A missing card gives research null, so runQa refuses with PQ-H6.
  - The batch records its census source in `censusRef`.
  - Measured on the fixture: 1.0 to 1.4 s per id. Over 145 ids that is about 3.4 min per generate run. At 9 runs per job it is about 29.7 min at the slower figure: under the 30 min line, but close. This is reported for the supervisor's ruling before LIB-9.
- **D-LIB2b-CARDID.** A library spec is keyed in both page builders by its chromeId with underscores (`plates-page.mjs` `cardIdOf`). So its card is `card-<chromeId>` and its svg ids sit under `<chromeId>-`, as for the 8. `pageConfig` keyed it by lib id before (`card-lib-…`, which `extractPlates` and the registry could never match), a LIB-2 gap no test reached.
- **D-LIB2b-REGISTRY.** `pages`, `view` and `censusRef` are checked by the registry when present. The generator and `candidateOf` require them for a shipped library row. They are not required in every registry read, because the synthetic scale build (`scale/synthetic.mjs`) registers shipped rows that have only generated modules. For the same reason `ids.mjs`, when called without the core's ctx (measurement only), reads hints only from ids that have a source and lists no inputs. The generator always passes ctx, and there `content.mjs` loads every shipped source or throws.
- **D-LIB2b-CYCLE.** `gen/plates.mjs` imports the QA modules lazily. `qa/node.mjs` imports `plates.mjs`'s `moduleText`, and a static import back made a cycle that hung vitest's parallel module loads (library-qa.test.ts timed out at 180 s).
- **D-A3-7b-3, a listener cannot break a replace** (supervisor ruling on #229, issuecomment-6229501272). `bumpGeneration` runs each replace listener in its own try/catch and logs a failure with `console.warn`, so a throwing listener never aborts the replace (Reset, Restore, another tab) or the listeners after it. `tests/a3-7b-replace-listener-throws.test.ts` registers a throwing listener ahead of the hold's clear; without the try/catch, Reset throws and another tab's state never lands.
- **D-LIB2b-REVIEW-1, review fixes before merge** (supervisor ruling on #234, comment 6231953220). (1) The HT8-A1 feel checks and the HT2-A1 + class-map checks in `library-rows.test.ts` run over the repo's own shipped library rows (`shipped-rows.ts` LIBRARY) and then the E1 tree's row, which is asserted present, so a real batch is checked once it ships; each row's committed feel- and ht- files are read from disk. A mark moved to another muscle in a How-to source, without a page rebuild, is red (a permanent failure-path test on a tree copy). (2) `zooms.test.ts`'s library crop count takes `want` from the How-to source's posture zooms, not from the same golden list it counts. (3) `ids.mjs` `missingChunks` also throws, naming the id and the file, unless `src/slices/howto/css/zoom-<chromeId>.css` is an output (through ctx.prev, or the file on disk when called without ctx); the synthetic scale clones copy that file, so they still build.
- **D-LIB2b-HANDCSS, a library hand loads its close-up CSS** (supervisor ruling on #234, comment 6237039950; lib4-cards.md 11.1 item 18). A library row's hand ships only as the shared `handpair-<key>.ts`, which stays exercise-neutral and imports no CSS, so its `zoom-<chromeId>.css` used to arrive only with its `posture-` chunk, and a hand close-up opened first was unstyled. `ids.mjs` now writes a pair's HAND line as `import('../../slices/howto/css/zoom-<chromeId>.css').then(() => import('./handpair-<key>'))`. Vite builds that as a preload of the built css before the pair chunk loads, the same dependency the 8's `hand-<chromeId>` chunks carry, so no new JS chunk appears (the D-LIB4-A4 and A9 sets are unchanged). The 8's lines are byte-identical: on main only the header hash moved. `library-rows.test.ts` D-LIB2b-HANDCSS checks every shipped library row's line (the repo's LIBRARY rows and the E1 tree's), and the E1 build checks the built loaders chunk against the vite manifest. L2-A21's pair-line matcher now requires the css-first form.

## D-BUG39: the streak follows weeks on plan (BUG-39 builder, 2026-10-17)

- **Why.** The owner reported the flame dropping from 9 to 0 with no skipped workout. `trainingStreak` stopped at the first past scheduled weekday with no session on that date, so training the planned count on other weekdays zeroed it (audit DATES-F7), against COACHING-PLAN :217 and :647 (never reset to zero for one miss; one free miss per 4 weeks).
- **R1, unit.** The number stays a count of distinct days up to today with at least one working set (`hasWorkingSets`).
- **R2, target.** A week's target is `plannedSessions({ schedule, daysOff, plannedDays }, <its Monday>)`, the same target as Today's week grade. When that is null (no weekday scheduled, no `plannedDays`), the old consecutive-day rule runs unchanged.
- **R3, walk.** Mon-Sun weeks back from today's week. Today's week and the week of the first working session are never judged: their days count and the walk ends at the first week. Any other week: short = max(0, target − sessions with working sets). Short 0 counts its days; short 1 is forgiven when no week within the 3 newer weeks was forgiven, else it stops; short 2 or more stops. A week that stops adds nothing.
- **R4, limits.** Days off lower the target. Past weeks are judged against the current schedule (same known limit as D-BUG38). Splits are not read. No new saved data. `selectors.ts` (`streak`) and `get_overview.streak` both pass `profile.plannedDays`.
- **Re-pins (supervisor ruling under D-BUG39).** The old values encoded the replaced rule: `tests/balance-weekly.test.ts:37` 0 → 2 (one missed Friday is forgiven); `tests/r6-features.test.ts:37` 1 → 4 (the missed Monday is in today's week; the new test's AC5, 0 without the day off and 5 with it, keeps that test's day-off intent).
- **Evidence.** `tests/bug-39-streak.test.ts` (AC1-AC8; AC1 is the owner's calendar: 9 on 15 Oct and 11 on 18 Oct under four schedules, 0 and 2 on main 49a2853).

Mutations checked red: chip back to `records.length`, the button's `onClick` as a no-op, the button always rendered, and `slice(0, 12)` kept after Show all.
