## GATE-FLAKE-4: BUG-34's launch probe races its captures (2026-10-18)

Card: `docs/supervisor/verify/gate-flake-4-card.md` (claude/sup-lib4-cards), Ruling D-GF4-R.
- **D-GF4-1 Decided** (cause and fix): train 25 head bdd309be, "gate-shard (4/4, Pacific/Auckland)", failed `silent-black 4x: at 740 ms strokeDashoffset is 45.8355, want at least 10 below 46.6814`. A2 compared states read after separate CDP captures and round trips, with nothing bounding how late s2 landed; the line's curve is flat around 720 ms, so an s2 about 360 ms late (CPU throttling slows the captures, not the clock) left less than 10 units to s3. Three more races share the root: the dot check after a first capture 620 ms or more late, the word counted as stray ink when the second capture lands after 1050 ms, and the 1500 ms bundle hold timed from the request while the samples are timed from the overlay script. Not an app defect: under 8 busy processes every recorded frame matched the designed curve within 0.084 units. Fix, BUG-34's block only, lines E1-E11 of the Ruling:
  - E5: the bundle is held until the context closes. The same condition as the 1500 ms hold, held longer, so race 3 cannot happen.
  - E6, E7, E8, E10: an in-page rAF recorder logs every frame's time, offset, dot and booted from the first frame with the line and dot. A2 reads f0 (the frame at FCP), fA (first at FCP + 100 ms) and fB (first at FCP + 700 ms). No capture or round trip can move these times. The drop and dot checks compare f0 with fA, as main did with s1/s2. The 10-unit rule compares fB with every frame up to FCP + 300 ms, and a new dot line asks fB's dot to be more than 0.1 units from every such frame. Both windows only make the checks stricter: main's s2 landed 240-320 ms on a quiet machine, and its dot check caught a glide that stops in the first frames, which f0 against fA alone would not (seed dot-freeze-40). Load can only make fA and fB later and the early frames fewer, which widens both gaps.
  - E2, E3, E4, E9: once the word's own `launch-reveal` animation has currentTime >= 1050 ms, A1 skips pixels inside its box (12 px wider on each side). A guard asks the box's top to be 54.6 mark units or more (the path's lowest point 50, plus the dot's radius 3.1, plus 1.5), so the skip never covers the line, the dot, or the SMIL build's cap and dot.
  - E1, E11: the comment and the log line. The log adds the smallest A2 margin and the smallest word top, so CI shows both. The two values are kept as `timings.margin` and `timings.top`, set only in E9 and E10 and read only in E11 (supervisor ruling 6245795450 on #253).
  - Every other number stays: 100 ms, 700 ms, 10 units, 0.1 units, 0.6 units, 50 pixels, 24/255, 3.1 + 1.5, `< 100`, the 8000 ms waits, and A3.
- **D-GF4-2 Reproduction and proof** (`tests/gate/gf4-bug34-race.mjs`, needs `npm run build`; Chromium 141.0.7390.37, 4 cores). It runs the block's own bytes; `--old` runs main's slice (sha256 d46e95eb…).
  - S1, main's read is red under capture delay: `--old --lag 200 --lag-on 2 --runs 20 --expect red` 20 of 20 red; `--old --lag 350 --lag-on 2 --trace` red in all 10 contexts in CI's shape (`silent-black 1x: at 745 ms strokeDashoffset is 45.836, want at least 10 below 46.9685`, s2 at 611 ms); `--old --t0-lag 1000 --runs 3 --expect red` red (`the app booted before the samples`).
  - S2, the new block is green under delay, all 5 themes at 1x and 4x: plain `--runs 20`, `--lag 800 --lag-on 2 --runs 20`, `--lag 800 --lag-on 1 --runs 20`, `--lag 1200 --lag-on all --runs 5`, `--t0-lag 1000 --runs 5`, each exit 0. Largest 4x frame gap 100.0 ms.
  - S3, real load (8 × `node -e 'for(;;){}'` on 4 cores): new block `--runs 10` 10 of 10 green (margins 10.62-10.64). Main under the same load: red 0 of 4 this time (margins 8.58-16.74; it was red 3 of 4 when the card was written). Main's result under load is not stable; the new block's is.
  - S4, seeds (`--theme silent-black --runs 3 --expect red`, new block with no lag, `--lag 800 --lag-on 2` and `--lag 350 --lag-on 1`, and `--old` with no lag): never, slow-5x, stall-500, freeze-150, early-freeze, late-600, dot-origin, word-early, creep and dot-freeze-40 are red 3 of 3 in all 40 commands. creep on `--old` was red 3 of 3 here (1 of 3 green when the card was written).
  - S5, margin medians over 20 plain runs: new block 10.62, main 16.22 (new ≤ main). E9's guard on `--lag 1200 --lag-on all`: smallest word top 55.46 at first paint and 55.11 at +100 ms (Inter).
  - Mutations, each a scratch copy run with `--gate`: M1 (+300 cut to +100) creep green 3 of 3, exit 1; M2 (no currentTime condition) word-early green 3 of 3, exit 1; M3 (box 14 px) guard red in all 10 contexts at 54.42; M4 (route `.jsx$`) red with `the app booted before the samples` in every context; M5 (10-unit rule against fA only) median margin 39.76, above main's 16.22; M6 (first-paint for FCP) `--t0-lag 1000 --runs 6` 3 of 6 red in 4x contexts naming frames 1005-1020 ms before __marcLaunchT0, exit 1; M7 (no dot line) dot-freeze-40 green 3 of 3, exit 1.
  - gate-times.json: BUG-34 alone (`MARC_GATE_JOB=45/103`, 3 runs each), before (main b99a9e25) median 10.0 s UTC and 9.9 s Pacific/Auckland, after 9.9 s and 9.9 s. The larger after median (9.9) is within 10% of the larger before median (10.0), so `"BUG-34": 8.7` stays.
- **D-GF4-3 Not fixed here** (for the supervisor, main b99a9e25 `scripts/screenshot-gate.mjs`):
  - `:8167` HT-8 shimmer tripwire: TaskDuration over fixed `waitForTimeout(TOTAL+1000)` windows, bracketed by two CDP round trips with no bound on the time between them (the median of 5 interleaved rounds softens it).
  - `:8498` BUG-36, `:8624` and `:8641` BUG-37, `:8944` COPY-2: each starts an in-page rAF recorder (900 ms from its own start), then clicks through Playwright round trips; nothing bounds how late the click lands inside the 900 ms window, so a late click leaves fewer frames after it.
  - A1's stray check still reads its state after the capture, as on main. A late read can only widen `reach`; it cannot hide the dot at (0, 0) (dot-origin stays red at every lag).
  - E9's word skip: a capture taken just before 1050 ms whose state is read just after it skips the word's box where main still counted it. This is the only case where A1 counts less than main.

## LIB-3b: PQ-H10 checks values and the envelope on the real path (LIB-3b builder, 2026-10-18)

Card: `docs/supervisor/verify/lib-3b-card.md` (claude/sup-lib4-cards @ 5551cadd, amendments 1 and 2). PR #258.
- **D-LIB3b-H10 Decided**: PQ-H10 applies derive.mjs's own `PARAMS` and `paramProblems` against the envelope that runQa holds (`h10(c, env)` at qa/index.mjs:38), for each key that validateParams did not already flag. It never reads the candidate's schema. Empty params give `H10.empty`; a PARAMS key the envelope does not name, or a malformed envelope row, gives `H10.envelope:<key>`; an inherited name (constructor, toString, __proto__) gives `H10.unknown:<key>`. envelope.json names no PARAMS key, so every D/T param stays red until a supervisor ruling gives PARAMS ranges a home.
- **D-LIB3b-H7 Decided** (supervisor ruling on #258 comment 6249576171, option 1): the B5(b) gap from #232 is real, and the fix moved to LIB-3c. Reproduced on a scratch mirror (golden `makeMirror()`, `vendor/exercises/seated_cable_row.mjs` pulley `r: 0.045` edited, `candidateOf('lib_seated_cable_row')` then `runQa`; nothing committed):

  | Edit | Two in-process renders | Fresh import vs first render | PQ-H7 |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | none | same | n/a | `[]` |
  | per call: `r: 0.045 + Math.random() * 0.02` inside `equipment()` | differ | n/a | `H7.determinism:normal.svg`, `H7.determinism:mistake.svg` |
  | import time: `const RR = 0.045 + Math.random() * 0.02` at module top, `r: RR` | same | differ | `[]` (missed) |
  | the per-call edit in `tools/plates/layers/exercises/seated_cable_row.mjs` (not vendor) | same | n/a | `[]`: QA renders the vendor copy (golden.mjs makeMirror copies `VENDOR`) |

  A render that throws leaves R null, so the determinism loop is skipped, but PQ-H1 is red (`H1.engine:render-threw`), so it fails closed. A fresh-import H7 needs an async h7 at index.mjs:38 and a spec loader for H/D/T rows in candidate.mjs, which is reserved in this card and collides with #245. Moved to LIB-3c ("PQ-H7 builds twice from fresh spec imports"). node.mjs's H7 lines are unchanged here.

## LIB-7-FIX loader contract on #193 (2026-10-18)
Card LIB-7-FIX (lib-7-fix-card.md, lib4-cards section 18 D-LIB7FIX-*). Builder decisions inside the rulings:
- **D-LIB7FIX-B1, panel source.** `pairPanel(key)` draws the key's first id (module order) with the uid `hp-<key>-<fault>`; F2 proves the panel is byte-identical through every id on the key, and F4 that each SVG is the approved render with only the uid swapped (22 of 22 pages).
- **D-LIB7FIX-B2, markup without golden B's helpers.** golden B's `zoomPanels`, `cleanZoom` and `prefixIds` are module-private (`artifact/howto-layers.mjs`), and `tools/plates/layers/**` is never edited, so `hands/panel.mjs` writes the same wrapper (`howto-layers.mjs:162-171`) and the cleaned pager (:148-153) itself, reusing LIB-6's `captions()` and `pagerOf()`. `splitHalves` is not applied: it rewrites SVG bytes, which D-LIB7FIX-CARRY holds to the approved render (hands/DESIGN.md §8 (b)).
- **D-LIB7FIX-B3, one more loader throw.** Besides the ruled throws, `keyIndexOf` refuses a drawn id that is one of the 8 (`tools/plates/plates.json`), so HAND_OF_ID can never map one; F1 shows it red. Stricter only.
- **D-LIB7FIX-B4, words.** Captions are the key's label only (D-LIB7FIX-WORDS): Right = the approved Right note, Wrong and pager = the D-LIB7-10 fault label, heading = golden B's "Hand: right and wrong"; golden B's close label and LIB-6's sr-only and pager words are recorded as `chrome` in `hands/panel-words.json` with their sources.
- **D-LIB7FIX-B5, inputs.** `inputsFor` (key or id) also lists `hands/panel-words.json` (read with readFileSync, so not in the import closure); `panel.mjs` and golden B's `copy-lint.mjs` enter through `pairs.mjs`'s closure. The id path is otherwise unchanged (hand-pairs.test.ts:514-524 and LIB-12's hand-views.test.ts:211, :217 stay unedited).
- **D-LIB7FIX-B6, gate time.** `scripts/gate-times.json` "LIB-7": 44.4 s: the group alone (`MARC_GATE_JOB=11/104`) took 47.7 s in UTC and 47.1 s in Pacific/Auckland locally (proof `meta.seconds`), the larger × 0.93 (GATE-SPLIT's local → CI ratio), as 11.1 item 16 (b) says.
- **D-LIB7FIX-PERKEY (supervisor amendment, lib4-cards section 19; built on #193).** Adding a hand key never edits a file a pinned row lists. panel-words.json is split into `hands/words/chrome.json` and one `hands/words/<key>.json` per key, with every word's text and source unchanged (PK1: the 8 panels are byte-equal to panels built from da65b13a's words, and their sha256 on both heads are in #193's body). Key modules may declare their own conventions, orientations and camera words, merged by `declarationsOf` with throws on clashes and foreign use (PK3). `inputsFor(key)` lists the shared engine plus that key's own files only (PK2), and a scratch worktree with a new key module and words file leaves every existing key's inputs, file bytes and pairs.mjs's esbuild closure unchanged (PK4). Builder note: PK4's dummy id is `box_jump` (in no hand module, outside LB1/LB2, not pinned); PK4 (c) runs once LIB-4c's ci/manifest.mjs is on the head.
## LIB-3c: fresh-import PQ-H7, PARAMS ranges and the D/T child proof (LIB-3c builder, 2026-10-18)

Card: `docs/supervisor/verify/lib-3c-card.md` (claude/sup-lib4-cards @ 95928e1). PR #264. Design: `docs/howto/library/LIB-3c-DESIGN.md` (accepted by the supervisor on #264).
- **D-LIB3c-H7 Decided**: PQ-H7 (qa/node.mjs h7, now async; index.mjs:38 awaits it) keeps its second in-process render and adds a render set from a fresh import of the spec ('approved': `ctx.engine.spec`; H, D, T: the candidate's `freshSpec`, `row.src?h7=n`). A difference gives `H7.determinism:<k>.<f>`, not repeated when the in-process check already gave it. A fresh import re-evaluates the spec module only, so a value fixed at import time in a module the spec imports (a composer) is not caught by it; for that **h7Builds is wired, not removed**: for H, D and T rows it builds the row's plate alone twice through buildPlatesPage, each build in its own process (`H7.determinism:build`). An H/D/T engine candidate without `freshSpec` or `rebuild`, or an 'approved' one whose engine has no `spec`, gives `H7.determinism:fresh-missing`; a fresh import, render or build that throws gives `H7.determinism:fresh-threw`. The 8 get no build pair: their vendored tree is hash-locked (build-page.mjs:84-85) and GOLDEN-pinned. Why wired: it closes the composer case in-process for about 1 s per library row; L1 (LIB-4c) still rebuilds shipped bytes in a fresh CI process.
- **D-LIB10-RANGES built**: `tools/plates/library/qa/param-ranges.json` (`{ $comment, families: { <family>: { ruling, params: { <PARAMS key>: { range, basis } } } } }`, committed with `families: {}`; no family row is ruled) and its loader `qa/param-ranges.mjs` (read on every call; a missing file has no family; throws on a missing or malformed ruling id, a basis not one non-empty string per bound or value, a key outside PARAMS, a range node.mjs's rowOk calls malformed, or an unknown field). The family is a T row's `template` or `D:<parent id>`. h10 builds D-LIB3c-SHARED's one object (`familyObject`: the family's range, else ctx.envelope's row) and passes it to both `paramEnvelopeProblems` (byte-identical) and the child rebuild; only h10's D/T branch loads the loader. candidate.mjs refuses a T row whose `template` is not `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$/` naming an existing `templates/<name>.mjs`. rowOk is exported (no logic change) so the loader and PQ-H10 share one "malformed" rule.
- **D-LIB10-CHILDPROOF built**: for a D or T row whose params have no H10 problem, PQ-H10 compares the batch page plate with the child rebuilt alone (D: `derive(parent, row.params, obj)`, the parent of the 8 imported from a mirror; T: `make(row.params, facts)`), on all 9 fragments: `H10.child:<fragment>`; a rebuild that throws or a derivation without `rebuildChild` gives `H10.child:threw`. For T, the parent is also rebuilt through `make(parentParams, parentFacts)` against its pinned fragments (`H10.byte:<k>`; a throw gives `H10.child:threw`; no `rebuildTemplateParent` gives `H10:missing`). Rebuild modules are written under os.tmpdir() and removed in `finally`. node.mjs:298's early return, validateParams and paramEnvelopeProblems are unchanged; library-qa-h10.test.ts passes unedited.
## LIB-4c: library CI (LIB-4c builder, 2026-10-17)

Card LIB-4c (docs/supervisor/verify/lib-4c-card.md and lib4-cards.md on claude/sup-lib4-cards). Decisions as built:
- **D-LIB4c-2 (builder, accepted by the supervisor at the Do 0 check-in).** `ci/affected.mjs` fails safe past the card's text: a class-2 file (ids.ts, lib-id.ts, coverage.ts, index.ts, loaders.ts, ids-node.mjs) that changed while no batch row differs selects every library id, not none; a batch file or batch golden file whose diff names no row selects that batch's ids. Test: library-ci.test C4 "ids.ts selects the changed rows (all when no row differs)".
- **D-LIB4c-3 (supervisor ruling, option A).** Class 3 stays literal: a `tools/plates/**` path listed by any output not owned by one id (ids.ts, ids-node.mjs, sources.json, a `docs/research/howto/<id>.json`, any of the 8's files) selects all ids. Every spec and How-to closure file is listed by ids.ts, ids-node.mjs and sources.json (content.mjs's plugin-wide inputs), so a composer change selects all. The single-id branch is reached by a batch's `pages.layersConfig` (listed only by that batch's feel-, posture- and zoom- files) and its plates page config (only its ht- files). Test: library-ci.test C4 class 3 "D-LIB4c-3".
- **D-LIB4c-4 (builder; told to the supervisor).** planK: a shard whose fixed part (c0 plus its once-states) is already over 900 s fits only when it holds no id. Measured locally on the scratch tree (6 ids, #181 merged): the once-states of shard 1 took 1,267.7 s, so no K could fit shard 1 under the card's literal rule and the fallback (one id per shard) would have put 1,290 + 975 s on shard 1, over the 1,800 s red line. Shard 1 now runs only the once-states (a note says so); every shard with ids still fits 900 s. Test: library-ci.test C2.
- **D-LIB4c-4, ruling (supervisor, 2026-10-17 21:1x UTC): accepted as a planning rule.** It changes only where planK places ids; the 1,800 s red line applies to every shard, the once-states-only one included, and over 900 s still notes "re-pick K/N". The plan note names the shard and its fixed part; the verdict's notes and its `library:` line also give the seconds that shard used. C14 records which branch CI takes.
- **D-LIB4c-ONCESEED (supervisor ruling on #246, 2026-10-18 12:08 UTC).** times.json `once` is seeded from C11's CI log (run 39123273382, Chrome 153): shard 1 printed `once-states 6 rows in 985.0 s`, shard 2 `2 rows in 13.9 s`, so `once.run` = 985.0 - 13.9 = 971.1 and `once.shard` = 13.9; `default` and `c0` are unchanged. With the 0/0 seed planK put 3 ids on shard 1 and that job hit the 30-minute limit (no proof). Do 7 (`ci/times.mjs`) replaces both values from C14's full runs.
- **D-LIB4c-4, the branch CI takes (C11).** On CI the once-states alone are over 900 s (c0 30 + 985 s on shard 1), so the D-LIB4c-4 branch is the one in force: shard 1 holds no id; the 6 scratch ids plan to K = 3 with 3 ids on each of shards 2 and 3. C14 confirms it with full runs.
- **D-LIB4c-5 (builder): times.json per-id seconds are wall-time shares.** The runner's `meta.seconds` per id sums that id's job durations; at P = 4 the jobs overlap, so on C14 run 1 (39127805072) shard 2 reported 1,054 / 1,065 / 1,039 s for 3 ids in an 847.6 s shard. Read raw, times.json would plan each id alone (over 900 s) and K would jump from 3 to 7. `ci/times.mjs` now gives each id its share of its shard's wall time after the once-states (`(shardSeconds - onceSeconds) x s / sum s`), so the planned seconds of a shard add up to what it used; a proof without `shardSeconds` keeps the raw sum. Test: library-ci.test 'D-LIB4c-5' (red on the old code).
- **D-LIB4c-P1 (supervisor ruling on #246 6250336861; builder detail).** LIB-4b withdrew P = 4 (#232 7679566, DEFAULT_P = 1), so howto-shard runs at P = 1 again (`LIB_P = 1`; the 'adopted P' test keeps it equal to DEFAULT_P) and the P = 4 times.json from C14's two dispatches is reverted (those runs do not count). (a) The `once` seed does not depend on P: `onceStates` runs its rows one after another and takes no P; it stays 971.1 / 13.9. (b) At P = 1 an id costs about its P = 4 job-seconds, so times.json `ids` is seeded with them (C14 run 39127805072: 956.7-1,064.7 s; locally LIB-4b's B2 gave 7,522 s for the 8, ~940 s per id) instead of `default` 280.8, which would have put 3 ids (~3,000 s) on a shard. C14 at P = 1 replaces them. (c) With every id over 900 s alone, planK's fallback 'one id per shard' now adds a shard so that a shard 1 already over 900 s holds none (before: K = 6 for 6 ids, two ids on the last shard, 1,963 s, over the 1,800 s line). Test: library-ci.test C2 (red on the old code). Plan at P = 1 with this seed: 6 ids K = 7; N = 4, 14, 145, 153 give K = 5, 15, 146, 154, every shard 1,015-1,109 s.
- **D-LIB4c-C0 (supervisor correction on #246 6251681301; builder measurement).** c0 is the in-shard fixed overhead that planK charges per shard (`c0 + offset + sum planned <= 900`), not job setup (40-52 s, outside shardSeconds). Measured on C14's 12 id shards at P = 1 as `shardSeconds - onceSeconds - sum meta.seconds`: run 1 1.0-2.9 s, run 2 1.4-2.8 s, so c0 = 2.9 (the larger run's maximum; against once.shard instead of each shard's own once it is -4.0-2.2 s). `ci/times.mjs` now leaves c0 out of each id's share, so c0 is not counted twice. The K table does not move (6 ids K = 7; N = 0, 4, 14, 145, 153 give K = 1, 5, 15, 146, 154); id shards plan at 929-937 s against 885-936 s measured, shard 1 at 1,186 s. Test: library-ci.test 'D-LIB4c-5' (the c0 case is red on the old code).
- **D-LIB4c-SCRATCHREDS (supervisor ruling on #246).** A scratch tree ships 6 extra library ids, so other tasks' main-tree checks go red there (source-gate unit tests that pin 0 rows or 8 ids, LIB-2 A10/A25 and HT-10 A4 aggregate budgets, and the gate job 1/4 UTC proof that source-gate skips). These are attributed scratch reds, listed once per C run by job, and not counted for C12-C14, which are judged on the library family's proofs.
## LIB-4d: scale test fixes (LIB-4d builder, 2026-10-18)

Card `docs/supervisor/verify/lib-4d-card.md` (lib4-cards 12 items 20-22, D-LIB4a-SCALE). No assertion, matcher or timeout changes; the one new limit is lib-4d R2's `300_000`.
- **D-LIB4d-OWNROWS.** Fixture trees hold the 8 alone plus their own rows. `ownRowsTree()` (tests/howto/fixtures/lib2b/fixture.mjs) copies the repo; with no batch .json file it is the plain copy it always was. Otherwise it removes each batch file, its batch golden file (`tests/howto/golden/library/<file>`) and every path its `pages` names, then renders the tree once, removes the generated files no plugin writes any more and writes the rest. render() reads no generated file (gen/ids.mjs reads ctx.prev), so the result is the generator's own output for the 8. stageTree() (library-rows E1, LIB-4c's fixtureTree) and library-core L2-A4 build on it, so no shipped batch row can collide with lb-test.json's `lib_barbell_row` or zz-lib2b.json's `lib_cable_lateral_raise`, and E1's counts compare its tree with the 8 alone at any scale. L2-A13 builds its rows from the 8 (`reg.rows(undefined, { batches: [] })`) plus its own row.
  - L2-A4's base comes from the tree's disk, not a second render: its generated files are its own generator's output for the 8 (rewritten by ownRowsTree, or main's committed files, which `generate --check` keeps fresh). One render instead of two: 67 s at 153 against 145 s.
  - L2-A4 and E1 render a whole tree, so they keep their own 180_000 / 600_000 / 900_000 limits, not vitest's 5 s default (one render per tree: L2-A4 took 33.5 s at the 8 and 66.7 s at 153 in the card's runs). "Under 5 s" (D-LIB4a-SCALE (c), lib4-cards 12 item 22) applies to the tests on the default timeout.
- **D-LIB4d-READ.** Tests that read every shipped ht-<slug>.ts read it from its text (`readHowTo`, tests/howto/generated-module.ts), not through vite's transform, which took 8.9-17.4 s for 153 modules against 129 ms. readHowTo accepts only the generator's exact shape (header, the one type import, `export default {` … `} satisfies BuiltHowTo;`, no import or export line inside) and throws naming the path otherwise; lib-4d R1 ties it to import() for the 8 and to loaders.ts for every shipped id. Used by css HT2-A6, generate 'own hashes', hint HT6-A8 and sections-risks HT9-A3. A test reads `generatedOnDisk()` once, never once per file: generate HT2-A2 'plates.mjs writes exactly…' counts its reads and is red at the 8 on a re-read per file.
- **D-LIB6-10 (LIB-6-PM: engine per plate, D-LIB9-ENGINE and D-LIB6PM-PLATE)**: a plate's crops and its layers-page plate block come from the engine its spec file imports (`render/closeups.mjs` engineNameOf: the spec's esbuild import closure holds the vendor engine → `vendor`, golden B's layers engine or none → `layers`, both → throws). `crop.mjs` exports `LAYERS_ENGINE`; `engineCrop`, `zoomApi`, `zboxApi` and zbox-geometry's camera, stills and guides take the engine as a parameter whose default is `LAYERS_ENGINE`, so the 8, LIB-2b's E1 and LIB-4b's B5 clones stay on it. A vendor spec's engine is loaded from the vendor files in its own closure (on the page, the mirror's staged copies). `closeupApi` and `plateEngine` check, naming the id, that the engine used is the spec's (with no spec file only the layers engine passes); the check compares engines, never bytes. The page applies the engine through the existing `renderer dispatch` patch and one new exact-once patch, `plate engine`, on the mirrored build-page.mjs specExercise head, applied for every plan. A card with no spec file (golden B's lateral raise, ref-src plate) stays on the layers engine. Tests: `tests/library/layers-engine.test.ts` with `tests/library/fixtures/engine/vendor-spec.mjs` (LIB-2b's tree, the clone's spec moved to the vendor engine with a `poly` part and a flat palm).
## D-BOXCAP-1: a named 50-word limit for the back-pain box (card BOX-CAP, 2026-10-18)
Card: `docs/supervisor/verify/box-cap-card.md` (claude/sup-lib4-cards @ 3d9b8e10, amendment 1 = ruling D-BOXCAP-WIRE).
- **The owner's answer:** decision 6, option 1, 2026-10-18 ~13:15 UTC. The back-pain box keeps all six NHS emergency triggers in 50 words (R-W0-1; `claude/libht-research` @ 0393aac9 `shared/back-pain-box.json`, status `owner-approved`).
- **The identity rule:** `tools/plates/library/lint/box-caps.mjs` holds the one table, `NAMED_BOX_CAPS = { 'back-pain-box': 50 }`, and `boxCapOf(key, fallback)`. A cap is chosen by the box's shared-card key only, never by its text, its word count or the card's own `wordCap`. The module imports nothing, and the fallback is required, so each site keeps its own default:
  - `validate.mjs`: settled red-flag cards use `boxCapOf(card.key, RED_FLAG_BOX_MAX_WORDS)`;
  - the library lint: `boxReach(path, box, names, key)`, with `lintLibraryRow(..., { boxKeys })` passing `boxKeys[flag]` (Do 9's gate call passes `{ back: 'back-pain-box' }`);
  - c7: `checkC7(content, { boxKey })`, with `LIMITS.redFlagBoxWords` as the fallback, so c7 still never imports copy-lint.mjs.
- **Every other box stays at 30.** Golden B's `copy-lint.mjs` (RED_FLAG_BOX_MAX_WORDS 30) is byte-identical (sha256 pin), c7's `LIMITS.redFlagBoxWords` stays 30, and library-lint.test's [30, 30] pin is unchanged. With no key, every site gives 30. S7 now also catches a quoted key holding a number; box-caps.mjs is its one named exception, pinned to `{ 'back-pain-box': 50 }`.
- **Evidence:** `tests/howto/box-cap.test.ts` BC-1..BC-4 (BC-1, BC-3's back-key rows and BC-4 red on base 1d1fe9ea), library-research.test.ts's wordCap rows (back-pain key red at 51 under 50; another key red at 31 under 30), and the mutations listed on PR #260.
- **D-LIB6-11 (LIB-6-PM: the back box on the layers page, D-LIB5-PAGE and D-LIB6PM-ANCHORS)**: patchHowtoLayers applies three more exact-once patches for every plan: `RF` (a row's `redFlag: 'back'` links the back box), `back holder` (a module-scope `const __BACK = {}` after the copy-lint import) and `FLAG` (a `back` getter that returns `__BACK.backBox()` and names the sheet's id when it throws). The existing `renderer dispatch` replacement loads `BACK_BOX_URL` (tools/plates/library/shared/back-box.mjs) into the holder only when a How-to on the page has 'back' in its riskFlags, so back-box.mjs is never imported statically and an unflagged page never loads it. With LIB-5's placeholder the back-flagged page rejects with `Error: <id>: LIB-5: back box waits for owner decision 6 (plan 8 item 6)`. The builder now has 10 exact-once patch anchors (D-LIB6-9's 6, `plate engine` from D-LIB6-10, and these 3), all covered by `tests/library/closeups.test.ts`'s anchor test. Tests: `tests/library/layers-back.test.ts`; fixture helper `tests/howto/fixtures/lib5/back-page.mjs` (D-LIB6PM-FIXTURE: E1's How-to with riskFlags ['wrist', 'back'], measured at 390 visible words before the box).
- **D-LIB6-12 (LIB-6-PM: pre-merge lines and the L0-lib back line, 11.1 item 9, D-LIB2b-FIXTURE (10), D-LIB6PM-L0)**: LIB-2b's rebuild compare (`tests/howto/library-rows.test.ts`, 'LIB-6 rebuild') runs unconditionally now that build-layers-page.mjs exists, so its absent-builder branch is gone; it builds through layersSpecOf, whose spec carries `files`, so it is also the spec.files test. `tools/plates/library/ci/manifest.mjs` filesOf adds, add-only, back-box.mjs with its import closure and docs/research/howto/shared/back-pain-box.json for every pinned row whose batch has a How-to with 'back' in its riskFlags (`batchHasBack`, read by importing each batch row's How-to); an unflagged batch lists none of them. Test: `tests/howto/library-manifest-back.test.ts`.

## GATE-FLAKE-5: the Paper misses are a dropped-frame snap and a lost How-to chunk (2026-10-22)

Card: `docs/supervisor/verify/gate-flake-5-card.md` (claude/sup-lib4-cards @ 684283ea), Ruling D-GF5-R; supervisor ruling D-GF5-TWINS (#292 6342524442). PR #292.
- **D-GF5-1 Decided** (cause and fix). The three Paper sightings are two mechanisms; nothing in the app or the gate takes a Paper-only path, and Paper runs beside Silent Black in every block involved.
  - (A) COPY-2's snap rule compared the panel's top between two consecutive rAF samples with nothing bounding the time between them. One dropped frame in the first ~85 ms of the 320 ms swap puts two frames of the ease in one sample pair, and any two neighbouring early frames sum to more than the 25 px limit (12.7 + 15.6 ... 19.9 + 11.4). Proven: a 40 ms stall at the swap's frame 1 gives sighting 3's exact line, and main's block also went red with no fault at all in 1 of 20 plain runs here (`COPY-2 swap paper 1x CPU: the panel's top snapped 28.5 px in one frame at 88 ms`, across a 38.8 ms gap).
  - (B) HT-8 and HT-9 Show: a lazy How-to chunk loaded for the first time in its context did not arrive. Feel and the sheet catch it (toast 'Could not load the guide.'), so HT-8 saw only an 8000 ms timeout; the zoom host does not, so HT-9 Show got a page error naming the chunk. Why a chunk failed in CI is not in any log (D-GF5-3).
  - A1-A7 (COPY-2): each sample also records `ts` (the rAF time) and `ct` (the first running swap's currentTime). A pair 25 ms or more apart is a gap pair; it is replayable only when the swap ran at its own speed across it (both cts with 0 <= ct step <= dt + 1 ms, or it started inside the gap with ct <= dt + 1, or it ended inside the gap with ct + dt >= end - 1). A replayable pair is checked on a paused copy of the swap (same panel, keyframes and timing) read at every 1000/60 ms of its span, against the same snapLimit and the same -0.5 px reversal; the raw reversal check stays on every pair, byte for byte; A6 asks the replay to match every recorded top that has a ct, and a ct-less side of a replayable pair at the start or end, within 0.5 px. The copy is cancelled before the screenshot. The success line adds the gap-pair count and A6's largest difference.
  - Why A is equal or stricter: only the snap verdict on a replayable gap pair changes, and such a pair is green only if the panel moved by its own swap animation alone, at its own speed, and that animation is smooth at one-frame steps. Every defect main catches stays red (S4: no-anim, snap-1ms, fast-80, overshoot, mid-jump, early-end, skip-ahead, each with and without a stall), and a bounce hidden inside a dropped frame, which main cannot see, is now red (blip).
  - B1-B6 (HT-8, HT-9 Show): `loads(page)` records, on the context, failed requests (file, errorText, time, page or service worker), /assets/ requests in flight, every toast and the worker's state. The four `.feel` waits keep `visible` within 8000 ms and end early, red, only when the page's own request for that exercise's feel chunk has failed in the context (a failed dynamic import is never retried); the crash lines keep their first line and add the call log's "waiting for locator" line and the load state. HT-9 Show records failed requests and toasts and adds them when a close-up did not open.
  - Why B is equal or stricter: no bound, wait or pass condition changed; the early end can only turn red sooner a run that its own 8000 ms wait would turn red; listeners are only added.
  - D-GF5-TWINS (accepted as **D-GF5-TWINS-B6**, #292 6348857532): LIB-4b's twin tripwire pins HT-8's and HT-9's groups. LIB-4b's B2 for the HT8 and HT9 families passed before the re-pin; the twins (fidelity/twins/ht8.mjs, ht9.mjs) are unchanged, because B1-B6 add no pass condition and B3's early end only turns red sooner a run whose feel chunk has already failed, which the twin's own 8000 ms wait (ht8.mjs:68) also turns red. New map entries: HT-8 `failed.push(` → collect:failed and `__gf5Toasts.push(` → collect:window.__gf5Toasts; HT-9's `failedReqs.push(` and `__gf5Toasts.push(` map to `HT9/show-<n>@390`, not collect, because B6 reads both inside its own `errors.push(` (the same-statement flow check rejects a collect there).
- **D-GF5-2 Reproduction and proof** (`tests/gate/gf5-race.mjs`, needs `npm run build`; Chromium 141.0.7390.37, Playwright 1.63, 4 cores). It runs one block's own bytes as an async function in a temporary module in scripts/ (deleted after the import, never committed); `--old` runs main 5158609c's slice and checks its sha256. Chunk faults use the worker's own path: once the worker controls the page the chunk is deleted from every `marc-*` cache, then a context route aborts ('connectionreset') or holds its network fetch.
  - S1 (main's blocks, probe commit 7c4ba25): copy2 `--stall 40 --at-frame 1 --runs 3 --expect red` exit 0, 12 of 12 contexts red, including `COPY-2 swap paper 1x CPU: the panel's top snapped 35.7 px in one frame at 102 ms (limit 25.0 px)`. ht9show `--fail-chunk hand-lat-pulldown-` exit 0 with sighting 2's two lines and nothing about the request. ht8 `--fail-chunk feel-barbell-back-squat-` and `--delay-chunk … 12000` both exit 0 with `HT-8: paper: crashed: locator.waitFor: Timeout 8000ms exceeded.` and nothing naming the chunk: main cannot tell a lost chunk from a slow one.
  - S2 (head): copy2 exit 0 on plain `--runs 20` and on `--stall 40|25 --at-frame 1|2 --runs 10` (all 10 of 10); largest replayed one-frame step 20.00 px against raw steps up to 48.37 px. ht9show `--fail-chunk` exit 0, naming `hand-lat-pulldown-Cx2R-rn0.js net::ERR_FAILED (page)` and `net::ERR_CONNECTION_RESET (service worker)`. ht8 `--fail-chunk` exit 0 with `dialog.sheet.ht .feel ended early after 0 ms: the page's request for feel-barbell-back-squat-BaT3jlD2.js failed (net::ERR_FAILED)` (the chunk failed inside openHowTo, before the wait started) and the toast 'Could not load the guide.' in the load state; `--delay-chunk 12000` exit 0, red at 8000 ms with `in flight feel-barbell-back-squat-….js (service worker, 8368 ms)`; `--delay-chunk 3000` green; plain ht9show 5 of 5 and ht8 2 of 2 green.
  - S3 (8 × `node -e 'for(;;){}'` on 4 cores): copy2 head 10 of 10 green; main 10 of 10 green under the same load (main's red is not load-stable: without load it was red 1 of 20 plain runs, S5). ht9show 20 runs and ht8 5 runs: all green, 0 failed requests in 80 app contexts, every one with the worker in control and registration active. `TZ=UTC MARC_GATE_JOB=3/4` and the whole gate in Pacific/Auckland, both under the same load: PASS.
  - S4 seeds (each `--runs 3 --expect red`, exit 0): no-anim, snap-1ms, fast-80, overshoot, mid-jump, early-end and skip-ahead red on the head with no stall, with `--stall 40 --at-frame 1` and with `--at-frame 2`, and red on `--old` with no stall. blip `--stall 80 --at-ct 133`: head red 3 of 3 (`… reversed -1.8 px at 255 ms (339.6 -> 337.8), a bounce (replayed)`), `--old` green 3 of 3 (the stricter half); blip with no stall red on both. hidden-feel (HT-8): red on both with `Timeout 8000ms exceeded`.
  - S5: plain counts, head 20 runs: 10 gap pairs, largest raw step 20.14 px, A6 within 0.000 px; `--old` 20 runs: 48 gap pairs, largest raw step 28.46 px, red 1 of 20 by itself (sighting 3 with no fault). A6's largest difference over every S2 run: 0.000 px. Mutations (scratch copies with `--gate`, each exit 1 as required): MA1 mid-jump at s40f2 green; MA2 and MA3 the s40f1 row red; MA4 plain runs red through A6 (`259.75 … 252.21 px`); MA5 blip green; MA6 early-end and skip-ahead green; MB1 no early-end line; MB2 delay 12000 green; MB3 no errorText; MB4 hidden-feel without 'Timeout 8000ms exceeded'.
  - D-GF5-TWINS: LIB-4b B2 `--ids golden8 --families HT8,HT9`: 714 rows, 714 pass, max px 0 per id, 1696 s. The tripwire was red before the re-pin (CI 344b3c7), green after it (library-fidelity.test 28 of 28), and red again on a 1-character edit to either group or on `collect:failed` reaching `problems` in one statement.
  - Do 9, gate-times.json: each group alone, 3 runs per time zone on main (b037020) and on this head, solo jobs under K = 104 (HT-9 `1/104`, HT-8 `2/104`, COPY-2 `27/104`; main added a group, so COPY-2 moved from 26/103), seconds from the proof's meta.seconds. Medians before → after, UTC / Pacific/Auckland: HT-9 341.7 / 338.7 → 338.0 / 335.8; HT-8 265.5 / 263.9 → 264.6 / 263.4; COPY-2 19.9 / 19.9 → 20.0 / 20.0. Each larger after-median is within 10% of the larger before-median (ratios 0.989, 0.997, 1.005), so gate-times.json is unchanged. All 36 runs passed.
- **D-GF5-3 Not fixed here.**
  - Part B's cause: Do 7's third branch, not reproduced. Every How-to chunk here was served by the app's own service worker; under 8 busy processes on 4 cores, 20 HT-9 Show runs and 5 HT-8 runs (80 app contexts) had 0 failed requests, the worker in control and its registration active in every one, and the UTC 3/4 shard and the whole Pacific/Auckland gate passed under the same load. So why a chunk failed in CI (the worker not controlling the page, or its cache lacking the file, and then the network fetch failing) is still unknown. B1-B6 ship as they are: the next CI sighting names the chunk, its errorText, page or worker, the requests in flight, the toasts and the worker's state. The supervisor keeps the watch item.
  - App finding (for the supervisor; src/** is not this card's): ZoomHost.tsx:169 and :188 call `void open(...)` with no catch, so a hand (or posture) chunk that fails is an unhandled rejection: no panel opens, and the user sees main.tsx's generic toast 'Something went wrong. Your data is saved.' (main.tsx:37-45, observed in the S2 fault runs), not 'Could not load the guide.' as Feel (Feel.tsx:48) and the sheet (lazy.tsx:17) show. The card's research read no toast at all on that path; the global unhandledrejection handler does show one. The gate stays red for it either way.
  - Do 10, other blocks' waits that end on a lazy How-to chunk (read only; how a failed chunk shows there):
  - HT-3 (ht3Fidelity, ht3Behaviour, ht3SectionGuards via openHowTo, harness.mjs:262, 8000 ms): only a timeout (`locator.waitFor: Timeout 8000ms exceeded`); a failed sheet or ht-<id> chunk gives no page error, and the toast is not read.
  - HT-6: openHowTo (8000 ms) only a timeout; the hand close-up taps (8000 ms, and the waits with no timeout, 30 s) give a page error (`Failed to fetch dynamically imported module`) plus the timeout; A6 inside ht3Fidelity gives the page error.
  - HT-3b: A3b/c (8000 ms, 4x) only a timeout, thrown out of the block; A4a and A4b named errors ('the How-to sheet did not open offline' / 'after build B'); A4c asserts the failure path itself (the toast and no page error); A4d only a timeout.
  - HT-7: openHowTo only a timeout; the posture close-ups (waitForSelector with no timeout, 30 s) a page error plus a 30 s timeout.
  - HT-9 C19, Order and main: openHowTo only a timeout (Order's disclaimer wait is swallowed by its .catch); HT-9 A5's feel wait a named error ('the feel section never mounted'), no page error.
  - HT-10: ht10Sweep and ht10Speed sheet opens only a timeout (the Speed feel wait a bare 8000 ms waitForFunction timeout); ht10Script chip taps a named error plus a page error, its "Feel it" a named error; build B named errors (hand and posture also a page error); the ht10.mjs fixtures only a timeout, or GF2's indirect messages for a failed hand chunk.
  - LIB-2 A10: openHowTo only a timeout, thrown out of the block.
  - FG-OFF G6: a named error ('tapping the entry opened no dialog with .ht-plate-fit'), no chunk named.
  - No other block waits on a How-to chunk. Only HT-8's earlyChip blocks service workers.

## LIB-22: LB10 H plates, build-ahead (LIB-22 builder, 2026-10-23)
- **D-LIB22-B1 (modes): all twelve LB10 H ids are H, no D row** (plan 6.2 :719, :759-760). Each needs a pose class, equipment or motion its census closest does not have, and three have no parent spec (dumbbell_lateral_raise is ref-src). Record: tools/plates/library/lb10/DESIGN.md section 1.
- **D-LIB22-B2 (kettlebell_swing's swing path, proposed).** Each key pose's arms are written as joint angles (the reach solved once), so the engine blends them as angles and the hands and bell swing on arcs between the hike, two via poses and the top, never on straight chords through the legs. The hike and the via poses balance the body and the 16 kg bell over the ankle joint (c17, "the weight back on your heels"); the top stands stacked over the ankles (c8). Record: DESIGN.md sections 4, 11 and 12.
- **D-LIB22-B3 (the static sled, proposed).** The sled is static in every pose (D-LIB22-IMPLEMENT), so sled_push's stride swaps the legs under a still body and sled_pull's lifter steps back from a still sled. Record: DESIGN.md sections 3 and 16 (risk 7).
- **D-LIB22-B4 (tempo map, proposed).** kettlebell_swing Drive (up 1) then Swing down (down 1); the five cycle constants from their verbatim quotes; jump_rope's Rest 60 from "1 minute" proposed, the ruling may keep Work only. Record: DESIGN.md section 7 and lb10/tempo.json.

Each of the three leaders crosses one named line (`hop` the rope, at 57.7°; `elbows` and `hands` the start rope, at 89.8° and 70.8°), once, at 45° or more, and keeps 6 px from everything else. It keeps its ruled Work "30–60" tempo, which leaves validateCardFile's pair problem on it, as on the NOSECS cards. That choice is asked on #305.

It is held at H6:no-card like the NOSECS cards, and A11 expects exactly validateCardFile's :216 pair problem on it, with a mutation. F-H6-TEMPO is a later supervisor card.
